> **ARCHIVED — a record, not instructions.** This file is closed work kept for its reasoning and
> its numbers. Checkboxes record the state at the moment it was archived: an unticked box means
> "not ticked when this closed", **not** "still to do", and nothing in `docs/completed/` is
> actionable. If you need a task, use the live docs; if something here reads as an instruction to
> a future reader, it was missed at archival — see the doc-closeout rule in
> `docs/parity-coverage-policy.md`, and move it to live policy or strike it.

> **This is the closed half. The live audit is [`docs/audit-2026-09-10.md`](../audit-2026-09-10.md)**,
> which after the 2026-09-16 extension below holds only seven Minor findings DEFERRED to individual
> review, half of P-15, and §6–§7 (Checked and found correct, Landscape) — this file is **not** the
> whole audit, just its closed findings, and the live doc is **not** empty.
>
> **First sweep, 2026-09-12: forty entries** moved because each carried a `DISPOSITION` or a closed

>
> **Second sweep, 2026-09-16: 147 more** — see the dedicated section near the end of this file for

> Archived under `docs/README.md`'s rule that finished work is kept **for the reasoning rather than
> the outcome**. `docs/completed/` is excluded from `scripts/queue_citation_lint.py`, so these
> entries' `path:line` citations are now retired from the live gate and will drift as code moves.
> That is the documented trade for archiving: a citation below is evidence of what was true when

## §1 — Critical

### C-01 · An adapter session on resident CUDA/Metal prefills its prompt with the BASE weights, then decodes with the adapter *(four reviewers, independently)*

- **Where:** `decoder/model.go:1234` (G3 admission), `decoder/model.go:1247-1213` (`SetAdapter`
  bound before the seed), `decoder/model.go:1094` (`residentPrefillSeed` took the `Prefiller`
  path for any suffix ≥ 8 with no adapter check — see the DISPOSITION note below); `cuda/prefill.go:768-1251` (`prefillCore`, no
  `loraLayers` reference in the file), `cuda/prefill.go:411-504` (`prefillStaticDecline` does not
  decline on `r.loraLayers != nil`); `metal/prefill.go:609-655`, `metal/backend.go:413-483` (same);
  contrast the CPU twin `decoder/forwardn.go:1195-1199` and the decode dispatch sites
  `cuda/resident.go:2457-2470`, `metal/model.go:1937-1976`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // decoder/model.go:1234
  useGPU := m.resident != nil && prefillFrom == 0 && (commit == nil || (lora != nil && resAdapter != nil))
  // decoder/model.go:1086 — FIXED 2026-09-10 (see DISPOSITION below); as it stood at audit time
  // this line read the same up to "len(suffix) >= 8 {", with no "&& !hasAdapter" — the citation
  // lint keys on live lines, so this shows the FIXED code
  if os.Getenv("GOINFER_BATCHED_PREFILL") != "0" && len(suffix) >= 8 && !hasAdapter {
      if pf, ok := m.resident.(Prefiller); ok {
          ...
          lg, perr := pf.PrefillLast(ctx, embs, from)
  ```
  ```go
  // decoder/forwardn.go:1195-1199 — the CPU path refuses batched prefill for exactly this reason
  // Compute-time LoRA (#7) is wired only into the sequential forward ... the prompt's K/V must
  // carry the delta or decode would continue a base-projected context.
  if cache.lora != nil || !m.canBatchN(len(prompt)) {
  ```
- **Failure:** `serve --backend cuda --model base=… --adapter ft=base=<peft dir>`, a chat request
  to `ft` with an ordinary prompt. `drive` takes the session path (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:1117`), `generateInto`
  admits resident, binds the adapter, then `residentPrefillSeed` hands the prompt to
  `cudaResident.PrefillLast` → `prefillChunked` → `prefillCore`, which never reads `r.loraLayers`.
  Every prompt position's K/V and the first token's logits are the base model's; decode then adds
  the delta per token. Plausible text, wrong model, HTTP 200. On Metal the same for prompts ≥ 512
  (the fast-prefill floor since 97b6e3b; the G3 status log at `task-gpu-paths-2026-09.md:272,389`
  records the design premise "Metal declines by default", which the flip one day later removed).
  WebGPU has no `Prefiller`, so the defect is backend-dependent — the shape that hides.
- **Fix:** in `residentPrefillSeed` (or at its two callers) skip the `Prefiller` branch when
  `cache.lora != nil`, mirroring `decoder/forwardn.go:1199` — the sequential `ForwardNoLogits` chain applies
  the delta; and/or have both backends' `PrefillLast` decline while `loraLayers != nil`. Then G-06.
- **Confidence:** confirmed (path traced end to end on both backends; absence of LoRA in both
  batched prefills confirmed by grep and by reading the entry points).
- **Prior:** new (G3, 65781ec/e13cc00/0547f60); sibling of M12's CPU rule.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the first half of the
  Fix note: `residentPrefillSeed` (`decoder/model.go`) now takes a `hasAdapter bool` and skips the
  `Prefiller` branch whenever it is true, mirroring `decoder/forwardn.go:1199`'s CPU-side rule
  exactly — this is a single, backend-agnostic chokepoint, so it closes the defect for CUDA and
  Metal (and any future `Prefiller`-implementing backend) in one place without touching either
  backend's `PrefillLast`. `generateInto` (the one call site where an adapter can be bound) passes
  `lora != nil`; the other two call sites (`GenerateVL`, the n-gram spec target) pass a hard-coded
  `false` — neither path binds an adapter today (confirmed: no LoRA reference anywhere in
  `generate_vl.go`; `spec_ngram.go`'s resident-verify arm is gated `cache == nil`, and an adapter
  session always supplies a non-nil `cache`), so this is provably safe rather than a guess.

  Also took the G-06 half: `fakeResidentAdapter` (`decoder/resident_adapter_seam_test.go`) now
  implements `Prefiller` (`PrefillLast`, incrementing a `prefillCalls` counter, otherwise a
  per-token loop over the same `Forward` the sequential path uses — the fake is a call-count
  recorder, not a numeric oracle, matching this test file's own stated philosophy), and a new test
  `TestSeam_AdapterSessionNeverUsesBatchedPrefill` drives a 10-token adapter-session prompt (above
  `GOINFER_BATCHED_PREFILL`'s 8-token floor — the 3-token existing gate is below it on purpose,
  per G-06's own finding) and asserts `prefillCalls == 0`. Mutation-checked by reverting the
  `&& !hasAdapter` guard: caught immediately (`PrefillLast called 1 times for a 10-token
  adapter-session prompt`). Restored and re-verified green.

  Not gated by the pre-existing `TestSeam_AdapterSessionBindsAndClearsResidentAdapter`/
  `TestSeam_AdapterSessionDeclinesToCPUWithoutResidentAdapter` (both pass unchanged) or numerically
  (the fake backend computes no real LoRA math either way — the defect is structural, only a call
  count can see it, exactly as G-06 says). `gofmt`/`go vet` clean; the three adapter-seam tests and
  every test at all three `residentPrefillSeed` call sites (`TestGenerateVL_*`,
  `TestNgram*`/`TestSpecMLA`/`TestSpecSample`/`TestSpecSession`) pass. The full `go test
  ./decoder/...` suite was NOT obtained clean this session — two consecutive attempts hit a
  contention-driven timeout (a concurrent `go run ./cmd/gate parity` re-run on the same box, load
  average up to 10.4) unrelated to this change; targeted verification plus a passing mutation test
  is the evidence base instead. Left open (out of this fix's scope): the second half of the Fix
  note (`PrefillLast` declining independently at the backend level) and the Metal-executor-
  pre-encoding half of G-06 (C-07's territory, Metal-only, needs the Mac).

### C-02 · Resident prefix reuse ignores WHICH adapter built the KV: a base turn reuses an adapter session's prefix, and vice versa *(four reviewers)*

- **Where:** `decoder/model.go:1296-1248` (`residentReuseLen(prompt, nil)` before the bind is
  consulted), `decoder/model.go:1393,1419,1467` (commits on the adapter session's path too),
  `decoder/resident_reuse.go:93-84,173-188` (`resIDs` carried token ids only; `residentForgetIDs`
  never called on adapter change); contrast `decoder/session.go:46-48` (the CPU cache's rule). Line
  numbers are the FIXED tree's — see the DISPOSITION note below.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // decoder/resident_reuse.go:184-178 — FIXED 2026-09-10 (see DISPOSITION below); at audit time the
  // record was the ids alone: no lora parameter, and no line after "m.resIDs = ids" — the citation
  // lint keys on live lines, so this shows the FIXED code
  func (m *Model) residentCommitIDs(prompt, generated []int, newBlock *residentImageBlock, lora *loraRuntime) {
      ids := make([]int, 0, len(prompt)+len(generated))
      ids = append(ids, prompt...); ids = append(ids, generated...)
      m.resIDs = ids
      m.resIDsLora = lora
  ```
  ```go
  // decoder/session.go:46-48 (the rule the resident cache has no equivalent of)
  // Switching adapters changes the projections, so any KV built under a different (or no) adapter
  // is stale — the caller should Reset first if the cached prefix was produced by another adapter.
  ```
- **Failure:** "N fine-tunes off one base" — the architecture `internal/serveapp/main.go:909-843`
  documents. Turn 1 to `ft` completes and commits `resIDs = S+U1+A1` (K/V computed under the
  adapter — any targeted projection changes the residual stream, hence every later layer's K/V).
  Turn 2 to `base` with the same system prompt `S` (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:1087`, stateless `Generate`) →
  `residentReuseLen` returns `len(S)` → base decode attends over adapter-projected K/V. The reverse
  and adapter→adapter are the same. `decoder/resident_reuse.go:27-29` names this ("the entire risk"); rule 1
  ("match on TOKEN IDS") is incomplete once two weight configurations share one cache.
- **Fix:** record the bound adapter alongside `resIDs` in `residentCommitIDs` and return 0 from
  `residentReuseLen` when it differs; or, one line, `residentForgetIDs()` in the adapter bind/clear
  `defer` at `decoder/model.go:935-1205`.
- **Confidence:** confirmed.
- **Prior:** new (G3 × resident prefix reuse, 3358e6b); same class as C-05 (09-02).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the primary Fix: the
  resident record now names the WEIGHTS as well as the ids (`decoder/resident_reuse.go`, rule 4).
  `residentCommitIDs` records the bound `*loraRuntime` in a new `Model.resIDsLora`,
  `residentReuseLen` returns 0 on any mismatch, and `residentForgetIDs` clears it. The adapter is a
  PARAMETER of both functions rather than a field set beside them, so the compiler forced all 13
  call sites to state theirs: `generateInto` passes `cache.lora`; `GenerateVL`, the Gemma 4 VL path
  and the n-gram spec target pass `nil` — the same paths C-01 established never bind an adapter.
  The one-line alternative (forget in the bind/clear `defer`) is also correct and was NOT taken: it
  discards adapter→same-adapter reuse too, i.e. the agent-loop prefix win, for every fine-tune.
  Pointer identity is sound because `LoadAdapter` always builds a fresh runtime and
  `registerAdapter` retires the one it displaces rather than freeing it, so a same-name reload never
  compares equal.

  Gated at both levels, each mutation-verified (deleting the refusal turns it red):
  `TestResidentReuseLen_adapterMustMatch` covers all six crossings including a same-name reload;
  `TestSeam_ResidentReuseNeverCrossesAdapters` drives this finding's own sequence through
  `generateInto` with real sessions, and asserts base→base reuse is nonzero FIRST — every "reused 0"
  check would otherwise pass on a fixture where prefix reuse never fires.

### C-03 · Bailing Hybrid's KDA state is recurrent state that neither the `Recurrent` bit nor `KVCache.hasRecurrentState` knows about — session reuse, session reset and speculative rollback all leak or rewind it wrongly *(three reviewers)*

- **Where:** `decoder/arch.go:809`, `decoder/kvcache.go:120-123,453-455,460-495,519-525`,
  `decoder/model.go:641-599` (`NewCache` allocates `c.kda`), `decoder/kda.go:86-91,112-113`,
  `decoder/kda_rehearsal.go:52-58`; consumers `decoder/session.go:63-66,73-80,98-102`,
  `decoder/forwardn.go:134-172`, `decoder/resident_reuse.go:120`, `internal/serveapp/sessions.go:
  180-201`, `internal/serveapp/openai.go:1133,1059`, `internal/serveapp/main.go:529`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // decoder/arch.go:839 — FIXED 2026-09-10 (see DISPOSITION below); at audit time the last field
  // read `false` (Recurrent: false) — the citation lint keys on live lines, so this shows the FIXED code
  {"bailing_hybrid", func(a *Architecture) bool { return a.kda != nil }, (*Model).runLayersBailingHybrid, false, true},
  // decoder/kvcache.go:453-455 — FIXED likewise: at audit time the predicate had no "|| c.kda != nil"
  // and its comment read "The fourth kind will be added here, once, or it will be missed at four sites again."
  func (c *KVCache) hasRecurrentState() bool { return c.mamba != nil || c.delta != nil || c.conv != nil || c.kda != nil }
  // decoder/kda.go:89-91,112-113 — mutated in place per token, no per-position history
  st.convWinQ = kdaSlideWindow(st.convWinQ, qm, K) ... S := st.s[hh*D*D:(hh+1)*D*D]; out := kdaRecurrentStep(qh, kh, vh, decay, beta, S)
  ```
- **Failure:** Ling 3.0 is CPU-only (`FeatKDA` has no backend), so every served request takes
  `lm.sessions.acquire` → `Session.Generate`. (a) The fifth distinct conversation at the default
  `--kv-sessions 4` evicts the coldest session via `Reset()` → `TruncateTo(0)` → `hasRecurrentState`
  false → `resetRecurrent` skipped (and it has no `kda` arm anyway) → conversation B's first token is
  mixed against A's `S` matrices and A's last K−1 q/k/v at 3 of every 4 layers — C-01 (09-02)'s
  cross-conversation leak, silent. (b) An identical resend gives `matched = len−1`;
  `TruncateTo(n−1)` reslices `mlaLatent` exactly and reports `exact=true` while the KDA state has
  consumed token n−1 — the qwen3.6 "identical greedy prompts diverge at token 0" repro
  `decoder/resident_reuse.go:112-106` records, now on the staged path. (c) `reconcile` (`decoder/session.go:98`)
  stays warm after a mid-sweep error. (d) `specRollbackSafe()` returns true (Recurrent=false,
  SlidingWindow=0), so `serve --spec ngram` admits the family: the verify runs K sequential
  `m.forward` calls each advancing `c.kda`, and the rejected suffix is "rolled back" by a
  `TruncateTo` that touches only `mlaLatent`. `Snapshot` is safe only by accident
  (`decoder/kvsnapshot.go:71` refuses on `mlaLatent`).
- **Fix:** `Recurrent: true` at `decoder/arch.go:839`; `|| c.kda != nil` at `decoder/kvcache.go:454`; a `kda` arm in
  `resetRecurrent` (zero `s`, nil the three windows). Every consumer then follows. Then G-05.
- **Confidence:** confirmed (state mutation read; every consumer traced; the serve trigger is the
  default flag set).
- **Prior:** regression of C-02 (09-02) for the family added 2026-09-06 (e6b31cc: `kvcache.go` +5
  lines — the field, not the predicate).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix as written:
  `Recurrent: true` on the `bailing_hybrid` registry entry, `|| c.kda != nil` in
  `KVCache.hasRecurrentState`, and a `kda` arm in `resetRecurrent` that nils all three conv windows
  and zeroes the matrix state. Every consumer follows from those two predicates — `TruncateTo` (a
  partial rewind now reports inexact; `TruncateTo(0)` resets), `Session.reconcile`,
  `specRollbackSafe` (so `serve --spec ngram` now refuses the family), and the resident reuse rule
  — which was confirmed by tracing each one rather than assumed.

  Gated by the census that also closes G-05 (below), run RED against the unfixed tree first —
  `bailing_hybrid` failed every assertion and every other family passed — and each of the three
  fix parts is independently gated: reverting the registry bit, the predicate term, or the reset
  arm alone turns the suite red. `TestSession_bailingHybridNeverReusesKDAState`
  (`decoder/recurrent_census_test.go`) drives failures (a) and (b) through a real `Session`: an
  identical resend warm-reused 2 positions over consumed KDA state before the fix, and `Reset` —
  sessionLRU's eviction at the default `--kv-sessions` — left all four state fields dirty.
  Bailing's tiny checkpoint fixture is not on the Linux box (its pin script needs a venv that is
  not here), so no end-to-end forward test ran; the gates drive the real `NewCache`, `KVCache`
  and `Session` code, which is where every one of the four failures lives.

### C-04 · Cohere/Command-R resident on CUDA is silently wrong: the final norm is RMSNorm, and the default-ON batched prefill knows nothing about `layerNorm`/`parallelBlock`

- **Where:** `cuda/resident.go:2977` (decode tail: `r.rms`, not `r.norm`), `cuda/resident.go:1984-1986` (the `r.norm` dispatcher the tail does not use), `cuda/prefill.go:1203` (prefill tail,
  same), `cuda/prefill.go:894,1100` (`bRmsB` at every norm site), `cuda/prefill.go:411-504`
  (`prefillStaticDecline` refuses `postOnly`/`qkNormWhole` — G5 Olmo — but not `layerNorm`/
  `parallelBlock`; `grep parallelBlock cuda/prefill.go` is empty), `cuda/backend.go:1131-1135`
  (`postNorm` deliberately not uploaded for `parallelBlock`), `cuda/prefill.go:1260-1263` (softcap
  applied, `applyLogitScale` not — both now); `decoder/features.go:570-572` (the G5 declaration).
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // cuda/resident.go:2977 — decode tail, every token, every family. FIXED 2026-09-11 (now r.norm;
  // see DISPOSITION below); at audit time it read:
  if e := r.rms(r.x, r.finalNorm, r.aq, r.aSc); e != nil {
  // cuda/prefill.go:1101 — batched pass re-normalises the post-attention residual with Ly.postNorm …
  if e := r.bRmsB(xB, Ly.postNorm, hidden, mqB, mScB, M); e != nil {
  // cuda/backend.go:1131 — … which for parallelBlock is never allocated
  if (!r.postOnly && !r.parallelBlock) || h.isDeltaNet { L.postNorm = r.up32(h.postNorm) }
  ```
- **Failure:** (1) Decode: the CPU reference (`decoder/model.go:961` → `normalize` →
  `layerNorm` for `NormLayer`) mean-centres the final norm; Cohere's residual stream is not
  zero-mean, so every token's logits come from the wrong norm. The pre-attention norm correctly goes
  through `r.norm` (`cuda/resident.go:2481,2513`), which is why the NaN-only smoke test
  (`cuda/cohere_resident_smoke_test.go:81`) cannot see it. (2) Any prompt ≥ 8 tokens takes the
  batched pass: `rmsnorm_quant_batched` at every site, then a re-normalise with a zero-value
  `Buffer` — an illegal device read (sticky context error) at best, garbage K/V for the whole prompt
  at worst. (3) Even a corrected pass returns unscaled last-token logits (`LogitScale` is
  Command-R's `logit_scale`; matters under temperature sampling, not argmax).
- **Fix:** make both tails call `r.norm` (a `layernorm_quant` batched twin exists: `layernorm_quant
  .cu`); add `r.layerNorm || r.parallelBlock` to `prefillStaticDecline` until the batched glue
  carries the LayerNorm and the parallel-block reuse; apply `applyLogitScale` in the prefill tail
  next to the softcap; give the smoke test a resident-vs-CPU cosine floor like every other family.
- **Confidence:** confirmed (norm dispatch and decline list traced end to end); the null-pointer
  consequence is plausible (aikit `Arg(Buffer{})` not readable here; the buffer is provably never
  allocated).
- **Prior:** new (7357856, G5 row 7 — post-review). The Metal twin dispatches via `encodeNorm`
  (`metal/model.go:1870`) and is correct.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** All three halves, and the
  third turned out not to be Cohere's alone.

  1. Decode: the final norm now goes through `r.norm` (`cuda/resident.go:2977`), as the
     pre-attention norm already did.
  2. Batched prefill: `prefillStaticDecline` refuses `layerNorm || parallelBlock`
     (`cuda/prefill.go:479`), so Cohere runs sequentially until the batched glue carries the
     LayerNorm and the parallel-block reuse. The per-row tail's final norm also moves to `r.norm`
     (`cuda/prefill.go:1203`). No test can see that one: behind the decline, no LayerNorm family
     reaches it.
  3. The prefill tail now applies the logit scale after the softcap, as `step()` does
     (`cuda/prefill.go:1262`). Dense Granite is CUDA-resident with `logits_scaling` and takes the
     batched path, so its seed-token logits had come back unscaled. That is invisible to argmax and
     wrong for every sampled first token.

  Measured, with the gates written first and red before the fix:

  - `TestCohereResidentParityCUDA` (`cuda/cohere_resident_parity_test.go:38`), resident vs CPU at
    the same quant: decode worst cosine 0.9756 → 0.99974 / 0.99975, relL2 0.22 → 0.022. The
    batched prefill went from "launch bRms: cuda: nil buffer" to a clean decline.
  - `TestGraniteDenseResidentPrefillLogitScaleCUDA` (`cuda/cohere_resident_parity_test.go:129`),
    batched vs sequential: relL2 5.0000 (exactly the missing 1/6) → 0.0000.

  Three mutations each go red: the decode tail back to `rms`, the decline removed, and the tail's
  `applyLogitScale` removed.

  **Four of this finding's citations had drifted and stayed lint-green.** Their index rows had
  been re-recorded onto unrelated lines: the decode tail at 2894 read `nWin = int(Ly.window)`, and
  the softcap citation at 1247 read an OOM return. All four are re-pointed above. A green lint says
  a citation points somewhere stable; only reading the line says it means what the sentence claims.

### C-05 · Dense Granite 4.2 GGUF loads llama.cpp's RoPE-permuted q/k rows as if they were in HF order — every position past 0 is rotated on the wrong pairs

- **Where:** `decoder/gguf.go:2914-2870` (`ggufQKPermuted`: `"llama", "mellum"` only),
  `decoder/gguf.go:2707-2662,2696-2699` (generic `loadLayer` with `permuteQK == false`),
  `decoder/gguf.go:153-162` (`ggufGraniteDenseConfig` → `ModelType: "granite"` →
  `llamaTensorSchema`), `decoder/registry.go:339-2012`; the sibling that got it right:
  `decoder/gguf.go:2267-2224` (granitehybrid: "llama.cpp converts GraniteHybrid attention with
  undo_permute=True … must be un-permuted").
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // decoder/gguf.go:2914 — FIXED 2026-09-10 (see DISPOSITION below); at audit time the case read
  // `case "llama", "mellum":` — the citation lint keys on live lines, so this shows the FIXED code
  func ggufQKPermuted(archName string) bool {
      switch archName {
      case "llama", "mellum", "granite":
          return true
  ```
- **Failure:** llama.cpp's `GraniteModel` subclasses `LlamaModel` and inherits its `modify_tensors`
  (`undo_permute = True`) — the same class chain the repo's own granitehybrid comment relies on —
  and `LLM_ARCH_GRANITE` uses `LLAMA_ROPE_TYPE_NORM`, so every published granite-4.2 GGUF carries
  `attn_q/attn_k` in the interleaved-pair order. Loaded without `permMat`, rotate_half pairs row `j`
  with `j+hd/2` instead of the adjacent rows: exact at position 0, position-dependent garbage after,
  fluent, no error. The only dense-granite tests load safetensors; ac026d4 verified the GGUF
  *header*; `docs/capability-matrix.md:109` advertises "safetensors, GGUF". Breaking-after-tag
  angle: any `<base>.<quant>.giw` sidecar already transcoded from a granite GGUF loads clean and is
  mtime-fresh (`internal/prequant/prequant.go:227`), so the fix needs a `giwVersion` bump or a
  writer stamp.
- **Fix:** add `"granite"` to `ggufQKPermuted` (granite has no q/k biases or QK-norm, so `permMat`
  on q/k is the whole change); gate with a synthetic granite GGUF written *with* the permutation or
  the real file behind `GOINFER_GRANITE_DENSE_GGUF` compared to the safetensors forward.
- **Confidence:** confirmed on the goinfer side; the llama.cpp-side fact is from the reviewer's
  knowledge of `convert_hf_to_gguf.py`, corroborated by the granitehybrid comment — verify with
  `gguf-dump` on the Mac before treating as closed.
- **Prior:** new (ac026d4); C-10's "one rule keyed on a two-name list" pattern.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix: `"granite"`
  joins `ggufQKPermuted`. The llama.cpp-side fact the Confidence line asked to verify on the Mac was
  verified instead from llama.cpp's own source on the Linux box (checkout `427291b`, 2026-09-05):
  `GraniteForCausalLM` converts through `GraniteModel(LlamaModel)` (`conversion/granite.py`), which
  inherits `undo_permute = True`; its `permute()` is exactly what `ggufInvPermute` inverts; and
  `LLM_ARCH_GRANITE` sits in the `LLAMA_ROPE_TYPE_NORM` group. The one granite converter that sets
  `undo_permute = False` is `GraniteSwitchModel`, which writes a different arch (`GRANITE_SWITCH`)
  goinfer never loads — so every `"granite"` GGUF is permuted, with no ambiguity for the rule.

  **Sibling scan:** all 19 GGUF architectures `ggufConfig` dispatches were checked against that NORM
  group and the converter classes. Granite was the only one llama.cpp permutes and goinfer did not
  un-permute: `llama4` is NORM but `Llama4Model` sets `undo_permute = False`, and `deepseek2` converts
  outside `LlamaModel` with its interleaved RoPE handled in the MLA forward. The rule is complete for
  goinfer's GGUF surface as of that checkout; it is still a name list, so a new NORM family needs the
  same check.

  **The breaking-after-tag half:** `giwVersion` 9 -> 10 with no layout change, and
  `LoadSerializedWeights` refuses a pre-v10 dense-granite bundle. Because prequant's `selfCheck` is
  `decoder.Load` -> `LoadSerializedWeights`, a stale sidecar now reads as not loading and is rebuilt
  from source. It also refuses a correct safetensors-sourced granite bundle, which costs one rebuild.

  Gated by `decoder/gguf_granite_permute_test.go`: a synthetic GGUF whose q/k are permuted by a port
  of llama.cpp's `permute()`, loaded through `ggufConfig` -> `resolveArchitecture` ->
  `buildWeightsFromGGUF` and compared ROW BY ROW (with `v_proj` asserted untouched), a llama control
  on the same harness, and a `.giw` test (a v9-stamped granite bundle refused, v10 loads, a v9 llama
  bundle still loads). RED before the fix on each gate's own assertion, and each fix part is
  independently gated. A first red run was VACUOUS — the synthetic model carried Granite 3.x
  multipliers (residual 0.22), which `validateGraniteDense` rejects, so it failed at setup before
  comparing a row; it now carries the real 4.2 values, and the red run asserts the failure message.
  No real dense-granite GGUF is on the Linux box and the converter's torch venv is absent, so a
  real-file forward check remains open (the Mac, or a fetched GGUF).

### C-06 · gpt-oss int4 on WebGPU dispatches the W8A8 down-projection kernel over the W4A8 expert stack — every routed expert's down matmul collapses to bias

- **Where:** `gpu/decoderunner.go:997-1002` (`moeExpertGptOssDown` — no `s.w4` branch), contrast
  `gpu/decoderunner.go:963-975` (`moeExpert` branches on `s.w4`), `gpu/decoderunner.go:1358-1363`
  (the dispatch), `gpu/moe.go:240-250,263-267,283` (the int8 kernel's layout), `gpu/residency.go:
  449-495` (`buildStacked`: `"int4"` → `UploadStackedExpertsInt4Packed` → `w4: true`),
  `gpu/moe_w4a8.go:237,273`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  moeExpertGptOssDown = func(aq, as *wgpu.Buffer, s *ResidentStackedW8A8, idx, wgt, dbias, dst *wgpu.Buffer, slot int) {
      d := uni([]uint32{uint32(s.kp), uint32(s.rows), uint32(slot), 0})
      gx, gy := gemvGrid(s.rows)
      add(c.moeExpertGptOssDownPipeline, bind(c.moeExpertGptOssDownLayout, aq, s.bq, as, s.bScales, dst, idx, wgt, dbias, d), gx, gy)
  }
  ```
  ```wgsl
  // gpu/moe.go:243-245 — the only kernel behind that pipeline
  @group(0) @binding(1) var<storage, read> bq:      array<vec4<u32>>;  // [nE*N, kp/16] stacked experts
  @group(0) @binding(3) var<storage, read> bScales: array<f32>;        // [nE*N] stacked
  ```
- **Failure:** `Quant: "int4"` — the config the gate itself uses (`gpu/gptoss_resident_test.go:43`)
  and the only one that fits gpt-oss-20b on the 8 GB card. The stack holds nibbles (kp/32 vec4 per
  row) and f16 scale pairs; the int8 kernel strides rows by kp/16 (2× the true stride), unpacks
  nibble pairs as int8, and reads `bScales[row]` as f32 from packed f16 pairs (~1e-19..1e-12). The
  routed contribution collapses to `wgt·(≈0 + downBias)`; the upper half of the stack reads out of
  bounds (robust-access clamped). Silent, every token, every layer. Gate/up (`moeExpert`) are
  correct, which is what lets it survive the gate (G-07). int8 experts are unaffected.
- **Fix:** branch on `s.w4` exactly as `moeExpert` does with an int4 twin of
  `moeExpertGptOssDownGEMVWGSL` (the `dot8w`/`unpack2x16float` body of `moeExpertGEMVW4WGSL` with
  the `wgt*(r+dbias[row])` epilogue), or decline `gptoss && w4` at build until it exists.
- **Confidence:** confirmed (dispatch traced from `buildStacked` → `w4:true` → the unbranched
  builder; kernel layout mismatch read directly).
- **Prior:** new (db81b08). Sibling of C-09 (09-02) in shape — a term dropped in one backend's port.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix. A new int4
  twin, `moeExpertGptOssDownGEMVW4WGSL` (`gpu/moe_w4a8.go:92`), is `moeExpertGEMVW4WGSL`'s body
  with gpt-oss's `wgt·(r + dbias[row])` combine. `gptOssDownPipelineFor` (`gpu/moe.go:554`)
  selects it on `s.w4`, and it is the one place the choice is made: both the resident builder and
  the test hook call it.

  The gates were written first, and both were red on today's code:

  - `TestGptOssDownW4_parity` (`gpu/gptoss_down_w4_test.go:18`) runs the combine through that
    selection against the CPU reference. The matmul term is forced to dominate the bias, since
    G-07 is the record of a gate that could not see it. Cosine went 0.03 / 0.19 / 0.33 → 1.000000,
    maxAbs ~1e-7.
  - `TestGptOssResidentParityWebGPU`, tightened per G-07. The bar, registered before the fix from
    Metal's 0.9989, is cosine >= 0.998 with argmax matching at every position
    (`gpu/gptoss_resident_test.go:112-118`). minCosine went 0.994273 → 0.999974. The bug had
    **flipped position 6's argmax** (CPU 59, resident 118), so on this fixture it changed output,
    not just a few percent of logit mass.

  Three mutations each go red: the selection ignoring `s.w4`, the kernel dropping the down bias,
  and the kernel dropping the router weight.

### C-07 · Metal's pipelined executor commits a command buffer pre-encoded under the PREVIOUS adapter state — the first token after every `SetAdapter` runs the wrong (or a released) delta

- **Where:** `metal/model.go:1447-1451` (`execLoop`: `next = r.encodeLogitsCB()` after commit,
  `cur = next` survives across `ForwardEmbPipe` calls and across generations), `metal/model.go:
  1845,1857,1870,1910` (LoRA dispatches gated on `r.loraLayers` AT ENCODE TIME), `metal/lora.go:
  78-80` (`SetAdapter`: `releaseLoRALayers` then `r.loraLayers = nil`; no `stopExec`),
  `metal/model.go:1487-1457` (`stopExec` exists; called only from `Close`).
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // metal/model.go:1462-1462
  cur.Commit()
  ...
  if !drain { next = r.encodeLogitsCB() } // overlaps cur's GPU execution — the encode-ahead win
  cur.WaitDone()
  ...
  } else { cur = next }   // survives across generations
  ```
- **Failure:** generation A ends with `cur` holding a fully encoded, uncommitted buffer whose
  dispatch list was fixed at encode time. Generation B calls `SetAdapter(...)` (bind, switch, or
  clear) and its first `Forward` commits that stale buffer: base→adapter, position 0 of a short
  prompt (or the first decode token after a batched prefill) runs with NO delta; adapter→base or
  A→B, the first token runs the OLD delta from buffers `releaseLoRALayers` already handed to
  `d.ReleaseBuf`. One position's K/V in the shared resident cache is computed with the wrong
  projection and every later token attends to it. Same class CUDA documents and refuses for graphs
  (`cuda/lora.go:18-24`). The Metal LoRA gate binds first (fresh executor, `cur == nil`) and only
  compares the last token, so it is green over this by construction (G-06c).
- **Fix:** `SetAdapter` must invalidate the pre-encoded buffer — a generation counter checked in
  `execLoop` before `cur.Commit()` (re-encode when it changed), or `stopExec()` + re-arm on the next
  call (needs `execOnce` replaced by a nil-check).
- **Confidence:** confirmed for the wrong-delta token (pure Go control flow); plausible for the
  use-after-release half (depends on aikit's encoder retain semantics, not readable here).
- **Prior:** new (e13cc00); sibling of the CUDA graphs hazard `cuda/lora.go` names and refuses.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED.** Took the `stopExec()` + re-arm route: `SetAdapter` calls `stopExec()`
  before touching `r.loraLayers` at all (any bind/switch/clear), tearing the executor goroutine
  down along with whatever it had pre-encoded; `ForwardEmbPipe`'s `execOnce.Do` became a plain
  `r.execReq == nil` check so the next call re-arms it, encoding fresh under whatever adapter
  state SetAdapter left behind. `TestLoRAResidentParityMetal_armedExecutorThenBind`
  (metal/lora_resident_parity_test.go) reproduces exactly the ordering this bug needs — arm the
  executor with a plain Forward first, THEN bind, THEN compare position-0 logits against CPU —
  which is also G-06's Metal fix (below). Mutation-checked: removing `stopExec()` from
  `SetAdapter` drops the cosine to 0.36 with a wrong argmax.

  **G-06 (Metal half) also closed by this fix.** The original `TestLoRAResidentParityMetal` binds
  on a fresh, never-yet-used executor and only compares the last token — the one ordering that
  cannot see C-07, exactly as G-06 named. `TestLoRAResidentParityMetal_armedExecutorThenBind` is
  the case G-06's own fix bullet asked for ("on Metal run once WITHOUT the adapter first (arming
  the executor), then bind and compare position-0 logits"). cuda/gpu's own G-06 gates
  (`cuda/lora_resident_parity_test.go`, `gpu/lora_resident_parity_test.go`) and the decoder-level
  fake-backend seam test (`decoder/resident_adapter_seam_test.go`) are untouched — this closes only
  the Metal-specific gap C-07 exists for, not the cross-backend wiring gap G-06 also names.

### C-09 · int8-KV + m-RoPE decode on WebGPU writes the K scale at the ROPE position, corrupting an earlier position's scale and leaving the current one unset

- **Where:** `gpu/attention.go:615,643` (`ropeStoreI8`: `p.pos` is both the rotation angle and the
  scale index), `gpu/decoderunner.go:850-866` (`ropeKUniFor` gen: slot 3 → `ropePos`, only slot 5
  keyed on `pos`), `gpu/decoderunner.go:896-899` (the i8 pipeline bound with that uniform),
  `gpu/attention.go:586-589` (the reader indexes `kScale[s*nKV+kvh]` at the true position);
  callers with `ropePos != pos`: `decoder/generate_vl.go:279-383`, `decoder/model.go:1157`.
- **Code:**
  ```wgsl
  scales[p.pos * p.nKV + h] = sc;          // gpu/attention.go:643 at audit time; FIXED 2026-09-11 to p.spos
  ```
  ```go
  // gpu/decoderunner.go:850-855 — "confirmed directly against the shader source" — the f32 one
  return []uint32{…, uint32(ropePos), f32bits(rs), uint32(pos * g.kvDim), uint32(g.nKV), 0}
  ```
- **Failure:** Qwen2.5-VL on `--backend webgpu --kv i8`, an image in the prompt: every decode
  step's `ForwardMRoPE(emb, gpuPos, gpuPos+mropeDelta)` (delta < 0 — the image compresses
  positions) writes K bytes at the right row but the K scale at `scales[(pos+delta)*nKV+h]`,
  overwriting a PROMPT position's scale, while `scales[pos*nKV+h]` stays at its zero-init/stale
  value. `attnI8` then scales the new key by 0 (or by a longer previous sequence's leftover) and
  `|delta|` prompt keys by the wrong absmax. Compounds for the turn and, through resident prefix
  reuse, into later turns. f32/f16 KV unaffected. a630a2c's message ("no shader changes — the
  RoPE WGSL kernel is pure rotation with no KV-write side effect") is true of `ropeStoreShaderWGSL`
  and false of the i8 variant.
- **Fix:** give `ropeStoreI8ShaderWGSL` its own slot (`_c`) carrying the true `pos` for the scale
  write and set it in the gen; or derive the scale index from `base / kvDim`.
- **Confidence:** confirmed (uniform generation, shader indexing and both callers traced).
  Narrow (WebGPU + Qwen2.5-VL + `--kv i8` + image) but every element is supported and documented.
- **Prior:** new (a630a2c / 16f97d8). Sibling of M-32 (09-02): the i8 KV variants drift from f32.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the first Fix. Slot 7
  of the shared rope-store uniform now carries the true `pos` (`gpu/decoderunner.go:862`), and the
  int8 shader indexes its scale write by it (`gpu/attention.go:615,643`; `P._c` → `spos`). No
  variant read slot 7 before, so the f32/f16 rope-store kernels are byte-identical. The f32
  shift-invariance test still reads cosine 1.00000000, maxAbs 0.

  The gate is `TestKVI8ScaleWrittenAtTruePosition` (`gpu/forwardmrope_kvi8_test.go:24`). It reads
  every layer's K-scale buffer back through `KScalesForTest` (`gpu/testhooks_kvscale.go:11`),
  after 24 decode steps on a fresh model with every rope angle shifted by delta. Slots [0,24) must
  be written, and nothing past them. The red counts were exactly the predicted
  positions × 2 KV heads × 28 layers:

  - delta +17: 952 unwritten and 952 stray;
  - delta −13: 728 unwritten.

  Green is 0 and 0. Two mutations each go red: slot 7 left at 0, and the shader indexing by
  `p.pos` again.

  A first version of the gate, a shift-invariance cosine, failed on its own precondition. int8 KV
  already sits at cosine 0.96 against f32 on qwen2.5-1.5B after a 24-random-token prompt, which is
  too noisy to hold a bar. That figure is recorded as a finding, not chased here.

---

## §2 — Gates that cannot fail

### G-01 · The parity manifest's `aikit_version` is two aikit releases stale again — the staleness gate re-disarmed three days after v0.17.0 "re-armed" it *(two reviewers)*

- **Where:** `testdata/parity_manifest.json:2` (`"v1.37.0"`), `go.mod:6` and the four submodule
  `go.mod`s (`v1.39.0`), `decoder/parity_manifest_test.go:265-280` (`freshDepsHash` mixes the
  manifest string; the only aikit input), `decoder/aikit_pin_test.go:32-73` (compares the five
  `go.mod`s to each other only); commits c098b1c (v1.38.0) and 15f9c91 (v1.39.0) touch all five
  `go.mod`s and not line 2.
- **Failure:** every `status: validated` family's `deps_hash` is computed over
  `aikit_version=v1.37.0`, so `TestParityManifest_fresh` reads green across two aikit bumps — one of
  which (v1.38.0) moved both vision towers onto `AttendTileFused`, "documented as *not*
  bit-identical to the materialized path it replaces". CHANGELOG v0.17.0:377-381 records this
  exact defect as fixed ("hand-typed and seventeen versions stale … Re-armed"); the fix re-typed
  the value instead of gating it.
- **Fix:** in `TestAikitPinsAgree` (the go.mod regexp already exists) or `TestParityManifest_fresh`,
  `t.Fatalf` when `m.AikitVersion != <root go.mod aikit require>`; or derive it at hash time and
  drop the field. Then apply 4da677b's refreshable-iff-additive rule to v1.38/v1.39 and record
  which rows were actually re-run (c098b1c says the vision rows were).
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** regression of the 2026-09-06 re-arm.
- **DISPOSITION — the drift mechanism is FIXED (drop-the-field route); the v1.38/v1.39
  re-validation half is separate and untouched.** The manifest's `parityManifest` struct
  (`decoder/parity_manifest_test.go`) no longer has an `AikitVersion` field at all —
  `freshDepsHash` calls a new `rootAikitVersion()` (`decoder/parity_manifest_test.go:221-231`,
  reusing `TestAikitPinsAgree`'s own go.mod regexp pattern) that reads the ROOT `go.mod`'s aikit
  require directly, at hash-computation time, every time. There is no longer a stored string for
  anyone to hand-type or forget to bump: the value the hash mixes in is now *structurally* always
  today's real pin, not a re-typed assertion of it. `"aikit_version"` no longer appears in
  `testdata/parity_manifest.json` at all (removed via the normal `-update` round-trip, a clean
  one-line diff — go.mod already said v1.40.0, matching what the field held, so no family actually
  restaled). `TestRootAikitVersion_readsFromGoMod`
  (`decoder/parity_manifest_test.go:240-262`) independently re-parses `go.mod` with a SEPARATE
  regexp (not calling `rootAikitVersion`'s own machinery) and asserts exact agreement, deliberately
  without hardcoding a version string (the same hand-typed-value failure mode this fix removes,
  one level up). Mutation-checked: hardcoding `rootAikitVersion`'s return to a wrong value fails
  BOTH this test and `TestParityManifest_fresh` (every family goes stale, proving the derived value
  genuinely feeds the hash, not just a cosmetic read). The v1.38/v1.39 re-validation-scope
  question this item also raised is unrelated to the drift mechanism and stays open.

### G-02 · `olmo_hybrid` is `status: validated` with a `deps_hash` that covers zero source files

- **Where:** `testdata/parity_manifest.json:561-573` (`"uses": [], "own": []`), `decoder/
  decoder/parity_manifest_test.go:193-221,306-320`.
- **Failure:** `sha256("aikit_version=v1.37.0")` = `626a6f34…b1bb`, computed — identical to the
  recorded hash. No edit to `deltanet.go`, `rope.go` or `registry.go` (all on this family's forward)
  can ever restale it; `validated_at: 493897af` is permanent. `bailing_hybrid` has the same empty
  sets and the same hash but is `experimental` (unenforced). The `enforced == 0` guard is
  manifest-wide, not per family.
- **Fix:** fail any `validated` family whose `familyDepFiles` is empty; give `olmo_hybrid` `uses:
  [core, loaders, quant, deltanet]` (as `qwen3_5`) and `bailing_hybrid` its `own` files when promoted.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** new.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix.
  `TestParityManifest_fresh` now fails any family with `status: validated` whose `uses`/`own` sets
  name no files; red on the old manifest, naming `olmo_hybrid`. It keys on status rather than
  `validated_at`: `bailing_hybrid` carries a `validated_at` but is experimental, and per the Fix
  gets its sets when it is promoted. `olmo_hybrid` now uses core + loaders + quant + deltanet and
  owns `decoder/forward_qwen35.go` (its architecture fills `qwen35Params`, so that is the forward it
  runs), plus its tiny and real gates and their pin scripts. Its hash was then refreshed through
  `scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh`, not by hand: forward goldens 59 passed, 1 skipped
  (`TestBailingHybrid_forwardParity`, no fixture on the refreshing box), 1 failed and pre-registered
  (`TestOlmo3_forwardParity`). `TestOlmoHybrid_forwardParity` is among the passes. One `deps_hash`
  line changed and `validated_at` stays `493897af`. Between that commit and the refresh, 29 commits
  touched the files the family now covers; the passing tiny golden is the evidence that none of
  them moved its numerics.

### G-03 · The seven v0.17.0 families are "parity-gated" by gates that cannot block anywhere: CI skips them (fixtures gitignored), the sweep files a FAIL as FIRST-RUN, and the ledger has not been promoted since 2026-09-02 — G-04 (09-02) reopened *(two reviewers)*

- **Where:** `.gitignore:22-53` (five tiny fixtures tracked; none of `olmo3-tiny`, `olmo_hybrid-
  tiny`, `smollm3-tiny`, `ministral3-tiny`, `qwen3moe-tiny`, `granite-dense-tiny`,
  `bailing_hybrid-tiny` — each holds only `config.json`), `decoder/olmo3_test.go:33-49` and
  siblings (`t.Skipf` on the missing safetensors), `decoder/serialize_census_test.go:87-107,163-170`
  (the .giw census `Skip`s the same seven — the v9 KDA tail and Olmo Hybrid's re-fused DeltaNet
  round-trip only on the developer's box; C-03 (09-02)'s disposition names this exact mechanism),
  `cmd/gate/parity.go:448-451,657-757` (`awaitingFirstConfirmation`, 35 entries),
  `cmd/gate/parity_test.go:460-465` (no age bound), `testdata/gate_ledger.json` (52 entries, none
  after 2026-08-27), `docs/measurements/parity-sweep-aikit-rearm-2026-09-06.md:28-38,83`.
- **Failure:** for `TestQwen3Moe/GraniteDense/Ministral3/SmolLM3/Olmo3/OlmoHybrid/BailingHybrid_
  forwardParity` and their seven `*Real_gate`s there is no ritual in which a FAIL stops a tag. This
  already happened: the 09-08..09-10 log records `TestOlmo3_forwardParity` red on the box across at
  least five commits ("36 pass/1 fail … pre-existing") with the family `validated` throughout. And
  the 09-06 sweep PASSED `TestCohereAyaReal_gate`, `TestCohere2R7bReal_gate`, `TestGptOssReal_gate`,
  `TestGptOssReal_logitParity`, `TestGraniteReal_oracle`; three tags shipped since; all five are
  still `awaitingFirstConfirmation`, so a regression in any of them today is "an ITEM".
- **Fix:** promote the five from the 09-06 log (`gate_ledger.py promote`); give
  `TestParity_everyRequiredGateIsConfirmed` an age bound (> 7 days or spanning a tag ⇒ fail); commit
  the seven tiny fixtures (KB–MB deterministic random-weight models — the policy's own promotion
  rule) so the census and the T1 gates run in CI; make the census `Fail` rather than `Skip` for a
  listed fixture dir whose weights are absent.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** reopened G-04 (mechanism fixed 09-02, obligation not
  honoured since); sibling of C-03 (09-02)'s disposition.

  **DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Francis promoted
  11 gates from the 09-06 sweep (tree `eaa8bb23`), each test function unchanged since, so each
  source key matches what ran: the five named above plus `TestGraniteReal_gate`,
  `TestGlm4MoeAir_gate`, `TestLagunaGGUF_gate`, `TestLagunaReal_gate`, `TestQwen38GGUF_gate` and
  `TestQwen38Real_gate`, which passed in the same sweep and sat pending. They left
  `awaitingFirstConfirmation`; 22 entries remain.

  **The age bound, decided 2026-09-11: tied to releases, not the calendar.** The Fix's 7-day bound
  was rejected: it fails CI at the maintainer's pace, not on anything that shipped. Instead, a
  pending gate may ride through one release unconfirmed, never two.
  `TestParity_noPendingGateOutlivesARelease` (`cmd/gate/release_pending_test.go`) fails when any
  entry is dated before the previous release. The previous release comes from the CHANGELOG's dated
  headers, because the release workflow's checkout carries no tags. It enforces only when
  `GOINFER_RELEASE_TAG` is set: in RELEASING.md's pre-flight, and as the first step of
  `release-assets.yml`'s build job. Otherwise it skips and names the would-be blockers. Today that is
  18 of the 22 remaining entries, those dated before v0.17.2 (2026-09-08); the other 4 were added
  since. The rule itself is `TestParity_pendingOutlivingRelease`, which runs everywhere; changing
  its `<` to `<=` turns it red.

  **The five tiny fixtures are COMMITTED, 2026-09-12** (Francis's decision). granite-dense,
  ministral3, olmo_hybrid, qwen3moe, smollm3 — 4.4 MB for all five, weights and config JSONs
  together, with `.gitignore` exceptions in the same style as mixtral-tiny/cohere-tiny/llama-tiny.
  Each was confirmed green on nobara-pc FIRST: forward parity cosine ≥ 0.999999999999 with exact
  argmax, and its own `TestSerializeCensus_noSilentFieldDrop` subtest passing. Committing a
  fixture whose gate is red only moves a red into CI, which is the failure mode this item is
  about, inverted. The dir-level ignores had to be REMOVED rather than negated — git does not
  descend into an excluded directory, so `!dir/file` under an ignored `dir/` cannot re-include
  anything.

  The other two stay out, for stated reasons rather than by omission: **olmo3-tiny**'s forward
  golden is red on the pre-registered config-parsing regression, and **bailing_hybrid-tiny** does
  not exist on the Linux box (its pin script needs a venv that box lacks), so it is the MacBook's
  to commit.

  **`TestQwen38GGUF_weightDiff` FIXED and now PASSES, 2026-09-12 — and the bug was in the GATE,
  not the loader.** This item read the test's own failure message ("GGUF loader transform bug")
  as a finding; it was the message that was wrong. The gate applied ONE 0.999 cosine floor to
  every tensor, inherited from its MoE sibling, whose asset is a uniform Q8_0 file. This one's
  asset is unsloth's UD-Q4_K_M — a *dynamic* quant that assigns a different bit-width per tensor
  by sensitivity, nine ggml types in one file. Under a single bar the gate had stopped describing
  the loader and started describing whichever tensor the quantizer spent the fewest bits on.

  The measurement settles it: over layers 0–3 the agreement is a function of the SOURCE QUANT
  ALONE — F32 1.000000 (max|diff| exactly 0), Q8_0 0.99998, Q6_K 0.99975, Q5_K 0.99920, Q4_K
  0.99700 — and the two "failures" were exactly, and only, the Q4_K tensors. Q4_K spans 7e-5
  across two different layer kinds and five tensor roles, which no transform defect can do, and
  it sits 3e-4 from the first-principles Q4_K dequant estimate. The old bar was also sitting
  INSIDE the Q5_K band: `q_proj`, `v_proj` and `out_proj` cleared 0.999 by 2e-4, so the green half
  of that gate was luck, one sensitivity choice away from more "transform bugs". The loader was
  correct throughout — and the F32 tensors being bit-exact (`negExpA`, `dt_bias`, `conv1d`,
  `ssm_norm` all at max|diff| 0) says the −exp(A_log) bake and the value-head reorder are not
  merely close but exact.

  The fix makes the floor per tensor, keyed on the source quant read from the GGUF's tensor-info
  table (`ggufQuantCosFloor`, `decoder/gguf_tensorquant_test.go`), each budget ~2× the measured
  (1−cos). **Loosening a gate demands proof it can still go red, so that was measured too:**
  deleting one real transform (the `untileVHeads` on `in_proj_z`) took that tensor to 0.045919 —
  47704% of its budget — while every other tensor stayed green, so the failure named the one
  broken transform. Dequant noise tops out at 61% of budget; a transform bug is three orders of
  magnitude past it. Logs: `goinfer-logs/qwen38-weightdiff-20260912-104137.log` (PASS) and
  `…-MUTATION-20260912-*.log` (the red). CLAUDE.md's "a tight real-checkpoint bar can catch a bug
  in the reference, not just in goinfer" generalises here to the bar itself.

  Still open, waiting on a decision:
  - **Promoting `TestQwen38GGUF_weightDiff`.** It is still `FIRST-RUN`; it now has a real PASS on
    nobara-pc with the log above. `promoted_by` means a person checked the gate's value, so this
    one is Francis's to record, not something a session should self-attribute — especially as the
    assertion itself just changed.
  - **The census failing rather than skipping** a listed fixture whose weights are absent. The
    five fixtures above now make it RUN in CI for those families, which was the substance of the
    complaint; failing-instead-of-skipping is the remaining half and is still undecided.

### G-04 · `deps_hash` was refreshed by hand, bypassing `refresh_parity_hashes.sh`'s refusal, at least five times while a required golden was red — and `-update` has no such refusal

- **Where:** `scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh:197-204` (refuses on any unexcluded FAIL), `decoder/
  decoder/parity_manifest_test.go:301-304,341-346` (`-update` rewrites every family unconditionally),
  `:167-176` (`merge` likewise); log entries at lines 1210–1230, 1255–1270, 2225–2250, 2585–2600
  ("via scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh's underlying mechanism"; "Independently verified both halves
  by hand rather than bypass on faith"); db0869e (the olmo3 rope fix that regenerated
  `olmo3_forward_golden.json`) and 9c6fc07 ("Non-numeric core refresh; validated_at preserved for
  every family").
- **Failure:** the refusal is the only mechanical link between "goldens green" and "hash
  refreshed", and it lives outside the test binary. The escape is one command, taken repeatedly
  with `olmo3` red, each commit asserting "pre-existing, unrelated" in prose. When the cause was
  found the golden itself changed — a numeric change — and the refresh still preserved
  `validated_at` for it. The refresh is also all-or-nothing across families, so families whose
  golden SKIPPED on the refreshing box get their hash refreshed on other families' evidence.
- **Fix:** make `-update` run the golden selector in-process (or require a proof token the script
  writes); refresh per family only when a golden exercising it PASSED in the same run.
- **Confidence:** confirmed for the bypass (the commit messages say so); plausible that the olmo3
  red was a merge regression of the 09-07 fix (the 09-09 merge hand-resolved `registry.go`).
- **Prior:** new; CLAUDE.md's "re-baselining a floor because a number moved" class.
- **DISPOSITION — the bypass-by-hand half is FIXED, the per-family half is not.** Added
  `KNOWN_UNRELATED_FAILURES`, a checked-in, documented allowlist inside
  `scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh` itself: an entry (`TestName|YYYY-MM-DD added|reason`)
  excludes THAT test's failure, alone, from the refusal — a NEW or unlisted failure still refuses
  exactly as before, the excluded failure is still printed (never silently absorbed), and the
  PROOF block records which entries fired so a reviewer of the resulting commit sees it too. A
  malformed entry (wrong field count) aborts immediately, before the goldens even run. Seeded with
  `TestOlmo3_forwardParity` (this exact cosine, 0.9899728674750051, already independently
  confirmed unrelated across at least three prior investigations named in this doc and
  docs/task-gpu-paths-2026-09.md). Verified end-to-end on this box: the unmodified script now
  completes (exit 0) where it previously refused; renaming the allowlist entry so it no longer
  matches correctly restores the refusal on the SAME real failure; a 1-field (malformed) entry
  aborts before the golden run. This closes the "one command bypasses the refusal with no
  mechanical link to any investigation" half. It does NOT address the OTHER half this item names —
  "all-or-nothing across families, so families whose golden SKIPPED get their hash refreshed on
  other families' evidence" — which needs the deeper per-family/in-process redesign the Fix bullet
  describes; that is unstarted.

  **The `TestOlmo3_forwardParity` regression itself: FIXED 2026-09-12, two real bugs, not one.**
  Bisecting found the failure predates this branch entirely (fails identically before and after
  today's merges), tracked exactly here, so root-caused it directly rather than re-allowlisting.

  **Bug 1 (code):** `olmo3Architecture` (`decoder/registry.go`) built ONE rotary table from
  `rope_parameters["full_attention"]` and discarded `["sliding_attention"]` entirely, on the
  belief (this section's own prior comment, "0b0f5c9") that the real model shares one table
  across every layer. Verified WRONG by calling the actual `Olmo3RotaryEmbedding.forward()`
  against the pinned `transformers==5.15.0` (the same venv `pin_olmo3_tiny.py` uses): its
  signature takes an explicit `layer_type` argument and returns a DIFFERENT (cos, sin) pair per
  call — `sliding_attention` comes back with `attention_scaling == 1.0` and no YaRN
  interpolation, `full_attention` comes back YaRN-scaled (mscale 1.1 on this fixture). A function
  requiring a layer_type argument cannot be building one shared table. This was the ORIGINAL
  family design (`docs/task-families-2026-09.md` G2, ship-time cosine 0.9999999999997883) before
  db0869e's "one flat table" fix — itself responding to a REAL error at the time
  ("rope_theta must be >0" on a fresh regeneration) but misdiagnosing the cause. Fixed by
  restoring the per-layer-type split: `full`→`RoPEGlobalBase`/`ropeScaling` (YaRN),
  `sliding`→`RoPELocalBase`/`ropeScalingLocal` (nil — plain RoPE at the same theta), the same
  local/global mechanism Mellum already uses.

  **Bug 2 (stale fixture, not code):** even with Bug 1 fixed, `TestOlmo3_forwardParity`'s cosine
  matched perfectly (0.9999999999997883, bit-identical to ship time) but 237 of ~288 sample/top_k
  value checks still failed by 0.01–0.06 — small relative to the vocabulary's dominant entries
  (invisible to cosine) but real. Cause: `testdata/olmo3_forward_golden.json` (git-tracked) was
  last regenerated BY db0869e — under whatever `transformers` behavior was locally installed
  09:54am 2026-09-10, which produced a FLAT `rope_parameters` (no per-layer split at all, hence
  `parseRopeFlat` "worked" then). The gitignored checkpoint/full-dump were regenerated later that
  same day (13:18) under a DIFFERENT installed `transformers` behavior that DOES nest
  `rope_parameters` by layer type — the same install confirmed above. The golden and the
  checkpoint it's meant to validate against were never from the same generation run.
  `~/.venv-nemotron3/bin/python scripts/pin_olmo3_tiny.py` regenerates all three files together
  from the CURRENT install; doing so made every remaining check pass (`maxSampleΔ=0.00000`).

  Both are necessary: Bug 1 alone (with the stale golden) still fails 237 value checks; the
  regenerated golden alone (with the old uniform-table code) reproduces the original 0.98997
  cosine failure — confirmed by testing each in isolation before combining them.

  `scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh`'s `KNOWN_UNRELATED_FAILURES` entry for this test is removed
  per its own stated rule ("remove the line the moment the test goes green again").

### G-05 · `TestOwnForward_recurrentBitMatchesTheCacheKinds` and the two spec-rollback guards carry hand-written family lists, so a family absent from BOTH the table and the list passes as "consistently non-recurrent" *(three reviewers)*

- **Where:** `decoder/ownforward_test.go:194-216` (FIXED — at audit time this was the hand-written `kinds` map:
  qwen3_5_moe, granitemoehybrid, nemotron_h, lfm2), `decoder/spec_ngram_test.go:83-91`, `decoder/spec_rollback_regression_test.go:
  20-25`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  set, hasKind := kinds[f.Name]
  if f.Recurrent != hasKind { t.Errorf(...) }
  ```
- **Failure:** for `bailing_hybrid`, `hasKind == false` and `Recurrent == false` agree; the cache
  built for it sets nothing, so `hasRecurrentState()==false` agrees again. The gate asserts the
  author's assumption back at them while C-03 is live — "the second list being born" one level down
  from the test that was written to catch it.
- **Fix:** derive the oracle from the cache the family actually builds — `m.NewCache(0)` on an
  `archWithFamily` descriptor, then assert `c.hasRecurrentState() == f.Recurrent` and fail if
  `NewCache` allocated any `[]*xxxState` slice (reflect over `KVCache`) for a family whose bit is
  false; iterate `ownForwards` in the rollback tests.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** new (the gate C-02's disposition added); V-12's class.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`), with C-03.**
  `TestOwnForward_recurrentBitMatchesTheCacheKinds` (name kept, body replaced) now asks the struct:
  each own-forward family's REAL cache is built by `NewCache`, its recurrent kinds are found by
  reflecting over `KVCache` (every `[]*struct` field except `rings`, which is positional and
  rewindable — a rule that fails SAFE, since a new positional field would be flagged rather than
  missed), and the registry bit, both `hasRecurrentState` views and `specRollbackSafe` must all
  agree with it. `decoder/recurrent_census_test.go` adds a generic fill / rewind / reset-to-zero
  check over every kind, so a missing reset arm is caught for a fifth kind too, with nothing to list.

  One deviation from the Fix note: the cache is built from `representativeConfig` +
  `resolveArchitecture`, not `archWithFamily`, because `NewCache` on `archWithFamily`'s minimal
  descriptor panics for `nemotron_h` (it indexes `blockKind[l]`). A family `representativeConfig`
  does not know fails loudly, the property `archWithFamily` already had. The two spec-rollback tests
  keep their hand lists as specific pins; the charge against them — that a family absent from both
  passes — no longer holds at the suite level, because the census asserts `specRollbackSafe` for
  every family from its real cache.

### G-06 · The G3 resident-LoRA gates cannot see C-01 or C-07: they drive per-token `Forward`, never `PrefillLast`; the seam test's fake has no `Prefiller` and a 3-token prompt; the Metal gate binds before the executor has pre-encoded *(three reviewers)*

- **Where:** `cuda/lora_resident_parity_test.go:79`, `metal/lora_resident_parity_test.go:88,97-121`,
  `gpu/lora_resident_parity_test.go:84` (`rf.Forward(…)` per token); the production call is
  `decoder/model.go:1094` (`pf.PrefillLast`); `decoder/resident_adapter_seam_test.go:41-55,194`
  (`prompt := []int{1, 2, 3}`, fake backend implemented no `Prefiller` — the CUDA/gpu half; FIXED
  2026-09-10, see the DISPOSITION note below); all three gates pass at
  cosine ≥ 0.95 while measuring 0.99996 (N-46).
- **Failure:** the gates prove the delta is applied by the kernel they call, not by the path
  `generateInto` calls for any prompt ≥ 8 tokens (CLAUDE.md's "a unit test that supplies its own
  calling convention"). The Metal gate binds (fresh executor), runs, clears, runs, and compares only
  the LAST token's logits — the one order that cannot see C-07.
- **Fix:** one case per backend that prefills through `residentPrefillSeed` (or `Session.Generate`
  on a resident model) with the adapter bound, compared to CPU `prefillLogits` with the same adapter;
  give the fake a `PrefillLast` that records whether an adapter was bound; on Metal run once WITHOUT
  the adapter first (arming the executor), then bind and compare position-0 logits.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** new.

  **DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`), the C-01/backend-
  agnostic half.** `fakeResidentAdapter` (`decoder/resident_adapter_seam_test.go`) now implements
  `Prefiller` (`PrefillLast`, incrementing a `prefillCalls` counter — records call count rather
  than "whether an adapter was bound" per the Fix note's exact wording, but the new test drives it
  with an adapter always bound, so the effect is the same: a nonzero count while bound IS the
  failure signal). A new test, `TestSeam_AdapterSessionNeverUsesBatchedPrefill`, drives a 10-token
  adapter-session prompt (above the 8-token `GOINFER_BATCHED_PREFILL` floor the old 3-token prompt
  sat under) and asserts `prefillCalls == 0`. Mutation-checked: reverting C-01's `hasAdapter` guard
  makes it fail immediately (`PrefillLast called 1 times for a 10-token adapter-session prompt`).
  This closes the finding's CUDA/`gpu` half (both share `decoder/model.go`'s
  `residentPrefillSeed` chokepoint, so one fake/one test covers both). NOT done: the Metal
  "binds before the executor has pre-encoded" half — that needs the Fix note's "on Metal run once
  WITHOUT the adapter first, then bind and compare position-0 logits" shape, which is C-07's
  territory (Metal-only, needs the Mac) and out of scope here.

### G-07 · `TestGptOssResidentParityWebGPU` cannot see a dropped expert-weight term: the fixture is bias-dominated and the 0.95 floor is below the damage

- **Where:** `gpu/gptoss_resident_test.go:43,112-118`, `scripts/gptoss_tiny_golden.py:26-31,71-74`
  (`p.copy_(torch.randn_like(p) * 0.05)` for every parameter, norm weights and biases included).
- **Failure:** a normed activation is ~0.05/element, so each matmul term is ~0.05·0.05·√K ≈ 0.02
  (K=64) against a ~0.05 bias; the expert down matmul (K=32) is ~0.01 against a 0.05 down-bias.
  Zeroing it — C-06 — moves the logits a few percent, inside a 0.95 floor. The recorded 0.9943 for
  WebGPU vs Metal's 0.9989 on the same fixture is consistent with a small missing term. The same
  construction makes the sink insensitive (q·k ≈ 0 ⇒ dropping the sink rescales by n/(n+1)).
- **Fix:** a mutation check (compare against the CPU forward with the expert down output, and
  separately the sink, zeroed — like `TestApplySoftcap_mutation`), or a fixture with norm weights
  ~1 and biases ~0; at minimum gate int4 gpt-oss at the argmax-exact bar Metal's record shows (8/8).
- **Confidence:** plausible (analytic magnitude; the sandbox cannot run the fixture). The defect it
  fails to catch is confirmed.
- **Prior:** new; G-06/G-07 (09-02)'s class.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`), with C-06.**
  `TestGptOssResidentParityWebGPU` now holds cosine >= 0.998 and argmax 8/8. That bar was
  registered before C-06's fix, and the C-06 defect fails it (0.994273, one argmax flip). The
  term-level check is `TestGptOssDownW4_parity`, whose fixture makes the matmul dominate the bias.

  The attention-sink half was measured the same day, and the same bar already sees it. With the
  sink dropped in the WebGPU resident runner (`hasSinkUni` forced to 0 in `gpu/decoderunner.go`),
  the gate goes red: minimum cosine 0.986949, argmax wrong at 4 of 8 positions; unmutated it reads
  0.999974 and 0 of 8. The finding's own rescale, n/(n+1), is 1/2 at the first position, so it is
  not small where the context is short. No seam was added to repeat this in-tree: it would put a
  hook into production code to re-run what one mutation records, and the test already fails if the
  fixture stops declaring `FeatAttnSink`.

### G-09 · PTX freshness has no device-free check; the only check is exact-NVRTC-patch-bound and its absence is a counted skip on a PASS *(two reviewers)*

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/gpu.go:893-950` (`probeNVRTC` wants exactly `V12.9.86`), `:973-1044,
  1030-1039,1438-1443` (skip listed, not failed), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (no PTX step),
  `cuda/backend.go:772-784` (`fns` table bound from embedded blobs), `cuda/kernel_local_memory_test
  .go:23,68-72` (has the `.visible .entry` regex; device-gated), `CHANGELOG.md:20-35` (the glue.ptx
  incident: "every CUDA resident build, on any machine … silently declining to CPU").
- **Failure:** 23c46b1 and 5b44383 both describe a `.ptx` lagging its `.cu`; the fix is a
  regenerated blob. A `.cu`+Go edit committed without the regen compiles, vets, passes `-short` CI,
  and at runtime `cuModuleGetFunction` fails → `BuildResident` declines → every family on CPU. The
  consolidating reviewer ran a static name/param-count/kind check over all 31 `.cu`/`.ptx` pairs and
  every production launch site: **0 drift at c7ef16a** — the class is clean today and unguarded.
- **Fix:** a `-tags cuda` device-free test: `NewComputePipeline(<mod>, "<name>")` in production
  sources → `.visible .entry <name>` in the embedded blob, plus `__global__` param count/kind vs
  `.param`; run in `cuda-suite` and CI's `-short` job. Make the gate's PTX group FAIL when zero
  artifacts could be verified.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** sibling of G-01/G-02 (09-02).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took both halves.
  `TestPTX_matchesSourcesAndBindings` (`cuda/ptx_consistency_test.go`) reads files only, so it runs
  in CI's existing `-short` cuda job. Every `__global__` in each `.cu` must be a `.visible .entry` in
  its `.ptx` with the same parameter count and kinds; no `.ptx` may carry an entry its `.cu` dropped;
  and every kernel name the production Go code binds, in all five binding forms, must be an entry in
  some embedded PTX. It found 22 pairs, 59 kernels and 55 bound names with 0 drift, as the audit's
  own static check did. Three mutations each go red: an extra `.cu` parameter with no rebuild, a
  binding to a name no PTX defines, and a renamed `__global__` (caught in both directions). It
  caught a real lag the same day: G-11's edited `rope_kv_batched` before its PTX was rebuilt (14
  parameters against 15). `gate gpu`'s PTX group now FAILs when zero artifacts could be verified.

### G-10 · `webgpu-parity` selects only `ResidentParity`; seven gate-shaped, `goinfer_testhooks`-tagged WebGPU tests — the freshest G6/G3 code included — run in no gate cell and no CI runner

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/gpu.go:1266-1272` (`Run: "ResidentParity"`), `:1234-1237` (suite cell
  untagged), `cmd/gate/parity.go:871-897` + `cmd/gate/parity_test.go:792` (listed-or-excluded test is Metal
  only); unmatched: `gpu/backend_w4a8_test.go:23` (`TestWebGPUBackend_MatmulW4A8_matchesCPU`, the
  G6 staged-int4 gate), `gpu/forwardn_parity_test.go:26`, `gpu/uploadkv_parity_test.go:38`,
  `gpu/mamba_realinput_test.go:22`, `gpu/moe_w4a8_expert_test.go:15`, `gpu/deltanet_test.go:41`,
  `gpu/moe_test.go` (`TestMoERoute_parity`).
- **Fix:** a `webgpuParityRun` covering `Parity|_parity|matchesCPU|matchesSequential` (or run the
  cell unfiltered) plus `TestWebGPUGateIsListedOrExplicitlyNotRequired`.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** sibling of V-07 (Metal-only fix); partial regression of G-09.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix.
  `webgpuParityRun` (`Parity|_parity|matchesCPU|matchesSequential`) is shared by the
  `webgpu-parity` cell and the new `TestWebGPUGateIsListedOrExplicitlyNotRequired`
  (`cmd/gate/webgpu_listing_test.go`), the WebGPU twin of V-07's Metal scan. The scan derives the
  gate-shaped set from the tagged `gpu/` test files and fails on any test the pattern does not reach
  unless `webgpuNotRequired` names it with a reason. Its predicate adds the lowercase `_parity`
  suffix and `matchesCPU`/`matchesSequential` to `gateShaped`'s `Parity`; without that, four of the
  unreached tests were invisible to the scan as well. Red on the old constant: 16 gate-shaped tests,
  9 matched by nothing. On this box the widened cell ran 52 pass / 0 fail / 6 skip.

### G-11 · `TestKernelFMALint` cannot see a MAC whose multiply is to the RIGHT of the add — and one ships in a linted kernel

- **Where:** `cuda/kernel_fma_lint_test.go:66-67` (`macExpr` matches `a * b + c`, not `c + a * b`),
  `cuda/prefill_batched.cu:144` (`qTempScale = 1.0f + attnTempBeta * log1pf(...)`), contrast
  `cuda/resident.go:2754-2751` (decode computes the same scalar on the host in float64).
- **Failure:** for Ministral 3 (the only `FeatAttnTemp` family, resident since 84b54de) the Q rows
  of a batched pass are not bit-identical to the sequential path: K/V unaffected (scale is Q-only),
  but spec-verify rows (`ForwardN`/`PrefillLastNArgmax`) can flip an argmax on a near-tie, breaking
  "lossless" for that family. The reviewer re-ran the lint's regexes over all 21 linted kernels;
  this is the only bare float MAC left.
- **Fix:** a second pattern for `[+\-] [^;/]* \* `; rewrite line 144 with `__fmaf_rn` or pass a
  per-row host-computed table.
- **Confidence:** confirmed (regex and kernel text); the flip is plausible (ULP-level).
- **Prior:** sibling of R-04 (09-02, the for-header hole in the same lint).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took both halves of the
  Fix. The lint gains the reversed pattern, with parentheses excluded (`(a - b) * c` and
  `c + f(a * b)` are not multiply-adds) and two integer idioms whitelisted by name; red before the
  fix on `prefill_batched.cu`'s query-scale line, the only real hit. `rope_kv_batched` now takes a
  per-row table built on the host with decode's own float64 expression and rounded to f32 once, so
  the batched scale is bit-identical to decode by construction. `prefill_batched.ptx` was rebuilt at
  its recorded 12.9.86 toolchain after a control rebuild reproduced it byte for byte; only
  `rope_kv_batched` changed. `TestMinistral3BatchedPrefillMatchesSequentialCUDA` compares batched
  and sequential logits bit for bit over 40 rows. On this fixture the old f32 formula happened to
  agree, so that test was green before the fix too: the change is the guarantee, not the repair of
  an observed flip. Its sensitivity is shown by mutation: a table 1 ULP off on the temperature rows
  turns 7 of 32 rows red.

### G-12 · Two `serve check` rows certify what they do not read: "tools" passes turn two on any 200, "stop sequences" passes when the stop never fires

- **Where:** `internal/servecheck/check.go:485-516` (turn two: `len(out.Choices) == 0` is the
  only check; `Content` decoded, never examined; doc comment `:438-441` says "Both halves are
  checked"), `:265-300` (`strings.Contains(txt, "5")` leak check; `finish_reason` printed, not
  asserted); fakes in `internal/servecheck/check_test.go:109,193-219` drive only the passing direction.
- **Failure:** a server that answers the `role:"tool"` turn with an empty message or another
  `get_weather` call (the M-18 agent-livelock shape) prints `ok … answer in 2 turns`; a model that
  replies "Sure, I can count." is `ok` on stops. The doctor was built as the cold user's first check.
- **Fix:** require non-empty `Content` and no `tool_calls` on turn two; run the stop row through
  `/v1/messages` and assert `stop_reason=="stop_sequence"`/`stop_sequence=="5"` (the Anthropic
  shape reports which fired), or assert "4" present and "5" absent.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** new (f1b5923).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix's "4 present
  and 5 absent" variant for the stop row, and both halves for tools. Turn two must have non-empty
  content and no `tool_calls`; a repeated tool call is reported as M-18's shape. The stop row must
  reach "4" as well as not leak "5". The fakes now drive the failing direction too: an empty turn
  two, a repeated tool call, a stop that never fires, and a passing stop control. Each was red
  before the fix, and dropping each new check turns its test red again.

### G-13 · Smaller instances

- **(a)** `decoder/staged_device_note_test.go:63-70` pins `decoder/residency.go:1030-1031`'s "no GPU
  dispatch exists for this quant here either" for `int4` on webgpu — false since db81b08
  (`decoder/weightmat.go:669`, `gpu/backend.go:160`); the test goes red only if someone fixes the banner.
- **(b)** `cuda/kernel_local_memory_test.go:32-56` hand-lists 15 of 22 embedded modules (missing
  `gptoss_act`, `lora`, `attn_block`, `attn_img_prefill`, `rope_mrope_prefill`, `layernorm_quant`,
  `gelu_quant`), so its "moe_route declares the maximum local memory" precondition
  (`cuda/backend.go:1384-1400`) is unverified for the gpt-oss expert-cache path it exists for. Derive
  from the `//go:embed` list as `TestKernelFMALint_coversEmbeddedPTX` does.
- **(c)** `decoder/gguf_maxpositions_test.go:19-20,107-109` hand-counts 18 cases and says a future
  family "fails here too"; a 20th `case` in `ggufConfig` with no `MaxPositions` passes untouched.
- **(d)** `.github/workflows/release-assets.yml:109-111,244-246,387-389` — `grep -qa "$TAG"` on
  darwin/windows assets is satisfied by the recorded `-ldflags` build setting whether or not the
  `-X` took; use `go version -m … | grep 'injectedVersion=$TAG'`.
- **(e)** `decoder/envvars_registry_test.go:32` matches only `os.Getenv("GOINFER_…")`; four
  `cmd/gate` knobs read via `env()` (`cmd/gate/configs.go:14-17,94`: `GOINFER_GATE_MODELS`,
  `GOINFER_HEAVY_RUN/TIMEOUT/PKGS`) are undocumented and invisible; `os.LookupEnv` likewise.
- **(f)** `scripts/readme_smoke.sh:55-66` `smoke-help` checks that a flag NAME exists, never that
  its value parses — the README's `-weight-cache 6GiB` (M-40) is green.
- **(g)** `examples/embed/main_test.go:54-56` turns every run failure into a SKIP, and its
  TinyLlama fixture takes the no-template path, so removing `chat.Detect` from the example cannot
  fail it.
- **(h)** `gpu/gemma3_resident_parity_test.go:52-55`, `gpu/nemotron_moe_resident_test.go:35-38` —
  `os.Stat(dir)` passes on the tracked `config.json`, `Load` fails on the gitignored weights, and
  the skip says "no webgpu device" (sibling of V-06).
- **(i)** `metal/moe_model_test.go:302-327` (`TestMoE_declinesPrefill`, 8-token prompt) and
  `metal/prefill_gate_test.go:75,254` (K=256) fail deterministically under the 512 floor landed
  09-09 (f12db39); `metal-suite` runs the former; no post-flip gate log exists under
  `docs/measurements/`.
- **(j)** `cuda/parity_floor_control_test.go:22-79`, `cuda/theta_probe_test.go:50-59`,
  `metal/theta_probe_test.go:50-59` — `Test*` measurement harnesses with no assertion, counted as
  PASS in the parity cells (G-10 (09-02)'s third instance).
- **(k)** `decoder/fidelity_testhook.go` `TeacherForcedTop1AgreementForTest` returns `0, -1` on a
  length mismatch — a caller checking only `firstDivergence == -1` passes vacuously.

  **DISPOSITION — 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Ten of eleven fixed; (i) needs
  the Mac.
  - **(a) FIXED.** The note now says int4 decode runs on webgpu and multi-token prefill on the host
    (`QuantBackend4.MatmulW4A8` declines at M>1); `int4mix`'s note, which made the same claim about
    its FFN tensors, likewise. `TestStagedDeviceNote` expects the decode claim: red on the old text
    for both, green on the new.
  - **(b) FIXED, and it was a live defect.** `ptxModules()` is derived from `kernels.go`'s embeds
    (`TestPTXModules_coverEveryEmbed`: red with 7 missing, green 22/22). The census then found
    `route_gptoss` at 4608 B/thread, above `moe_route`'s 4416, on the gpt-oss path the precondition
    exists for. Measured (`TestRouteGptOssGrowsPoolPastMoERoute`, RTX 2070 SUPER, assertions written
    before the run): `moe_route`'s first launch takes 138,412,032 B, then `route_gptoss`'s takes a
    further 8,388,608 B, which on gpt-oss landed after `allocSlots` had sized the cache.
    `BuildResident`'s warm-up now also launches `route_gptoss` wherever it is bound; with both
    forced, a gpt-oss-shaped launch takes 0 B. The census pins `route_gptoss` and
    `rope_kv_mrope_batched`, requires `moe_route` to be the maximum of every other kernel, and goes
    red naming `backend.go` if the new warm-up is deleted.
  - **(c) FIXED.** The covered set comes from `ggufConfig`'s own case labels (19, two of them
    aliases); a fake label turns it red.
  - **(d) FIXED, by a different mechanism than the one described.** Under `-trimpath` the
    `-ldflags` are not recorded in the build info, and `go version -m … | grep injectedVersion=`
    would fail every build. The vacuity is real by another route: Go stamps the main module's
    version, which for a root-module build at a tag checkout is `$TAG` or `$TAG+dirty`, so
    `grep -qa "$TAG"` passes whether or not the `-X` took. The windows serve asset is the exposed
    one: it is built from the root after the metal/cuda builds `go mod edit` the same checkout. The
    byte check now requires a copy of `$TAG` not preceded by a tab, since every build-info copy is a
    tab-separated field. The native `--version | grep -qF "$TAG"` had the same hole, because
    `$TAG+dirty` contains `$TAG`; it is now an exact match on the version field. Measured with a
    misspelled `-X` symbol on a dirty tree: both old checks pass the mutant, both new checks fail it
    and pass the good build.
  - **(e) FIXED.** The registry matches `os.Getenv`, `os.LookupEnv` and `cmd/gate`'s `env()`. Red,
    it named the four `cmd/gate` knobs, now documented in `docs/env-vars.md`.
  - **(f) FIXED, with M-47.** The README example reads `-weight-cache 6`, and `smoke-help` parses
    each serve line's values (then `-h`, under a timeout). Red on `6GiB` with the binary's own parse
    error.
  - **(g) FIXED.** The test uses the qwen2.5-0.5b-instruct fixture, whose ChatML template
    `chat.Detect` recognises (asserted first); a run error on a present fixture is Fatal; a reply
    over 400 chars fails. Measured 32 chars templated and 852 raw; with the example's `chat.Detect`
    branch disabled it goes red at 852.
  - **(h) FIXED.** Both stat `model.safetensors`. Absent weights skip as "no fixture weights", no
    device as "no webgpu device", and a Load failure with both present is a FAIL.
