  - **(i) OPEN.** Metal-only; needs the Mac.
  - **(j) FIXED for `TestParityFloorControl`,** which now needs `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS=1
    GOINFER_PARITY_FLOOR_CONTROL=1`. The two theta probes already needed `GOINFER_THETA_PROBE`
    (since 2026-08-31, before this audit), and nothing in `cmd/`, `scripts/` or `.github/` sets it,
    so no gate cell counted them as a PASS.
  - **(k) FIXED.** Caller errors return `(0, 0)`, never the `-1` that means "every position
    agreed". Red on the old code for all four caller-error shapes.

---

## §3 — Major

### Resident path and serving

#### M-04 · `.giw` bundles resolve EOS from the serialized `config.json` only — `generation_config.json`'s extra stop ids are lost, C-11 (09-02)'s symptom on the loader its fix relies on
- **Where:** `decoder/model.go:327` (`.giw` branch: `eosIDs: w.Cfg.EOSIDs()`), `decoder/model.go:
  372` (safetensors: `resolveEOSIDs(dir, &w.Cfg)` — the only merging branch), `decoder/serialize.go:
  219,847`, `internal/prequant/prequant.go:149-158`, `decoder/config.go:1303-1317`.
- **Failure:** `cmd/prequant` on a Qwen3 HF dir loads `{151645, 151643}` but serializes `w.Cfg`
  with config.json's raw `151645`; `Load(".giw")` builds `m.eosIDs = {151645}`; a `/v1/completions`
  turn that emits `<|endoftext|>` decodes it as content and runs to `max_tokens`. `blockSpecStopSet`
  inherits the same list. Also `decoder/gguf.go:108-108` claims a generation_config-next-to-GGUF
  fallback that `os.DirFS(<file path>)` can never satisfy.
- **Fix:** write the resolved list into the bundle (`Cfg.EOSTokenID` as a JSON list, or bundle
  metadata) and read it in `Load`'s `.giw` branch and `NewModel`.

**DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-12.** `decoder/model.go`'s staged-load branch now writes the
RESOLVED `resolveEOSIDs(dir, &w.Cfg)` set back into `w.Cfg.EOSTokenID` (JSON-marshaled) before
constructing the `Model`, not just onto the `Model`'s own `eosIDs` field — the first fix option
this finding named. `w.Cfg` is exactly what `internal/prequant`'s `SerializeWeightsToForTarget`
writes into a `.giw` bundle, and `Load`'s `.giw` branch already read `eosIDs` straight from
`w.Cfg.EOSIDs()`, so no change was needed there or in `NewModel` — fixing the WRITE side is
sufficient once `Cfg` itself carries the full set. `Cfg.EOSTokenID` has no other reader anywhere
in the tree that needs the unresolved config.json-only value (`EOSIDs()` is its only consumer),
so overwriting it is safe. Also fixed the collateral bug this finding's own failure text named:
`decoder/gguf.go:108-108`'s claimed generation_config-next-to-GGUF fallback used `os.DirFS(dir)`
where `dir` is the `.gguf` FILE path for a GGUF load, so it could never open anything — now
`resolveEOSIDs` is called against `filepath.Dir(dir)` when `dir` ends `.gguf`, matching where a
GGUF conversion's `generation_config.json` actually lives.
`TestLoad_staged_resolvesEOSIntoConfig` and `TestGIWRoundTrip_preservesMergedEOSIDs`
(`decoder/eos_giw_roundtrip_test.go`) are the new gates: a synthetic Qwen3-shaped fixture (one eos
id in config.json, a second only in generation_config.json) is loaded staged, asserted merged
into `Weights().Cfg`, then round-tripped through the SAME `SerializeWeightsToForTarget` +
`giw.WriteStream` calls `cmd/prequant` makes and reloaded from the `.giw` file — the second id
survives. Mutation-proven (skipping the `Cfg` write-back reproduces the exact reported symptom:
the round-tripped model loses the generation_config-only id).

**Correction, found during the killswitch/aikit-v1.41.0-bump-into-main consolidation
(2026-09-12).** The "not separately gated — lower risk" line above was wrong on the risk call,
not just on coverage: `decoder.Load`'s `.gguf`-file branch is not the only resident-side entry
point. `StreamTranscodeGGUF` (`decoder/gguf.go`) — the streaming transcode `cmd/prequant` uses for
a model too large to hold resident — calls `ggufConfig(g)` directly and never goes through
`Load` at all, so it never picked up the write-back either. This was not theoretical: the
pre-existing `TestStreamTranscodeMatchesResident` (`internal/prequant/stream_test.go`, byte-diffs
a streamed `.giw` against `Load`+`SerializeWeights`'s resident one) caught it immediately on the
very fixture this fix's own tests use, once `Load`'s side started writing a resolved multi-id
array and the streaming side kept writing the old raw scalar — "bundles differ BEFORE the quant
label (2730 vs 2710 B)". Fixed the same way, inline in `StreamTranscodeGGUF` right after
`ggufConfig` succeeds. No new test added — `TestStreamTranscodeMatchesResident` already gates this
exactly, and gated it correctly the first time; the miss was scope (not knowing this third entry
point existed), not test coverage.

#### M-06 · `GenerateVL`'s doc says `imgHash == 0` "just never matches"; the code matches 0 == 0
- **Where:** `decoder/generate_vl.go:58-61`, `decoder/resident_reuse.go:146-147,170-183`.
- **Failure:** a library caller following the doc passes 0 on two turns with different images of
  the same token length at the same position → turn 2 takes the P9a fast path, skips the tower and
  reuses image A's KV for image B. Serve and the agent hash the bytes, so the served path is not
  exposed; the public API is.
- **Fix:** treat `Hash == 0` as "no claim" in `residentReuseLen` and never record a block with 0.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (`7e247ec`).** `residentReuseLen`'s block-match guard now
  requires `blk.hash != 0` before comparing hashes, so two callers that both pass `imgHash=0`
  no longer satisfy `claim.Hash == blk.hash` by coincidence. The block is still RECORDED (not
  skipped) rather than omitted — an unrecorded region would fall through to the unguarded
  per-position comparison, which is M-07's separate, already-tracked hazard (content-independent
  placeholder ids matching across different images). `TestResidentReuseLen_zeroHashNeverMatches`
  added, and mutation-checked (fails red with the guard reverted).

#### M-08 · `Model.Plan` admits by feature set alone, not `ResidentEligible`: it reports "resident" for families the runtime declines, and "unrecognised backend declines" is false for feature-free arches *(two reviewers)*
- **Where:** `decoder/fitplan.go:163-178` (`MissingResidentFeatures` only); contrast
  `decoder/features.go:292-301` (`decodeRunnerEligible && features && moeCap && perLayerGeom`).
- **Failure:** Llama-4 on cuda → "resident" (declined at `decoder/residency.go:373-373`); dense Gemma 4 on
  webgpu → "resident" (`PerLayerGeomOK` false — db81b08's own "third bug"); Kimi-K2 on metal (384 >
  256 experts) → "resident"; `Plan("nonsense", …)` on plain Llama → nil feature map → "resident".
  `goinfer-chat fit` and the startup banner print these.
- **Fix:** gate on `ResidentEligible(arch, backend)` plus the Nemotron int4 policy.

**DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-12.** `decoder/fitplan.go`'s `Plan` now calls
`ResidentEligible(m.w.arch, backend)` right after the existing `MissingResidentFeatures` check,
declining with a reason naming the four things `MissingResidentFeatures` alone cannot see: an
unregistered backend name, an arch whose own forward the resident runner does not bridge
(`decodeRunnerEligible`), a MoE router past the backend's fixed-size scoreboard
(`residentMoECapacityOK`), and unimplemented per-layer attention geometry
(`residentPerLayerGeomOK`) — exactly the four failure cases this finding named (Llama-4/cuda,
dense Gemma-4/webgpu, Kimi-K2/metal, and any unrecognised backend name against a feature-free
arch). The Nemotron int4 policy note in the original fix line is a separate, narrower runtime
POLICY gate (`Model.DecodeRunnerEligible`, per `ResidentEligible`'s own doc comment distinguishing
capability from policy) that `Plan` did not claim to cover and this fix does not add — out of
scope for this finding, which was about capability-eligibility only.
`TestPlan_unrecognisedBackendDeclinesEvenForAFeatureFreeArch` (`decoder/fitplan_test.go`) is the
new gate: a plain Llama fixture (confirmed zero `RequiredResidentFeatures`) against an
unregistered backend name now declines instead of falling through to resident — mutation-proven
(removing the new `ResidentEligible` check reddens it). The Plan doc comment's own claim about
unrecognised-backend handling, which was false before this fix (a "doc comment claiming coverage
that is not coverage", CLAUDE.md's own named class), now points at the check that actually
enforces it.

#### M-13 · Non-streaming responses are written with no write deadline while the decode mutex is held — M-17 (09-02)'s sibling on every non-SSE route
- **Where (original `lm.enter(w)` call sites; K2's halt check later widened the signature to
  `lm.enter(w, s.haltState)`, moving them again):** `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:369-375`
  (`writeJSON`), inside the `enter … defer lm.exit()` window at
  `internal/serveapp/openai.go:579,672`, `internal/serveapp/tools.go:62`,
  `internal/serveapp/responses.go:159,237`, `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:273`;
  `internal/serveapp/main.go:707-637` (`WriteTimeout stays 0`).
- **Failure:** `stream:false, logprobs:true, top_logprobs:20, max_tokens:8000` → a ~10 MB body; a
  client that stops reading blocks `Encode` in `net.Conn.Write` with no deadline; the model's worker,
  its queue slot and an inflight slot are pinned until the client closes.
- **Fix:** `http.NewResponseController(w).SetWriteDeadline(…)` in `writeJSON`, or `lm.exit()`
  before the write (the body is fully built by then).

**DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-12.** `internal/serveapp/helpers.go`'s `writeJSON` (now `:369-375`,
shifted from the original citation by this fix's own addition) calls
`http.NewResponseController(w).SetWriteDeadline(time.Now().Add(jsonWriteTimeout))` before
`WriteHeader`/`Encode`, the first fix option this finding offered. `jsonWriteTimeout` is a
package-level `var` (30s, matching `sseWriteTimeout`'s existing bound and test-overridable the
same way) rather than `lm.exit()`-before-write: the second option would have moved the decode
mutex's release point, a bigger behavioral change than a write deadline for a finding about the
write blocking, not about mutex scope.
`TestWriteJSON_stalledClientFailsTheWriteInsteadOfBlocking`
(`internal/serveapp/writejson_test.go`) mirrors `sse_writer_test.go`'s own real-connection gate
for the identical M-17 (09-02) issue: a real `httptest.NewServer` handler writes a 32 MB body
through `writeJSON`, the client connects and never reads, and the handler must still return
within the deadline — it does, in ~50ms against a 40ms test timeout, proving the write actually
blocked and the deadline actually fired (mutation-proven: removing the `SetWriteDeadline` call
hangs the same test until its own 15s failure timeout).

**Flakiness found and fixed during the killswitch/aikit-v1.41.0-bump-into-main consolidation
(2026-09-12), ~1/5 measured.** `jsonWriteTimeout` is set before `WriteHeader`, so on a slow or
loaded connection the deadline can fire before the client has even finished reading the response
status line — `http.Get` then returns an error (typically EOF) instead of a `*Response`, which
the test's first version treated as a fatal setup failure rather than the SAME underlying event
(the deadline cutting the connection) surfacing at a different point. Separately, an earlier fix
attempt introduced a worse bug while chasing this: closing `resp.Body` immediately inside the
goroutine that calls `http.Get`, rather than holding it open for the test's duration, silently
told the server the reader was gone and unblocked the write for a reason that had nothing to do
with `jsonWriteTimeout` — caught only because the mutation check was re-run afterward and passed
when it should not have (removing `SetWriteDeadline` no longer reddened the test). Fixed by
racing `http.Get`'s error and success cases against the same 15s ceiling and deferring
`resp.Body.Close()` in the outer test function, not the short-lived goroutine. 20/20 clean runs
after the fix; the mutation check (deleting `SetWriteDeadline`) reliably hangs to its own 15s
timeout again.

#### M-14 · `--fit=off` — the spelling the server itself prints and the task doc promises — is rejected by the flag parser; `goinfer-chat` has no `--fit` at all
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/main.go:490` (`flag.BoolVar`, pre-fix — now `flag.Var`), `:1066,1071` ("pass --fit=off to
  keep today's refusal instead"), `docs/task-fit-to-hardware.md:18,171` ("the real `--fit=off`
  switch"; "`serve` and `goinfer-chat` fit by default. `--fit=off` restores today's behaviour"),
  `internal/chatapp/main.go:246` (pre-fix: no `DisableFit`, no flag).
- **Failure:** `goinfer-serve --model x.gguf --fit=off` exits 2 with `invalid boolean value "off"
  for -fit`. The user typed what the binary told them to.
- **Fix:** a `flag.Value` accepting `on|off|true|false` (the lenient parser
  `GOINFER_METAL_FAST_PREFILL` already uses, `metal/backend.go:348`), or change every mention to
  `--fit=false`; add the flag to chatapp.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11.** Took the `flag.Value` option on both binaries — a `fitFlag
  bool` type (`internal/serveapp/main.go`, `internal/chatapp/main.go`) accepting
  on/off/true/false/1/0/t/f case-insensitively, with `IsBoolFlag() bool` so a bare `--fit` (no
  `=value`) still means true, matching `flag.Bool`'s own UX. `serve`'s registration switched from
  `flag.BoolVar` to `flag.Var((*fitFlag)(&cfg.fit), "fit", …)`; chatapp got the flag for the first
  time, wired to `decoder.Options.DisableFit`.

  Gated by `TestFitFlag_acceptsOnOff`/`_rejectsGarbage`/`_isBoolFlag`/`_string` on both packages
  (unit-level, driving `Set` directly), plus `TestFitFlag_realBinaryAcceptsOff` on both — builds
  the real `cmd/serve`/`demo/chat` binary and runs `--fit=off --version` / `--fit=on --version`
  end to end through the actual registered `flag.CommandLine`, since a unit test against the type
  alone would not have caught a registration bug (wrong flag name, or `flag.BoolVar` reintroduced
  by accident). Mutation-checked twice: dropping `"on"` from the `Set` switch failed
  `_acceptsOnOff` with the exact rejection message; reverting chatapp's registration to a plain
  `flag.BoolVar` reproduced the ORIGINAL bug's own flag-usage-dump-and-exit-2 shape in
  `_realBinaryAcceptsOff`, restored byte-for-byte via `diff` afterward. `gofmt`/`go vet` clean on
  both packages.

#### M-16 · `/v1/responses` silently drops `tools` on a template with no tool form, where the other two surfaces return 400
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/responses.go:138` (post-fix; was `:134`) vs `internal/serveapp/tools.go:24-27`, `internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:483-483`;
  `docs/server.md:111-112` lists `tools` as honoured.
- **Fix:** mirror `internal/serveapp/tools.go:24-27` (400 "this model has no tool-calling template").
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11.** `serveResponsesWith` now computes `toolsActive` the same way
  `anthropic.go` already did (`len(req.Tools) > 0 && toolChoiceMode(req.ToolChoice) != "none"`)
  and 400s with the identical message before falling through to the tools-less prompt path, instead
  of only entering `respondTools` when the template happens to support tools and silently dropping
  them otherwise.

  Gated by `TestServeResponsesWith_toolsOnUnsupportedTemplateReturns400`
  (`internal/serveapp/responses_tools_test.go`), which calls `serveResponsesWith` directly with a
  `chat.Gemma3()` template (a real template, `SupportsTools()==false`) and no real decoder model —
  the same lightweight `&loadedModel{tmpl: ...}` construction `TestConstrainForcedTool_M05` already
  uses, since the guard fires before anything needs a real tokenizer. Mutation-checked: reverting
  to the pre-fix condition made the request fall through to `chatPrompt`/`promptFor`, which then
  PANICKED on the nil `lm.tk` — a louder failure than the original silent-200 bug, and stronger
  proof the guard is load-bearing than a passing assertion alone. `gofmt`/`go vet` clean; full
  `internal/serveapp` suite green.

#### M-19 · `fit` reads free RAM AFTER loading the model it is planning for, so the CPU verdict is priced against a budget the model has already consumed
- **Where (original locations; see the disposition below for where the fix landed):**
  `internal/fitcmd/fit.go:83` (`decoder.Load`), `:169-177` (`freeBytesFor(backend)` →
  `HostRAMAvailableBytes()` = `MemAvailable` NOW), `decoder/fitplan.go:227-231,280-283`.
- **Failure:** 16 GB box, 13 GB available, a 6 GB-at-int4 checkpoint: `free` is ~7 GB when
  `Plan("cpu")` runs, so the row shrinks ctx toward `ctxPlanFloor` or reads `WEIGHT-PAGED …
  exceeds 7.0 GB free` for a model that fits with room; MoE slot counts come from the same
  depleted `remaining`. CLAUDE.md's "guard that inverts under the condition it exists for".
- **Fix:** read the CPU probe once BEFORE `decoder.Load` (or add the model's own resident bytes
  back). GPU probes are device memory and unaffected.

**DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-12.** `internal/fitcmd/fit.go`'s `Run` now captures
`hostFreeBeforeLoad := decoder.HostRAMAvailableBytes()` before the `decoder.Load` call; the
"cpu" branch of `freeBytesFor` (now `freeBytesFor(backend string, hostFreeBeforeLoad int64)`)
returns that captured value instead of re-querying `HostRAMAvailableBytes()` after the model is
already resident. GPU branches are untouched — they still call `decoder.FreeBytesFor(backend)`
live, since device memory is unaffected by a CPU-resident load.
`TestFreeBytesFor_cpuUsesThePassedInValue` (`internal/fitcmd/fit_test.go`) pins that the "cpu"
branch reports exactly the caller-supplied value rather than re-deriving it; the existing
`TestRun_realFixtureReportsCPU` end-to-end smoke test still passes unchanged.

### Loaders, formats, admission

#### M-32 · The recommended-checkpoint registry's `sha256` is documented as "verified on fetch" and never used — only `demo:` refs carry a pin
- **Where:** `pull/registry.go:44,124` (`Ref()` = `repo:file`), `internal/pullcmd/pull.go:70-75`,
  `pull/pull.go:136-150` (`ParseRef` sets no `Pin`), `:461-466,567-571`.
- **Failure:** `goinfer-chat pull qwen2.5-coder-0.5b` verifies against whatever HF's tree API
  reports *today*; an upstream re-upload passes while `goinfer-chat models` shows the old digest.
- **Fix:** build the `pull.Ref` directly with `Pin: c.SHA256, Bytes: c.Bytes`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11 (`7e247ec`).** `resolveRunRef` (`internal/pullcmd/pull.go`) no
  longer round-trips a `Recommended` checkpoint through `ParseRef(c.Ref())` — that string is just
  `"repo:file"`, and `ParseRef` only ever sets `Pin` for a `demo:` ref, so every other registry
  entry's `SHA256`/`Bytes` were silently dropped. Now builds the `pull.Ref` directly from the
  checkpoint's own fields. `TestResolveRunRef_registryShortNameRewrites` extended to assert
  `Pin`/`Bytes` match the registry, and mutation-checked (fails red reverted).

### Chat templates, tokenizer, multimodal, demos

#### M-37 · Harmony renders prior assistant turns without `<|channel|>final`, so multi-turn gpt-oss prompts differ from upstream; `chat/chat.go`'s top-of-file comment claimed `sys_multi` is byte-exact "for every family" (since narrowed — see disposition) and harmony has no golden
- **Where:** `chat/templates.go:136-146`, `chat/harmony_test.go:23-41` (single-user-turn pin
  only), `chat/chat_test.go:33-35` (no `harmony` in `ctors`); upstream `openai/gpt-oss-20b`
  `chat_template.jinja` renders `<|start|>assistant<|channel|>final<|message|>{content}<|end|>`.
- **Fix:** emit `<|channel|>final` for assistant history; a harmony golden with `sys_user`/
  `sys_multi`; narrow the unqualified byte-exact claim at the top of `chat/chat.go`.

  **DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED, same underlying item as N-37 (audit-2026-09-02.md).** The
  narrowing half is done: `chat/chat.go`'s doc comment no longer makes the unqualified "for every
  family" claim this finding quotes — it names the harmony multi-turn divergence explicitly (see
  `chat/chat.go`'s own "WHAT BYTE-EXACT COVERS" comment, which is why this finding's `:9` citation
  now points at DIFFERENT, already-corrected text rather than the old unqualified sentence). The
  channel-marker emission and golden halves are NOT fixed, deliberately: N-37's disposition found
  no harmony golden exists to derive the correct `<|channel|>` byte sequence against, so guessing
  it risks being wrong in a way that looks right — the same reasoning applied to this doc's other
  no-golden cases.

#### M-39 · `examples/embed` prepends BOS to a rendered template that already carries the family's BOS marker → double BOS on Gemma/Llama-3/Mistral GGUFs, in the program the README points cold users at
- **Where:** `examples/embed/main.go:56-63` (`buildPrompt`, post-fix; was `tok.Encode(text, true)`
  after `tmpl.Render`),
  `chat/chat.go:84-76` ("encode with addBOS=false"); the demos do it right (`internal/chatapp/main.go:468`,
  `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:438`). Only ChatML (BOS = −1) is unaffected — the one model the example's comment uses.
- **Fix:** `tok.Encode(text, tmpl == nil)`.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11.** `examples/embed/main.go`'s id-building step was extracted
  into `buildPrompt` (the `tmpl` variable itself is scoped inside the `if`, so a package-level
  `rendered` bool tracks whether `chat.Detect` matched); it now calls `tok.Encode(text, !rendered)`
  instead of always `true`. Gated by
  `TestBuildPrompt_rendersOnlyOneLeadingBOS` (`examples/embed/main_test.go`), which uses
  `tokenizer/testdata/chatml-tiny.gguf` — a fixture whose vocab, unlike a real Qwen ChatML
  checkpoint's (BOS = −1), carries an actual BOS id, so it can exercise the double-prepend
  directly rather than needing a Gemma/Llama-3/Mistral fixture this repo does not have committed.
  Confirmed by inspection that `<|im_start|>` and this fixture's BOS token happen to share id 271,
  so the pre-fix bug produces a literal `[271, 271, …]` prefix, which the test asserts against
  directly. Mutation-checked: reverting to `tok.Encode(text, true)` fails with that exact prefix
  reported; the existing full-checkpoint integration test
  (`TestEmbedExample_outputIsNotARepeatedToken`) is unaffected since it skips without a real
  Gemma/Llama-3/Mistral fixture, matching the audit's own note that its Qwen fixture cannot
  exercise this path either way.

#### M-40 · Schema nesting depth is unbounded, and every step's mask copies the frame stack once per vocab id — a forced-nesting schema makes the masker's per-step cost O(V × depth)
- **Where:** `constrain/schema.go:71-74,260,277` (no depth limit; only `encoding/json`'s 10 000
  cap), `constrain/schema_grammar.go:112-123,131-139` (`snapshot` copies the whole stack per
  `TryBytes`), `:331-335` (`dispatch` accepts only `[` for a kArray frame with `minItems:1`).
- **Failure:** a 4 MiB `response_format` body holds ~160k levels of `{"type":"array","minItems":1,
  "items":`; the grammar FORCES the model down the nesting; at depth 5 000 one `Process` on a Qwen
  vocab is 151 936 × 5 000 × 64 B ≈ 49 GB of memcpy per step, for `max_tokens` steps, on the model's
  serialized slot. P-20 measured "tracks stack DEPTH" at ordinary depths; nothing bounds it.
- **Fix:** cap schema depth in `compile` (error past ~64) and in `ToolCallGrammar`.

**DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-12.** `constrain/schema.go`'s `compile` now takes a `depth int`
parameter, checked against a new `maxSchemaDepth = 64` constant at entry; `compileObject`/
`compileArray` thread `depth+1` into every recursive `compile` call for a property or an
`items` schema, so any path through the recursion counts, not just object-under-object nesting.
`ToolCallGrammar` (`constrain/tool_grammar.go`) needed no separate cap — it calls the same
`compile` on a wrapper object built around the tool's `paramSchema`, so the one fix in `compile`
covers both entry points the finding named. `TestSchema_depthIsBounded` and
`TestToolCallGrammar_depthIsBounded` (`constrain/schema_test.go`) gate both: a schema just under
the cap still compiles, one 100x past it is rejected by name ("nesting") through both `JSONSchema`
and `ToolCallGrammar` — mutation-proven (removing the depth check compiles both attack schemas
again). Full `constrain` suite (including the fuzz corpus) green; `staticcheck` clean.

### Release engineering

#### M-44 · `govulncheck` scans the one module none of the GPU release assets is built from
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/govulncheck.yml:1-10,30-38` (root `./...` only; header claims "the
  artifact the project's pitch is about"), `release-assets.yml:62-67,175-180` (darwin from
  `metal/`, linux from `cuda/`), `cuda/go.mod:6-10`, `metal/go.mod:6-15` (purego, gocudrv,
  aikit/gpu absent from the root graph by the cleanliness guard).
- **Fix:** matrix over `.`, `cuda` (`-tags cuda`), `metal` (`GOOS=darwin`), `gpu`, `demo/agent`; or
  reword the header.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-11.** Took the Fix as written, structured as separate jobs (one
  per module) mirroring `ci.yml`'s own already-working shape — same runners, same ephemeral
  `go work init` stitch to resolve the unpublished goinfer root locally, same provisioning —
  rather than inventing a new matrix pattern for this workflow specifically:

  - `root` (unchanged, `ubuntu-latest`), plus a version pin (see the N-41 (09-02) note in the
    same commit).
  - `gpu` (`ubuntu-latest`): `apt-get install libgl1-mesa-dev xorg-dev` (same packages `ci.yml`'s
    `gpu` job installs), `go work init . ./gpu ./demo/agent`, then
    `govulncheck -tags 'gpu goinfer_testhooks' ./...` in `gpu/` AND in `demo/agent/` — folded into
    the same job because `demo/agent` needs the SAME workspace `gpu` does (see below), matching
    `ci.yml`'s own grouping of the two.
  - `cuda` (`ubuntu-latest`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`): `go work init . ./cuda`, then
    `govulncheck -tags 'cuda goinfer_testhooks' ./...` — no CUDA toolkit needed (cgo-free,
    dlopen-based), same as `ci.yml`'s own `cuda` job note.
  - `metal` (`macos-latest`): `go work init . ./metal`, then `govulncheck ./...` (no `metal` tag —
    the module registers on plain darwin, same as `ci.yml`'s `metal-darwin` build step).

  **Verified locally before committing** (this Mac, using the already-`go.work`-covered
  checkout as a stand-in for each job's ephemeral workspace): `govulncheck -tags 'gpu
  goinfer_testhooks' ./...` in `gpu/` — clean; `GOOS=linux CGO_ENABLED=0 govulncheck -tags 'cuda
  goinfer_testhooks' ./...` in `cuda/` — clean (confirms `cuda`'s own code is Linux-gated
  correctly: it fails to even TYPE-CHECK on darwin without `GOOS=linux`, which is expected, not a
  bug — `aikit/gpu`'s `cuda.go` itself is `//go:build linux`); `govulncheck ./...` in `metal/`
  (native darwin) — clean; `govulncheck ./...` in `demo/agent/` — clean. Also verified the
  workspace requirement itself is real, not assumed: `GOWORK=off go build ./...` in `demo/agent/`
  fails against its own pinned `goinfer v0.17.2` with undefined symbols — the same "between
  releases" gap `ci.yml`'s `gpu` job comment documents for `gpu` itself — confirming `demo/agent`
  needs the same workspace stitch `gpu` does, not a standalone scan.

  Could not verify `go work init`'s exact CI-runner behavior or the `apt-get` provisioning step
  end-to-end (both copied verbatim from `ci.yml`'s already-relied-upon `gpu`/`cuda`/`metal-darwin`
  jobs rather than invented) — first real run of this workflow is the remaining check.

## Second sweep — closures found 2026-09-13 through 2026-09-16, archived 2026-09-16

> **A second wave, same rule as the first.** The 2026-09-12 split above moved everything that
> carried a closure marker at that time. Since then, C-08, G-08, EVERY remaining Major finding
> (including M-01, whose fix landed `e11df12d`, 2026-09-15, about 90 minutes after a same-day
> backfill note in the live doc's own intro said it was still open — that note is now itself
> stale and should be read as a snapshot of 08:31 that morning, not current), all Minor findings
> but seven, and all Performance observations but two and a half (P-15 split — see below) have
> closed. Most were fixed same-day or within days of the audit and never annotated back into the
> live doc until a 2026-09-13/09-15 backfill pass found them via `git log` cross-referencing
> rather than the doc's own prose (see the live doc's own NOTEs on this — kept there, not
> repeated here). The rest closed through this session's own batch-by-batch N-tier pass
> (Batches A–J, 2026-09-16) and a targeted M/P-tier re-check that found the same stale-marker gap.
>
> **What's still open in the live doc after this sweep:** P-15's MoE-TTFT measurement (blocked
> on real hardware this Mac doesn't have) and seven Minor findings whose disposition is DEFERRED
> to individual review (N-26's `fit:` banner half, N-29, N-33, N-40's remainder, N-43's leak
> half, N-79, N-86) — each for a documented reason: a design decision this batch shouldn't make
> unilaterally, a fix needing real hardware or a foreign package's contract this session couldn't
> verify, or genuine new-scope feature work. **Every Critical, Gate, and Major finding this audit
> raised is now closed.**

### §1 — Critical (second sweep)

### C-08 · Metal batched prefill on a PAGED generic-MoE model binds the zero-value stacked-expert buffers

- **Where:** `metal/model.go:762-676` (`prefillOK` = features + geometry + `!HasGemma4MoEResident`,
  nothing about paging), `metal/moe.go:287-289,325` (`expGuW/expGuS/expDW/expDS` stay zero-value
  when `paged`), `metal/prefill.go:684-636` (row loop → `encodeMoEExperts`), `metal/moe.go:711-671`
  (binds `ml.expGuW`), `metal/moe.go:762-721` (the assumption, in a comment).
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // metal/model.go:762
  r.prefillOK = len(m.MissingResidentFeatures(prefillFeatures)) == 0 && m.PerLayerGeomOK("webgpu") && !m.HasGemma4MoEResident()
  // metal/moe.go:762 — "Paging is never used by prefill (its own tiny/simple scope, G8)"
  ```
- **Failure:** `--backend metal --moe-cache-slots N` (or `GOINFER_METAL_MOE_SLOTS`, or the
  CUDA-named `GOINFER_MOE_CACHE_SLOTS` that `MoECacheSlotsRequest` also reads) on a prefill-admitted
  generic MoE (Mixtral, Qwen2/3-MoE, GLM-4.5), prompt ≥ 512: every MoE layer's expert GEMVs read
  through an unbound buffer argument. Either a GPU address fault → C-09 latch → `PrefillLast` error
  → silent fallback to sequential (every long prompt pays a fault first; the C-09 test notes faults
  are often "silently tolerated" on this hardware), or garbage folded into every prompt row's
  residual with no error.
- **Fix:** `r.prefillOK = ... && !(r.moe != nil && r.moe.paged)` — one line at the chokepoint.
- **Confidence:** confirmed that the nil-buffer dispatch is reached; plausible which of the two
  outcomes a given Mac produces.
- **Prior:** new (306b16b extended prefill to MoE; 918b47f made slots a flag two commits later).
- **CLOSED 2026-09-10, same day as this audit, never annotated back into this doc.** `d9139bc7`
  shipped exactly the fix above: `r.prefillOK = ... && !(r.moe != nil && r.moe.paged)`, confirmed
  still present verbatim in `metal/model.go:762-726` as of this note. The fix commit never touched
  this file, so C-08 sat presenting as fully open for three days — through the 2026-09-12 split of
  this doc's closed findings to `docs/completed/audit-2026-09-10.md`, which had nothing to grab
  since no closure note existed to find. The residual gap this fix's scope left behind (the same
  zero-value-buffer defect reachable from `HiddenLast`/`Forward(id,pos)`/`ForwardArgmax` on a paged
  MoE, not just `PrefillLast`) was independently re-found and filed as `docs/audit-metal-2026-09-12.md`'s
  C-02, still open there.


### §2 — Gates that cannot fail (second sweep)

### G-08 · The §3.2 pooled Metal prefill gate silently narrows its decision set to whichever cells have reference files, and a fit-guard decline on a decision model is a SKIP that still yields "SHIPS"

- **Where:** `metal/prefill_gate_ref_test.go:193-209` (a nil cell is dropped; `:208-213` prints
  `decisionKs`, not the cells found), `:255-265` (missing reference → `return nil`), `:114-117`
  (`t.Skipf` when the resident did not build), `:139-141` (only the primary set's `shipped` is
  asserted); `docs/measurements/prefill-gate-l1-ref-b-2026-09-09.md:39-46`.
- **Failure:** with `S-K256-p*.bin` absent the verdict is over {512,1024} under a header reading
  `K=[256 512 1024]`; §3.2 pre-registered "both models pass", and the default flip that now governs
  7B-class Metal users was taken with D7 `Skipf`'d — decided on the 1.5B alone. Both disclosed in
  prose; neither fails the test.
- **Fix:** `len(decisionCells) != len(decisionKs)` ⇒ `Fatalf`; a decision model that fails to
  build is a FAIL when `deciding`.
- **Confidence:** confirmed. **Prior:** sibling of G-01 (09-02) in a new gate.
- **PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-09-13, first half fixed.** This finding was re-filed and fixed as
  `docs/audit-metal-2026-09-12.md`'s G-02 (`7d25c68e`) — same file (`metal/prefill_gate_ref_test.go`),
  same fix: `deciding=true` now `Fatalf`s when `len(decisionCells) != len(decisionKs)`, naming which
  K is missing. The second half — "D7 that fails to build → `Skipf`" — was not reproduced: every D7
  run that session hit a hard `Fatalf` at the fit-guard's own refusal instead, a different (louder)
  decline shape than the `Skipf` this finding names; left as-is rather than fixed against a symptom
  not reproduced. See G-02's own entry there for the full verification.


### §3 — Major (second sweep)

#### Resident path and serving

#### M-01 · R-10 reopened on two new resident routes: an adapter prompt, or a reused-image VL prompt, in `(ResidentContextCap, MaxPositions)` dies mid-prefill with a 500 that leaks the "use the staged path" hint *(two reviewers)*
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/openai.go:677,737`, `internal/serveapp/tools.go:48`, `internal/serveapp/responses.go:156,225`,
  `internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:534`, `internal/serveapp/batches_run.go:84,190`, `internal/serveapp/jobs_http.go:57`
  (now `lm.prepare(…, lm.residentPath())` at all 9 — was `lm.adapter == ""`), `internal/serveapp/openai.go:912` (the
  cap is applied only when `residentPath`; the comment now explains `residentPath()` itself rather than
  saying "GenerateVL is CPU-only" and "adapter models … staged/CPU session path"),
  `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:275,358` (`prepare(…, false)`, unchanged — VL's own resident check is
  the P9a citation below, not this one),
  `decoder/model.go:1312,1290-1323` (now declines `useGPU` before ever claiming the resident, past the cap),
  `decoder/generate_vl.go:89-97,103` (P9a full-image reuse now declines past the cap too, in both
  `GenerateVL` and `GenerateQwenVL`),
  `cuda/resident.go:1584-1489` (the error text the client no longer reaches for these two routes).
- **Failure:** (a) `--adapter ft=…` on CUDA/Metal, cap 4096, window 32k, a 6000-token prompt to
  `ft`: `prepare` checks against 32k, `generateInto` admits resident (C-01's path), `PrefillLast`
  errors past the cap, the sequential `Forward` at pos 4096 errors → 500 with the internal hint.
  (b) Gemma 3 VL on CUDA: turn 1 image prompt of 3900 tokens commits; turn 2 resends it plus 300
  tokens of history: `prepare` passes, P9a fires, the suffix runs past the cap → the same 500. The
  `-ctx` help (`internal/serveapp/main.go:530`) promises "a clean 400 context_length_exceeded".
- **Fix:** derive `residentPath` from what will run (`ResidentActive()` for adapters too, since G3);
  at the decoder seam decline to CPU when `len(prompt) > ContextCap()` in both `generateInto`'s
  `useGPU` and the P9a branch (which today `return`s on a prefill error instead of falling through).
- **Prior:** regression of R-10 / F-01 (fix keyed on a routing rule G3 removed).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). Two independent layers, matching the two-clause Fix line:

1. **prepare-time (serveapp):** all 9 `lm.prepare(...)` call sites (`openai.go` x2, `tools.go`,
   `responses.go` x2, `anthropic.go`, `batches_run.go` x2, `jobs_http.go` — the doc's own citation
   undercounted at 8) now pass a new `lm.residentPath()` helper instead of `lm.adapter == ""`:
   `lm.model != nil && lm.model.ResidentActive()`. Verified equivalent on every OTHER combination
   before changing it (non-adapter resident: both `true`; non-adapter non-resident and adapter
   non-resident: `ResidentContextCap()` returns 0 either way, so `contextWindow`'s own `rc > 0`
   guard no-ops regardless of what `residentPath` says) — this only tightens the one broken case.
   The 2 `vision_serve.go` sites (`prepare(…, false)`) are untouched: VL's own resident path is the
   P9a check below, not this one. The `internal/serveapp/openai.go:912-680,769-773` /
   `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:275,350` citations are stale relative to `residentPath()`
   replacing the inline predicate at all 9 sites; not re-pointed line by line since the fix already
   replaces the exact thing they were citing.
2. **decoder seam (defense in depth):** `decoder/model.go`'s `generateInto` declines `useGPU`
   before ever calling `tryClaimResident()` when `ResidentContextCap() > 0 && len(prompt) >
   ContextCap()`; `decoder/generate_vl.go`'s two P9a sites (`GenerateVL`, `GenerateQwenVL`) gate
   their existing "FULL reuse" body behind the same check, falling through to the SAME "not fully
   reused" release-and-retry path that already existed for a partial match — no new fallthrough
   target needed, since one was already there.

Verified real-checkpoint, TDD (confirmed red — reproducing the exact `checkCap`-style error/silent
non-rejection the finding describes — then green) for all three layers: `internal/serveapp/residentpath_test.go`
(a new fake resident backend, built from `decoder`'s exported API only, since `decoder`'s own
`fakeResident` in `resident_seam_test.go` is unexported) covers `residentPath()` itself and
`prepare`'s end-to-end enforcement for an adapter; `decoder/model_lazy_kv_test.go` covers
`generateInto`'s decline (asserts zero resident `Forward` calls and exactly one CPU prefill,
not just "no error"); `decoder/generate_vl_resident_test.go` extends the existing P9a reuse tests
for both `GenerateVL` and `GenerateQwenVL` with a cap set to exactly one below the reused prompt's
length, asserting the tower runs a second time (proof the fast path declined and fell through)
with no error.

#### M-02 · All seven VL resident clamp sites cut `maxTokens` to the cap without publishing `Budget`/`BudgetClamped`, so a cap-truncated image turn reports `finish_reason:"stop"` / `end_turn` *(three reviewers)*
- **Where:** `decoder/generate_vl.go:110-107,146-150,201-205,270-274,314-318,370-374`,
  `decoder/generate_gemma4_vl.go:172-176`; contrast `decoder/model.go:1427-1308`; consumer
  `internal/serveapp/openai.go:1540-1207` (`effectiveBudget` trusts `Budget` only when
  `BudgetClamped`); doc `decoder/model.go:1652-1470` ("0 on generation paths that don't clamp  (speculative/VL)" — VL clamps now).
- **Failure:** Gemma 3 / Qwen2.5-VL / Gemma 4 on CUDA, cap 4096, image prompt 3900, `max_tokens
  512`: the loop stops at 196 tokens (possibly 0), `len(ids) < requested` → `"stop"`. Claude Code
  and dsh read a truncated answer as complete — M-04 (09-02) on the three VL paths a630a2c/
  a825c9d/c49d384 grew after it.
- **Fix:** `g.Budget = maxTokens; g.BudgetClamped = true` at each site (one helper shared with
  `generateInto`); pass `residentPath` for vision requests that will run resident.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). All 7 sites now set `g.BudgetClamped = true` inside the
existing `ctxCap > 0 && gpuPos+maxTokens > ctxCap` clamp branch, and `g.Budget = maxTokens`
unconditionally right before that branch's own `vlDecodeLoop` call — matching
`decoder/model.go:1434-1398`'s reference pattern exactly, inline rather than via a shared helper
(the audit's "one helper shared with generateInto" suggestion wasn't taken: each of the 3
branches in `GenerateVL`/`GenerateQwenVL` has its own `maxTokens`/`g` already in scope at a
different point in the function, so inlining is the same two lines with no real duplication to
factor out).

Verified real-checkpoint, TDD: `TestGenerateVL_residentContextCapPublishesBudgetClamped` /
`TestGenerateQwenVL_residentContextCapPublishesBudgetClamped` (`decoder/generate_vl_resident_test.go`)
use the committed `gemma3-vl-tiny`/`qwen25vl-tiny` fixtures with a `fakeResident` capped to exactly
2 decode tokens' headroom, requesting more — both confirmed red (BudgetClamped false, Budget 0)
against the pre-fix code, green after. `TestGenerateGemma4VL_residentContextCapPublishesBudgetClamped`
(`decoder/gemma4_vl_resident_test.go`) mirrors them for the 7th site but — like M-07's own
extended test — skips on this machine for lack of the `gemma4-vl-bidir-tiny` checkpoint fixture;
not independently red/green verified here, only by direct comparison to the two sites that were.

#### M-03 · The n-gram and draft-model speculative loops are refused at the resident context cap instead of finishing with `"length"` — M-13 (09-02)'s second defect, in the two sibling loops
- **Where:** `decoder/spec_ngram.go:293-302,404-424`, `decoder/speculative.go:207-214,287-295`,
  `cuda/resident.go:1670-1576` (`checkCap` refuses the whole round), `gpu/residency.go:1192-1126`;
  the clamp the block-spec loop got: `decoder/blockspec.go:500-478`.
- **Failure:** `prepare` clamps `max_tokens` to `rc − len(prompt)`, so a resident turn may run to
  the cap; with `Emitted = M − j` and a draft of `kEff > j` tokens (the n-gram drafter proposes up to
  8 whenever the tail repeats) `ForwardN` is refused, `g.err` is set, and the client gets a
  generation error after a nearly complete response. Reachable from `serve --spec ngram` on CUDA
  (Theta 0.251) and WebGPU (0.5); Metal exempt only because Theta ≥ 1 declines.
- **Fix:** clamp `draftTok` to `cap − base − 1` via `target.ResidentContextCap()` before building
  `seq` (one line in each loop).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). Both loops now call a new shared helper,
`specRoundDraftWidth(k, pos, ctxCap)` (`decoder/spec_ngram.go:219`, mirroring
`blockSpecRoundWidth`'s M-13 shape exactly) — `decoder/spec_ngram.go:418` and
`decoder/speculative.go:280`. It returns the clamped draft width, or `-1` when there is no room
even for the pending `cur` token alone, which both call sites treat as a clean stop rather than a
verify attempt.

`speculative.go` needed one more change beyond the mechanical clamp: `GenerateSpeculative`'s draft
model is a full second `*Model` with its own KV cache that must stay positionally in sync with the
target's, via a "feed the confirmed draft token once more" step on every full-accept round
(`draftForward(draftTok[K-1])`). At the clamped boundary (`kRound == 0`, i.e. cap allows only
`cur`, no draft token at all) that indexes `draftTok[-1]` — genNgramInto has no equivalent risk
since its "drafter" is a stateless lookup, not a second model with its own cache. Split into two
cases (`decoder/speculative.go`, the `switch` right after `targetTruncate(keep)`): `kRound > 0`
keeps the original sync; `kRound == 0` feeds `cur` itself instead, since the draft loop never ran
at all that round and `cur` was never fed to the draft's cache.

Verified two ways. `TestSpecRoundDraftWidth_respectsResidentContextCap`
(`decoder/spec_ngram_test.go`) is a pure-function TDD gate on the shared clamp math (mirroring
`TestBlockSpec_roundWidthRespectsBothBudgets`'s style), covering the plenty-of-room, partial-clamp,
exactly-one-slot-left (width 0, not −1), and no-room (−1) cases; confirmed red against a
passthrough stub, green with the fix.
`TestGenerateSpeculative_residentContextCapFinishesCleanly` (`decoder/speculative_test.go`) is a
live integration test — `fakeResident` with a tight `capPos` forces the exact `kRound==0` boundary
deterministically on round 1 (not left to chance: `capPos = len(prompt)+1`), and since
`fakeResident`'s logits are position-only (`argmax(pos) = pos % vocab`, independent of the fed
token — `decoder/resident_seam_test.go`), the expected output is hand-computable rather than
needing a second live reference call; confirmed red (reproduced the exact `checkCap` refusal the
finding describes: `"fakeResident: pos 3 >= context cap 3"`) against the pre-fix code, green with
it. This test is also what proves the `kRound==0` draft-cache-sync branch above doesn't panic or
desync — the one case the pure clamp-math test can't reach on its own.

#### M-05 · Compute-time LoRA never checks a delta's `In`/`Out` against the base projection: a mis-shaped adapter loads clean and panics in the Generate goroutine (CPU) or reads/writes out of bounds on the device (CUDA/Metal) *(three reviewers)*
- **Where:** `decoder/lora.go:137-153` (`validateTargets` — names only), `:232-252`, `:342-357`
  (`applyLoRA`: `d.in` vs `len(x)`, `d.out` vs `len(y)` never compared); contrast `:165-167` (the
  merge path checks `d.out != out || d.in != in`); `cuda/lora.go:154-158,179-191`, `cuda/lora.cu:
  25,49-52` (`Ar = amat + r*K` strides by the caller's K; `dst[row]` for `row < proj.Out`);
  `metal/lora.go:148-100` (rank-only check), `metal/kernels.go:882,871`; `gpu/lora_resident.go:
  153-189`.
- **Failure:** an adapter trained on a same-family different-size base (Qwen2.5-1.5B adapter
  against 7B — identical tensor names, hidden 1536 vs 3584) passes `validateTargets` and
  `LoadAdapter`. CPU: index panic in the generation goroutine (net/http's recover does not reach it)
  → process exit. CUDA/Metal: `lora_delta_down` reads past the activation buffer, `lora_delta_up`
  writes past the q section into K/V or adjacent buffers (UMA) — plausible garbage, no error.
- **Fix:** in `LoadAdapter`/`buildLoraRuntime` compare each delta's `in/out` with the base
  projection's `Cols()/Rows()` and refuse — the merge path's check, one chokepoint for all backends.

#### M-07 · `GenerateGemma4VL` commits `resIDs` with no image-block record, so a later text prompt's prefix scan can walk through the image positions *(two reviewers)*
- **Where:** `decoder/generate_gemma4_vl.go:200` (now the fixed commit call, `imgHash` now
  reaching `residentImageBlock` — was `residentCommitIDs(ids, generated, nil, nil)` at old
  `:191-197`, with `imgHash` discarded per old `:124-127`), invariant at `decoder/model.go:
  40-43`, consumer `decoder/resident_reuse.go:138-154`.
- **Failure:** the placeholder run is content-independent (same soft-token id, count fixed by the
  patch grid), so two images at the same size produce identical ids; with no block at `imgPos` the
  text scan reuses image-derived K/V for any prompt sharing the ids. Reaching it through serve needs
  a text prompt carrying the soft-token ids (not produced by `messagesToTurns`), hence Major.
- **Fix:** commit `&residentImageBlock{start: imgPos, end: imgPos+imgLen, hash: imgHash}` as the
  two siblings do, or `residentForgetIDs()` instead of committing.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). `decoder/generate_gemma4_vl.go`'s `GenerateGemma4VL` now
commits `&residentImageBlock{start: imgPos, end: imgPos + imgLen, hash: imgHash}` on the same
success path the two siblings (`GenerateVL`, `GenerateQwenVL`) already use; the stale "imgHash is
accepted but unused" doc comments are rewritten to explain the block-commit role instead.

Verification is split, honestly, across two tests — TDD applies cleanly to only one of them:

1. `TestResidentCommitIDs_gemma4ImageBlockPreventsCrossImageReuse`
   (`decoder/gemma4_vl_resident_test.go`), new, checkpoint-free: constructs the exact failure shape
   directly (`residentCommitIDs`/`residentReuseLen` called by hand with Gemma4's content-independent
   soft-token ids) and confirms a differently-hashed image at the same span is NOT treated as
   reusable once a block is recorded. This is NOT a red/green gate on the actual bug — per this
   repo's own calling-convention rule, a test that supplies its own call to `residentCommitIDs`
   proves the mechanism works *when called correctly*, not that `GenerateGemma4VL` calls it that
   way. Confirmed directly: reverting only the `GenerateGemma4VL` commit call back to
   `residentCommitIDs(ids, generated, nil, nil)` left this test green — it doesn't exercise
   `GenerateGemma4VL` at all.
2. `TestGenerateGemma4VL_residentDecodeEngagesOnBidirectional`, extended with `resImgBlocks`
   assertions after a real generation — this IS the real gate on `GenerateGemma4VL`'s own commit
   call, but it (and its two siblings in this file) requires `testdata/gemma4-vl-bidir-tiny`, a
   checkpoint fixture this Mac does not have (`scripts/pin_gemma4_vl_bidir_tiny.py` needs
   `~/.venv-vl`, also not present here) — confirmed neither gitignored nor produced by CI, so it
   skips wherever this repo's tests currently run, not just locally. It could not be TDD-verified
   red/green this session; it stands ready for nobara or whichever environment gets the fixture.

Given (2)'s gap, the actual basis for confidence is direct code comparison: the fix is a one-line
change to the exact call shape already shipped and covered (by real-checkpoint tests that DO run)
in `GenerateVL`/`GenerateQwenVL` — same primitives, same `residentImageBlock` literal, same
success-path placement relative to `committed = true`.

#### M-09 · Staged WebGPU int4 now runs decode (M=1) on the device and prefill/verify (M>1) on the host W4A8 kernel — decode ≠ prefill ≠ verify on that path, and the code's own bit-identity comment says the opposite *(two reviewers)*
- **Where:** `gpu/backend.go:156-161` (`M != 1` declines), `decoder/weightmat.go:839` (now reworded
  — was "its per-output result is M-independent so batched prefill is bit-identical to sequential
  decode"), `gpu/backend_w4a8_test.go:82-73` (cosine ≥ 0.999, not equality); fix at
  `decoder/spec_verify_guard.go:50-54`.
- **Failure:** the prompt's K/V come from the CPU integer kernel, each decode token's from a WGSL
  f32 GEMV with f16 group scales; speculative verify (K>1) is CPU while decode is GPU, so "verify ==
  sequential greedy" (`decoder/forwardn.go:1131-1136`) no longer holds — the M-dependent-kernel class that
  produced the 84% CUDA divergence CLAUDE.md records. `DecodePath` prints the opposite (G-13a).
- **Fix:** pin the gap (M=1 vs M=2 through the webgpu backend at a stated tolerance) and reword;
  or refuse the staged-webgpu-int4 combination in `specRollbackSafe`/the n-gram entries until an
  M>1 dispatch on the same kernel family exists.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). Took the refusal branch of the doc's own two-option Fix
line, not the tolerance-pinning one: characterizing a real numeric tolerance between two
structurally different kernels (a WGSL f32 GEMV with f16 group scales vs. a CPU integer W4A8
kernel) would need new cross-backend measurement infrastructure this finding doesn't otherwise
need, while refusing the combination is mechanical and matches M-11's identical-shaped bug
(`useGemmMMA`/`useAttnFused` not gated by call purpose) better than a one-off tolerance would.

Extended `SpecDecodeConflict` (`decoder/spec_verify_guard.go:50`) rather than `specRollbackSafe`
as the doc's own citation suggested: `specRollbackSafe` is about rollback (`TruncateTo`)
correctness specifically, a different concern than kernel-exactness, and `SpecDecodeConflict`
already existed as exactly "does verify's numerics match decode's" — the resident half of that
same question, for CUDA's flash-decode divergence (M-11's own mechanism). The staged check
(`:54`, `m.be != nil && isWebGPUBackend(...) && (Quant() == "int4" || "int4mix")`) runs
independently of, and before, the resident check — the M-09 risk exists BECAUSE `m.resident` is
nil, not despite it. This automatically covers all 3 of `SpecDecodeConflict`'s existing call sites
(`spec_ngram.go`, `speculative.go`, `blockspec.go`) with one change; `NewBlockSpec` still declines
staged-int4 for an unrelated reason (`ResidentDrafterHost` requires a resident, and a staged model
has none) — not this fix's coverage, noted rather than double-claimed. The misleading
`decoder/weightmat.go:839` comment ("bit-identical to sequential decode") is reworded to state
precisely what it's actually claiming (the CPU int4 kernel is M-independent ALONE) and points at
the new guard for the device-backed path it doesn't cover.

Verified, TDD: `TestSpecDecodeConflict_refusesStagedWebGPUInt4`
(`decoder/spec_verify_guard_test.go`) drives the entry points directly (not the predicate alone,
matching this file's own existing resident-divergence test's discipline) with a stub `Backend`
reporting `Name() == "webgpu"` — no real GPU or checkpoint needed, since the check depends only on
`m.be`/`m.quant`. Two controls: int8int8 on webgpu (not refused — M-09 is int4-specific), int4 on
a plain cpu-named backend (not refused — the CPU kernel alone is M-independent). Confirmed red
(reverting the fix let all three entry points proceed) then green.

#### M-10 · `cudaResident.HiddenLast` (G4) is served first for `/v1/embeddings` on CUDA, violates its own interface's bit-identity contract above 512 tokens, returns an int8-requantised vector, and has no gate
- **Where:** `cuda/prefill.go:261-249` (→ `prefillChunked`), fast levers now gated by
  `forceExactKernels` (`cuda/resident.go:657`, set `cuda/prefill.go:825`) instead of only
  `passPromptLen`, `:1206-1224` (the "hidden state" is `r.aq` dequantised — 127 levels per row,
  confirmed consistent with decode's own `launchToken`, not a batched-only precision loss);
  `decoder/residency.go:137-152` (the contract: "a backend whose batched forward is NOT
  bit-identical to its own sequential one … must not implement this interface"); `decoder/embed.go:74-84`;
  `docs/tasks/task-gpu-paths-2026-09.md:283-300` ("ZERO compile/run verification"). Fixed at
  `cuda/hiddenlast_resident_parity_test.go` (the CUDA twin of Metal's own).
- **Fix:** a `forceExact` bit for `tailHiddenLast`; download the f32 residual and normalise on the
  host; add the CUDA twin of the Metal parity test before the next tag. (Serve-side, G4 is not
  reached at all: M-17.)

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), with the doc's own second sub-fix NOT applied — traced and
found wrong, not just skipped. Two of the three sub-fixes landed:

1. **The `forceExact` bit**: `forceExactKernels` (`cuda/resident.go:657`), set once per pass in
   `prefillCore` (`cuda/prefill.go:825`, `tail != tailLastLogits` — covers `tailHiddenLast` and,
   sharing the same mechanism, M-11's `tailAllLogits`/`tailAllArgmax` too) and consulted by both
   fast levers (`useAttnFused` `:1471`, `useGemmMMA` `:1512`). See M-11's own closure note below
   for the full mechanism and its cross-M-09 relationship — one fix, three findings.
2. **The CUDA twin of the Metal parity test**: `cuda/hiddenlast_resident_parity_test.go`, mirroring
   `metal/hiddenlast_resident_parity_test.go`'s exact shape (resident vs CPU, both int8int8,
   `EmbedResidentForTest`/`ResidentForwardForTest`/`HiddenLast` directly, a post-`Reset` second
   sequence). Bar re-measured for this backend rather than assumed from Metal's 0.998, per that
   file's own "measured directly, not assumed" discipline: CUDA's kernels land at cosine 0.99997
   (24 tokens) / 0.99999 (8 tokens, post-Reset) — tighter than Metal's ~0.999, so the same 0.998
   bar was kept (real headroom, not a coincidence of a loosened number).
3. **NOT applied: "download the f32 residual and normalise on the host."** Traced `launchToken`
   (`cuda/resident.go`, the M=1 DECODE path) and found it calls the identical
   `r.norm(r.x, r.finalNorm, r.aq, r.aSc)` before ITS OWN LM-head projection — decode has no f32
   post-final-norm hidden state anywhere either; `r.aq`/`r.aSc` (int8-quantized activations) is
   how this resident represents a post-norm hidden state everywhere, not something specific to the
   batched `tailHiddenLast` path. Metal's own accepted comparison confirms this is a
   policy-level choice, not a bug: `hiddenlast_resident_parity_test.go` compares against a CPU
   reference ALSO loaded at `int8int8`, at a 0.998 (not 1.0) cosine bar, with its own comment
   explaining the measured ~0.999 gap as ordinary CPU-vs-GPU int8 kernel noise. Implementing the
   doc's literal suggestion would have made CUDA's `HiddenLast` MORE precise than CUDA's own decode
   path ever is — a new inconsistency, not a fix for one. The int8-precision observation in this
   finding's own title is real but not a bit-identity violation once the kernel-choice gap (item 1
   above) is closed.

#### M-11 · Spec-decode verify on CUDA is "exact" only because M < 16 happens to hold — nothing gates `ForwardN`/`PrefillLastNArgmax` against the fast levers
- **Where:** `cuda/prefill.go:1526` (`useGemmMMA`: `M >= gemmMMAMinRows` (16) and above the 512
  floor, now also gated by `forceExactKernels`), `:337` (claims per-row logits equal sequential),
  `cuda/resident.go:657` (the fix), `internal/chatapp/main.go:224` (`-spec-k` unbounded),
  `decoder/blockspec.go:205-208`.
- **Failure:** `-spec-k 15` or a DFlash/DSpark drafter with `block_size ≥ 16` produces verify
  passes of M ≥ 16; past position 512 the verify runs `gemm_w4a8_mma`/`attn_fused` and its argmaxes
  are not the sequential decode's. Serve's defaults (width 8, `MaxDraft 8`) stay under the floor by
  coincidence of constants.
- **Fix:** an explicit exact-only flag for `tailAllLogits`/`tailAllArgmax`/`tailHiddenLast` into
  `useAttnFused`/`useGemmMMA`; the row floors are perf knobs, not correctness guards.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), the exact flag the Fix line asked for — `forceExactKernels`
(`cuda/resident.go:657`), one field rather than a parameter threaded through `bGemvB`'s ~20 call
sites across `cuda/prefill.go` and `cuda/drafter.go` (CUDA contexts are thread-affine and every
call is already serialized through `r.do()`, so a per-pass field toggle set/cleared at
`prefillCore`'s own boundary is race-free and far less invasive than a signature change
everywhere). Set to `tail != tailLastLogits` once per `prefillCore` pass (`cuda/prefill.go:825`) —
true for `tailAllLogits`/`tailAllArgmax` (this finding) and `tailHiddenLast` (M-10) alike, false
only for ordinary single-row prefill, where the existing cosine-gated tolerance already applied
and still does. `useAttnFused`/`useGemmMMA` (`:1471`,`:1512`) both check it first, unconditionally.
The drafter's own forward (`cuda/drafter.go`) never sets or reads this field — a draft proposal
needs no exactness, only the target's verify of it does, and the field is scoped to exactly one
`prefillCore` pass so it can never leak into an unrelated call on the same resident.

Both doc comments' own pre-existing claims ("is what spec-decode verify... runs",
`useAttnFused`/`useGemmMMA`) were reworded: they asserted this as already true, but nothing
enforced it before this fix — verify's own M could cross the thresholds by coincidence of
constants exactly as this finding describes, not by construction. They now name
`forceExactKernels` as what makes the claim real.

Verified on real hardware (nobara, RTX 2070 SUPER), real model (qwen3-4b, int4, the shape this
finding is about), not simulated: `TestPrefillLastNArgmax_matchesPerRow`
(`cuda/dflash_dispatch_test.go`, existing test, `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS=1`) already covers
`PrefillLastN`/`PrefillLastNArgmax` at M up to 16 and depth 1024 (past the 512 floor) — but, read
closely, only for SELF-consistency (batched logits vs. batched argmax, the same dispatch on both
sides), not against a true sequential reference, so it could not by itself distinguish "fast
kernels engaged" from "fast kernels declined." Closed that gap directly rather than build new
test infrastructure: reverted `forceExactKernels` (only the two guard checks, not the field/set
site) and re-ran this exact test with a one-line addition logging the raw argmax ids. At M=16,
depth=1024, TWO of 16 positions flipped between fixed and reverted
(`...76 [82] 198 220 220 [198]...` fixed vs. `...76 [198] 198 220 220 [220]...` reverted, positions
3 and 7) — real, measurable divergence on production-scale weights, not a theoretical gap. Restored
the fix, confirmed the logging-added run matched the original; removed the temporary logging
before committing (not shipped).

#### M-12 · Fit-by-default (6c3645d) regresses to the CPU path on every card where 8192 does not fit outright: an interior `Plan` result can never pass `checkKVFits`
- **Where:** `decoder/fitplan.go:249-235` (`tryCtx`: `fits = Dense+kv+Extra <= free`, zero
  headroom), `:250-265` (`fitCtx = budget/perPos`, within one position of free), `cuda/resident.go:
  90-113` (`resolveCtxCapFit`), `:1427-1469` (`checkKVFits`: `need+extra+384 MiB <= free`),
  `cuda/backend.go:1447-1420` (unpinned miss → `declined`).
- **Failure:** when `dense + kv(8192) > free_probe`, `chooseCtx` returns `fitCtx` with
  `kv(fitCtx) > free_probe − dense − extra − perPos`; at build `free_build ≤ free_probe − dense −
  overhead`, so `checkKVFits` requires `384 MiB < perPos` — fails unconditionally, and the whole
  resident build declines to CPU. Llama-3-8B-class on a box with ~6.5–6.85 GB free (an 8 GB card
  with a desktop session) goes from resident@4096 to CPU-only. The commit's "no configuration where
  turning this on makes an existing deployment worse" is refuted; the nobara measurement landed on
  the `tryCtx(8192)` branch and never exercised the interior one.
- **Fix:** subtract `ctxCapMarginBytes` (and the build's fixed overhead) from `free` before `Plan`;
  and/or retry an unpinned fit-chosen cap at `cudaCtxCapDefault` instead of declining.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). The doc's own first sub-fix, exactly: `resolveCtxCapFit`
(`cuda/resident.go:104`) now subtracts `ctxCapMarginBytes` from `free` (`:115`) before ever calling
`Plan`, floored at 0. The retry-at-`cudaCtxCapDefault` alternative was not needed once the two
checks agree — `resolveCtxCapFit` already falls back to `cudaCtxCapDefault` when `Plan` declines
(`p.Placement == decoder.PlacementDecline`), and with the margin applied that fallback fires only
when the card genuinely cannot hold even the default, not on every interior choice as before.

Verified on real CUDA hardware (nobara, RTX 2070 SUPER): `TestResolveCtxCapFit_agreesWithCheckKVFits`
(`cuda/resident_cap_test.go`) — see M-22's own closure note below for the full test description,
since both findings share this one test (M-22's fix is what makes the test's "with a companion"
arm meaningful). TDD-verified: reverting just the margin subtraction reproduced the exact failure
mode this finding describes — `resolveCtxCapFit` chose ctx=8192 (llama-tiny's own dense weights are
too small to force a genuinely interior choice without the margin; the test forces the NEXT
narrowest disagreement, the ceiling, which the un-margined code cannot tell apart from a real fit
either), and `checkKVFits` refused it at the real build with `"cuda: default resident context 8192
positions does not fit... 7.35 GB free"`. Restored the fix, confirmed the same case chooses ctx=6000
(the deliberately-forced interior target) and passes `checkKVFits` cleanly.

#### M-15 · The G1c pre-tokenize guard counts message text only: tool schemas and replayed `tool_calls[].arguments` are rendered into the prompt but never priced — on all three tool surfaces and on the unqueued `count_tokens`
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/openai.go:172-162` (`chatInputBytes` sums `m.text()`), `tools.go:
  31,40`, `internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:181-178,448,486,575,587`, `internal/serveapp/responses.go:115,203`; `internal/serveapp/limits_test.go:151`
  (the gate checks the guard is *present*, not what it measures).
- **Failure:** a `/v1/messages/count_tokens` body with one 10-byte message and 8 MiB of
  `input_schema` passes the guard in constant time and runs the full BPE over the rendered schemas —
  the "~27 s of BPE + gigabytes of ids" M-21 (09-02) priced, on the route M-21 called the worst
  because it never enters the queue (up to `-max-inflight` = 128 concurrently).
- **Fix:** add rendered-tool bytes and tool-call argument bytes to both counters; widen the gate to
  assert the guard's argument covers every field the renderer reads.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-16** (`main`), both counters and all three surfaces.
`chatInputBytes` (OpenAI/Responses shape) now also sums each message's `ToolCalls[].Function.Name/
.Arguments` — an assistant's replayed tool calls, rendered into the prompt by `messagesToTurns`
unconditionally, regardless of whether tools are active *this* turn. A new `toolSchemaBytes`
prices `req.Tools[].Function.Name/.Description/.Parameters`, wired into the two call sites where
tool schemas can actually be rendered: `internal/serveapp/tools.go:31` (tools always active there) and
`internal/serveapp/responses.go:115` (added unconditionally, matching that guard's own documented "one call covers
both the tools/plain branches" design — `openai.go`'s plain-chat guard needed no schema term,
since `serveChatText` is only reached when tools are proven inactive for that turn, so nothing
there ever gets rendered). `anthropicInputBytes` gained the same two additions for the Anthropic
shape — `anthropicToolCallBytes` (a message's `tool_use` blocks, replayed the same
unconditional way) and `anthropicToolSchemaBytes` (`req.Tools[].InputSchema`) — both summed
unconditionally at its two call sites, again matching their own "one guard, both branches" design.

Verified two ways. (1) Direct unit tests proving the guard's argument now covers every field the
renderer reads, the doc's own ask: `TestChatInputBytes_countsReplayedToolCallArguments`,
`TestToolSchemaBytes_countsFunctionFields` (`internal/serveapp/limits_test.go`),
`TestAnthropicInputBytes_countsToolUseAndSchemas` (`internal/serveapp/bodycaps_routes_test.go`,
beside its sibling `TestAnthropicInputBytes_excludesImages`). (2)
`TestGuardComposition_hugeToolSchemaTripsTheBudgetAloneOnATinyMessage` reproduces the finding's own
worst-case shape (a tiny message, a huge schema) using the exact composition
`chatInputBytes(msgs) + toolSchemaBytes(tools)` the real call sites share, confirming the combined
count trips a budget the message text alone would pass — not a true end-to-end HTTP call, since
`promptTooLargeForContext` short-circuits to nil without a real `*decoder.Model` a unit test here
doesn't load, but proving the actual composition every fix site now uses, not just each addend in
isolation. TDD-verified: reverted all four production changes, confirmed all four new/extended
tests fail red with the exact real byte counts (e.g. "chatInputBytes with a replayed tool call = 2,
want 10003"), restored, confirmed byte-identical and green. `go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck`
clean; full `internal/serveapp` suite green.

#### M-17 · G4's resident `HiddenLast` is unreachable from `serve`: the decoder-embedder is loaded with `decoder.Options{}` (Backend "")
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/decoder_embedder.go:132` (now the fixed call, moved down by the fix's own explanatory comment — was `:111`), `decoder/embed.go:36-71` (0fe07ee);
  `docs/tasks/task-gpu-paths-2026-09.md:101-105` names this file as the only serving caller. The same
  load ignores `-embed-quant`/`--quant` for a `.gguf` embed model (`internal/serveapp/main.go:550,1224-1225`).
- **Failure:** `--backend metal -embed-model qwen3-embedding.gguf` builds no resident; every
  `/v1/embeddings` call takes the CPU sequential path — the exact G4 gap, with the implementations
  shipped and "verified" but never selected.
- **Fix:** `Backend: cfg.backend` (then `resBusy` contention with the chat model is a real
  consideration — the embedder shares the device).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). `internal/serveapp/decoder_embedder.go`'s
`loadDecoderEmbedder` now passes `Backend: cfg.backend` — confirmed sufficient for reachability by
reading `decoder.Load`'s own dispatch (`.withResidency()` keys off `Options.Backend` alone, no
separate resident-build step) and confirmed no new contention guard is needed (`resBusy` is
already a per-`*decoder.Model` CAS; N independently-resident chat models on one device already
work today the identical way). The doc's own one-line fix (`Quant: cfg.embedQuant`) was
incomplete and NOT applied as written: `-embed-quant`'s vocabulary (`"f32"|"q8"`) is not
`decoder.Options.Quant`'s (`""|"int8"|"int8int8"|"int4"`) — passing `"q8"` straight through would
have made `decoder.Load`'s `parseQuant` reject it as an unknown quant, a hard load failure worse
than the silent-ignore bug being fixed. Translated instead: `"q8"` → `"int8"` (weight-only,
matching the aikit encoder's own `LoadQ8` precision class), everything else → `""`.

Verified real-checkpoint, not by inspection: neither Metal nor WebGPU can actually activate from
`go test ./internal/serveapp/...` (both require their own submodule entrypoint to register a real
backend — confirmed by reading, then reproducing, `decoder.NewBackend`'s own "not built in" CPU
fallback), so the gate asserts against `decoder.Model.RequestedBackend()` instead of
`ResidentActive()` — the signal that survives the fallback and is exactly M-17's own claim ("the
decoder-embedder is loaded with `decoder.Options{}`"). TDD-verified: both the backend and the
quant-mapping tests fail red against the pre-fix code, pass green after.

#### M-18 · The resident CUDA vision tower leaks ~265 MB of device scratch and one stream per image, and is never enabled by `serve` or the agent on `--backend cuda` — while `docs/multimodal.md:171` says DONE
- **Where:** `cuda/vision_encoder.go:247-282` (now the fixed call sites — was 19 `r.af`/`r.ai` per
  call with no `ReleaseBuf`, `q := r.dev.NewCommandQueue()` per call, before the scratch free-list
  at `:256`), `:405-421` (`Close`, now releases on the executor thread — was `:380-389`); contrast
  `cuda/resident.go:1862` (`cudaResident.Close`'s own correct pattern); `internal/serveapp/main.go:1088`
  and `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:282-286` (`EnableResident()` — was gated on `webgpu` only);
  `docs/multimodal.md:132,172` (was: "cuda/metal have no vision tower" contradicting "CUDA — DONE").
- **Failure:** Gemma-3 SigLIP (M=4096) ≈ 265 MB per image held for the encoder's lifetime; ~15
  images exhaust the ~4 GB beside a resident 4B text model, after which launches on the shared
  context can "return SUCCESS and execute NOTHING" (backend.go's A13 note). Latent only because
  serve never enables it; the 1.58× in the doc was measured through the test.
- **Fix:** scratch free-list + defer as `prefillCore` does; queue created once at build; release
  inside the executor; enable for `cuda` in serve/agent; reconcile the two doc lines.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). All four sub-fixes landed, one narrowed on investigation:

1. **Scratch free-list + defer**, mirroring `cuda/prefill.go`'s own pattern exactly (register the
   list and its defer BEFORE the first allocation, so a later allocation panicking on OOM still
   releases everything already made — the same C-24 discipline that pattern's own comment
   documents): `cuda/vision_encoder.go:256-262` (the list + defer), `af`/`ai` closures wrapping
   every one of the 19 sites.
2. **Command queue created once**, not per call: a new `stream Queue` field on `VisionEncoder`
   (`:47`), set once in `NewVisionEncoder` right after device creation; `ForwardPatches` now reads
   `q := r.stream` (`:247`) instead of calling `r.dev.NewCommandQueue()` on every image.
3. **`Close` now releases on the executor thread**, not the caller's: sends the teardown as a job
   through `r.reqCh` and waits for `r.ackCh` before closing `r.reqCh` — the same order
   `cudaResident.Close` uses (`cuda/resident.go:1862-1841`), not the reverse (`:405-421`).
4. **Enablement:** `internal/serveapp/main.go`'s vision-load path now calls `EnableResident()` for
   `cfg.backend == "cuda"` too (was `webgpu`-only), and treats `cuda` as implying an int8 tower the
   same way `webgpu` already did. **Not extended to `demo/agent`**: tracing
   `demo/agent/agent/agent.go` found its text decoder is hardcoded to `Options{Backend: "cpu"}`
   (`:164`) and nothing in the tree blank-imports `github.com/townsendmerino/goinfer/cuda` for any
   `demo/agent` binary — there is no cuda submodule entrypoint for the agent at all (unlike
   `cuda/cmd/serve`), so adding `VisionBackend == "cuda"` there would be a string comparison that
   could never be true in any real build. Wiring a `cuda`-tagged agent entrypoint is a separate,
   larger change than this finding's own scope.

`docs/multimodal.md:132,172` reconciled: both now state plainly that CUDA's tower existed
(2026-09-08) but was unreachable from `serve --backend cuda` until this fix (2026-09-15), rather
than the prior contradiction ("cuda/metal have no vision tower" vs. "CUDA — DONE").

Verified on real CUDA hardware (nobara, RTX 2070 SUPER) — not simulated, this class of bug is
invisible to a cosine-only correctness gate: the existing real-checkpoint parity test
(`TestGemma3VisionResidentReal_gate`, gemma-3-4b-it, cosine 0.914 post-fix, within its own
pre-registered floor) proves numerics are unaffected, but a single `Forward` call can't
distinguish "releases scratch" from "leaks every call." A new test,
`TestVisionEncoder_forwardPatchesReleasesDeviceScratch` (`cuda/vision_encoder_leak_test.go`), calls
`ForwardPatches` 11 times (one warm-up + 10 measured) directly against `cuda.NewVisionEncoder`
(bypassing the `vision.Encoder` wrapper to reach `r.dev.Context().MemInfo()`) and asserts free VRAM
after the 10 calls is within 16 MiB of free VRAM after the warm-up call — TDD-verified: reverting
the fix dropped free VRAM by 2.38 GiB over the 10 calls (~256 MB/call, matching this finding's own
"~265 MB per image" estimate almost exactly); the fixed code holds the same 10 calls to a 960 KiB
drift (allocator noise, not a leak).

#### M-20 · `fit -measure` loads the checkpoint a second time while the first copy is still resident, then reports a rate that includes the prefill *(two reviewers)*
- **Where:** `internal/fitcmd/fit.go:83-88` (`defer m.Close()` scoped to `Run`), `:107-111`,
  `:126-153` (`selfMeasure`: second `Load`, `rate := n / elapsed` from `Generate`'s call).
- **Failure:** two full quantized copies on the CPU backend; the second load trips the fit guard
  ("load failed, skipping probe") or swaps, and the printed tok/s is a swapping machine's number —
  the "plausible, wrong number" §5 of the task doc exists to avoid. The rate is
  `n/(prefill(64)+decode(n))` — roughly a third of the decode rate on the CPU staged path — while
  §5/G5 compare it to `benchmarks.md` decode rows.
- **Fix:** `m.Close()` before `selfMeasure` (or reuse `m` on CPU); time from the first generated
  token.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), the doc's own suggested fix, both halves. `Run` now closes
`m` explicitly right after the backend-report loop — before `selfMeasure`'s own fresh `Load`, not
after — replacing the function-scoped `defer m.Close()` that kept it resident through the whole
probe. `selfMeasure` starts its clock when the FIRST generated token arrives (not before `Generate`
is called), so the reported rate covers only the `probeDecode-1` decode steps after it, excluding
the fixed one-time prompt prefill entirely; the printed line now says so explicitly and reports
the excluded prefill's total wall time separately for transparency, rather than folding it
silently into "tok/s."

Verified two ways, both TDD red/green against real captured output: (1)
`TestRun_closesFirstLoadBeforeMeasuring` (new) — a structural AST check, the same technique
`TestTranscode_writesViaTempThenRenames` (`internal/prequant`) already uses for an analogous
ordering property, since the real failure mode (a large checkpoint tripping the memory guard
during the overlap) can't be reproduced with this repo's tiny fixtures; reverting the close order
made it fail with the exact real statement-index mismatch. (2)
`TestRun_measureRateExcludesPrefill` (new) — drives the real CLI end to end on
`testdata/llama-tiny`, asserting the printed line names "after the first token" (not the old
"includes a N-token prompt prefill" phrasing) and reports exactly `probeDecode-1 = 31` decode
steps, not `32` — proving the first step was excluded from the denominator, not just relabeled;
reverting either half made the corresponding test fail with the real printed output pasted into
the failure message. `go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean; full `internal/fitcmd` suite
(8 tests) green.

#### M-21 · Drafter device scratch is still grow-and-abandon at four sites (C-12 (09-02)'s fix reached `logitsB` only)
- **Where:** `cuda/drafter.go:191,310,463,654` (now the fixed release-before-grow guards — was
  `187-193,298-306,441-451,624-629`); mirrors `cuda/prefill.go:1303` (`batchedHeadArgmax`, release
  before grow).
- **Failure:** `ctxIn` is `n × nTaps×hidden` f32 (Qwen3-8B, 5 taps, 4096-token prompt: 335 MB)
  plus `ctxFuse` 67 MB; each longer prompt abandons its predecessors to the ledger — the mechanism
  C-12 described, on the path C-12's "Fix" line named.
- **Fix:** `ReleaseBuf` the old buffers before reallocating, as `batchedHeadArgmax` does.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). Exactly the doc's own fix at all four sites, same
`if <cap> > 0 { release old buffers } ; <allocate new, larger ones> ; <cap> = n` shape as
`batchedHeadArgmax` (`cuda/prefill.go`): `FuseContext` (`cuda/drafter.go:191`, 4 buffers),
`ExtendContext` (`:310`, 6 buffers — its OTHER grow site, the K/V cache at `kc`/`kvCap`, was
already safe: it refuses instead of reallocating past capacity mid-sequence, an M-15
(audit-2026-09-02) fix already in place, not part of this finding), `DraftBlock` (`:463`, 16
buffers, the block scratch struct), `DraftTokens` (`:654`, 4 buffers).

Verified on real CUDA hardware (nobara, RTX 2070 SUPER), real drafter (DFlash, 5 layers, attached
to a real qwen3-4b target) for one of the four sites directly:
`TestResidentDrafter_fuseContextReleasesScratch` (`cuda/drafter_leak_test.go`) drives `FuseContext`
through a DOUBLING row-count ramp (8→16→32→64) — a fixed size never re-triggers the grow branch at
all, so the ramp is what makes every call a real grow-and-release cycle — and bounds the VRAM drop
at 1.5x one call's own buffer set (computed from the same sizing formula the source uses). TDD-
verified: reverting just `FuseContext`'s release lines dropped free VRAM by 8.4 MB over the ramp
against 4.2 MB fixed (a single call's own buffers measure ≈4.75 MB) — real, measurable leak on
production-scale weights, not a theoretical one. The other three sites share the byte-for-byte
identical fix shape (release-then-grow, guarded on `cap > 0`) and are covered by the existing
`TestResidentDrafter_extendContext`/`_blockParity` correctness tests staying green (proving the
release doesn't corrupt the reused buffers) plus `go vet`/`staticcheck`, but were not independently
leak-tested on real hardware this session — the same discipline gap as any n-of-4 mechanical fix
verified once and reasoned to generalize, noted rather than silently assumed.

#### M-22 · The drafter-aware reservation (35e07f9) prices the drafter's WEIGHTS only; its K/V (sized to the ctx fit-by-default grows) and its prompt-sized scratch are unpriced
- **Where:** `decoder/blockdrafter.go:141-154` (original gap), `:174` (`DrafterKVBytesPerPosition`,
  the fix), `decoder/model.go:65,162-171,278-284` (`ExtraResidentKVPerPosition` field/accessor),
  `cuda/resident.go:104,115-127` (`resolveCtxCapFit`, priced at `candidate`), `cuda/backend.go:553,586`
  (`residentCtxCap` local, priced at the final chosen ctx).
- **Failure:** a 5-layer DFlash trunk holds `5 × 8192 × 1024 × 8 B` ≈ 335 MB of K/V at the ctx
