  fit-by-default now chooses, plus ~400 MB of `FuseContext` scratch for a 4096-token prompt, none
  of it in `ExtraResidentBytes`; the attach or the first long seed OOMs — the §2 scenario the commit
  says it closed, one term short (its own message records the residual risk).
- **Fix:** include `layers × ctx × kvDim × 8` and a scratch term, or size the drafter's KV
  independently of `r.ctxCap`.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), the doc's own first sub-fix — `layers × ctx × kvDim × 8` —
not the "size independently of `r.ctxCap`" alternative: the drafter's K/V genuinely NEEDS to track
the target's chosen context (that's what lets it draft as deep as the target can reach), so
decoupling it would be a real capability regression, not just a pricing fix. The scratch term
(FuseContext etc.) is still NOT priced, on purpose — `DrafterResidentBytesEstimate`'s own doc
comment already argued those buffers are "a few MB... the kind of small residual the existing 384
MiB `ctxCapMarginBytes`... margin is there to absorb," and M-21's own real measurement (one
`FuseContext` call ≈ 4.75 MB) confirms that was right, not a hand-wave — now that M-12 restored the
margin's real effectiveness, that reasoning holds again.

**The circular dependency the doc's own two-clause Fix line was hedging around** — the drafter's K/V
needs `ctx` to price, but `ctx` is exactly what `Plan` is being asked to choose — is resolved
without iteration: `DrafterKVBytesPerPosition` (`decoder/blockdrafter.go:174`) returns a RATE
(bytes/position), not a total, computed once at `loadDecoder` time from the drafter's own geometry
alone (no target ctx needed for that part). Two multiplications, at two different points that each
already know the ctx they have in hand:
- **Planning time**: `resolveCtxCapFit` (`cuda/resident.go:143`) multiplies the rate by `candidate`
  — the widest ctx THIS call is asking `Plan` to fit, before `Plan` has shrunk it to whatever the
  card actually holds. `Plan`'s own `chooseCtx` only ever shrinks from the value it is asked with,
  never grows past it (`fitplan.go`'s own "never GROW past what was asked"), so pricing at
  `candidate` can only over-estimate the eventual real cost (safe) or land exactly on it.
- **Build time**: `cuda/backend.go:586` multiplies the SAME rate by `residentCtxCap` — the FINAL,
  actually-chosen ctx (computed once, referenced by both `ctxCap` and `extraBytes` since a struct
  literal cannot reference a sibling field being set in the same literal) — the real number
  `checkKVFits` enforces against the real allocation about to happen.

`decoder.Options` gained the mirrored field, `ExtraResidentKVPerPosition` (`decoder/model.go:171`
for the accessor), alongside the existing `ExtraResidentBytes` — `internal/serveapp/main.go`'s
`loadDecoder` sets both from the same `DrafterResidentBytesEstimate`/`DrafterKVBytesPerPosition`
pair when a `--drafter` is loading.

Verified on real CUDA hardware (nobara, RTX 2070 SUPER), sharing M-12's own test:
`TestResolveCtxCapFit_agreesWithCheckKVFits` (`cuda/resident_cap_test.go`) forces the INTERIOR
`chooseCtx` branch deterministically (a real card usually has far more free VRAM than
`testdata/llama-tiny` could ever need, so the default candidate hits the ceiling outright — never
buggy even before either fix, and not what either finding is about) via a computed
`ExtraResidentBytes`, then runs with and without an ADDITIONAL simulated companion K/V rate (40960
B/position — the audit's own cited real number, "5 × 8192 × 1024 × 8 B ≈ 335 MB" for an
8192-position target). Both arms land at the same deliberately-forced ctx=6000 and pass
`checkKVFits`, proving the planning-time estimate (at `candidate`) and the build-time actual
(at the final ctx) stay consistent, not just individually plausible. TDD-verified together with
M-12 (both fixes reverted at once reproduces one compound failure, not two independent ones, so
they were verified as the single change they are): the reverted run chose ctx=8192 for BOTH arms
(the companion term added no NEW divergence once the margin was already gone) and `checkKVFits`
refused it; the fixed run separates them again, landing the no-companion arm at 6000 and the
companion arm slightly lower internally before settling back to the same forced 6000 target via
this test's own per-case forcing term (see that test's comments for why the forcing budget is
computed per sub-case, not shared).


#### Metal

#### M-23 · G8's "Gemma set" batched prefill never engages on a real Gemma 3 — `FeatPerLayerRoPE` is required by every mixed local/global Gemma and is not in `prefillFeatures`; the gating fixture has two sliding layers
- **Where:** `metal/model.go:112-122` (`prefillFeatures`), `:647`, `decoder/features.go:152`
  (`add(!a.ropeUniform(), FeatPerLayerRoPE)`), `metal/prefill.go:645-591` (the dispatch already
  binds `L.invf` per layer), `testdata/gemma3-vl-tiny/config.json:22-25` (`[sliding, sliding]`),
  `metal/prefill_gemma_test.go:44-47`, `metal/model.go:739-657` (claims Gemma 3 is covered).
- **Failure:** every real Gemma 3 (5:1 local/global, 10k vs 1M base) derives `FeatPerLayerRoPE`,
  `prefillOK` is false, `PrefillLast` declines, the prompt runs sequentially. Correct output; the G8
  row and the code comment promise a batched TTFT no shipped Gemma 3 gets. CLAUDE.md's "minimal
  repro is minimal in exactly the dimension that matters".
- **Fix:** `FeatPerLayerRoPE: true` in `prefillFeatures`; a global layer in the fixture.
- **CLOSED 2026-09-13, shipped exactly as scoped.** This finding was re-filed and fixed as
  `docs/audit-metal-2026-09-12.md`'s M-06 (`84c3f29f`) — `FeatPerLayerRoPE: true` declared in
  `metal/model.go`'s `prefillFeatures`, and the fixture given a global layer (that doc's G-03).
  See M-06's own entry there for the full verification (freshly regenerated checkpoint, both
  dependent goldens, `go test ./metal/...`/`./decoder/...`/`gpu/...`/`multimodal/...` all green).

#### M-24 · The Metal memory-fit guard double-counts `.giw`-mmap-backed int4 weights as an anonymous host copy
- **Where:** `metal/backend.go:317-227` (need = paged + host copy + KV), `decoder/weightbytes.go:
  101-107` ("Dense weights … always double this way"), `metal/model.go:416-402` (`int4DirectBytes`
  — nibbles "ALIASED straight from the mmap"), `:428-429`, `decoder/model.go:65`.
- **Failure:** a 14B int4 `.giw` (~8.5 GB) on a 16 GB Mac: real anonymous footprint ≈ 8.75 GB
  under the 11.2 GB budget; the guard computes ≈ 17.3 GB and declines to CPU — M-02 (09-02)'s class
  reintroduced from the other direction by 6af5189's host-copy term.
- **Fix:** exclude matrices whose `Int4()` payload resolves via `MmapByteOffset` (the test
  `buildMoELayer` already does per expert, `metal/moe.go:539-531`).
- **Confidence:** plausible (relies on the loader's own comments; `giwReader` not read).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-16** (`main`). The "plausible" confidence is now confirmed real, not just
inferred from comments: `decoder/serialize.go`'s `.giw` reader (`giwReader.weightMat`) aliases
int8/int4/int4Row4 payloads straight into the mmap for EVERY tensor the format supports zero-copy
for — not only paged experts. `residentWeightBytesSplit` (`decoder/weightbytes.go`) gained a
parallel accumulator, `mmapAliasedBytes` — the same int8/int4/int4Row4 cases `wmBytes` already
sums, but only counting bytes that resolve via `m.MmapByteOffset` — computed in the SAME
enumeration pass so the "how many bytes" and "how many are mmap-backed" questions can never
disagree about which matrices exist. `ResidentHostCopyBytes` now subtracts that mmap-aliased
portion (both the dense and, when unpaged, the full-expert term) before returning; `MmapByteOffset`
itself does no page-rounding (unlike aikit's `WeightMat.MappedSpan`, used for the SEPARATE paging
registry, `metal/moepaging.go`) — a byte-accounting question ("did this allocation happen") is not
the same question as a pageable-span one ("can `MADV_DONTNEED` release whole pages of it"), and
page-rounding would undercount a small-but-real mmap-backed tensor for no reason that matters here.

Verified with real TDD red/green, real numbers: `TestResidentHostCopyBytes_exemptsMmapAliasedGIWWeights`
(new, `decoder/weightbytes_test.go`) loads `testdata/llama-tiny` at `int4` two ways — heap
(safetensors dir, a genuine second host allocation) and via a real `.giw` built from the same
fixture (`SerializeWeightsToForTarget` + `giw.WriteStream`, mmap-reloaded) — and asserts the heap
load still doubles exactly (`ResidentHostCopyBytes(0) == ResidentWeightBytesPaged(0)`, the control
case) while the `.giw` load's host-copy addend is strictly smaller. Measured on this fixture: heap
`device=219136 host=219136` (doubles, correctly) vs. `.giw` `device=126976 host=0` (every byte
resolved as mmap-aliased once the fix landed). Reverted the fix, confirmed the test fails red with
exactly `.giw-loaded: ResidentHostCopyBytes(0) = 126976, want strictly < ... 126976` (the real
un-fixed number), restored, confirmed byte-identical and green. Every pre-existing
`ResidentWeightBytes*`/`ResidentHostCopyBytes`/`TestFitEstimate_*` test (none of which loads via
`.giw`, so `mmapAliasedBytes` correctly contributes 0 for all of them) passed unchanged. `go build`/
`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean on `decoder` and `metal`; full `decoder` suite (211s) green — this
file is not tracked by the parity manifest (pure byte accounting, no forward-pass numerics), so no
`TestParityManifest_fresh` staleness or refresh was triggered, unlike every other core-file fix
this session.

**Scope note:** the fix landed in `decoder/weightbytes.go` (`ResidentHostCopyBytes`, the shared
accessor both `metal/backend.go`'s `residentNeedBytes` and CUDA's own guard call) rather than in
`metal/backend.go` directly — the doc's own citation of `metal/model.go`'s `int4DirectBytes` (the
zero-copy paged-expert sibling of `int4DirectWords`) turned out to describe the PAGED-expert case
`ResidentHostCopyBytes` already exempted before this fix; the actual gap was DENSE (and unpaged
full-expert) mmap-aliased bytes, which this fix closes at the one shared chokepoint rather than
per-backend.

#### M-25 · The int8→int4 requant label (G10) covers only `int8int8`; `--quant int8` and `int4mix` on Metal still report a precision the GPU never runs, and the chat/gemma banners print the raw request
- **Where:** `decoder/residency.go:1025-1004` (`residentQuantLabel`: `quant == "int8int8"` only),
  `metal/model.go:496-474` (`int4Buf` requantizes ANY `Int8()` kind),
  `decoder/staged_device_note_test.go:48` (`{"metal", "int8", "int8"}` pins the wrong answer), `internal/chatapp/main.go:402-358`, `internal/gemmaapp/main.go:100` (print `model.Quant()`, not `DecodePath()`).
- **Fix:** `backend == "metal" && quant != "int4"`; chat/gemma banners print `DecodePath()`.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-16** (`main`). `residentQuantLabel` now covers `int8`, `int8int8`, and
`int4mix` — not the doc's own literal `quant != "int4"` (which would also relabel `f32`), narrowed
after confirming f32 genuinely doesn't hit this bug: an f32-kind `WeightMat` has neither an
already-int4 nibble path nor an `Int8()` fallback for `int4Buf` to silently requantize through,
and every `int4Buf` call site `panic`s rather than proceeding, so Metal never actually produces a
resident build to mislabel for f32 in the first place — it errors/declines well before `DecodePath`
would print anything. `int4mix`'s int8-kind tensors (the ones it deliberately did *not* keep at
int4) hit the identical fallback as plain `int8`/`int8int8`, so even a "mix" ends up effectively
uniform int4 on this backend — worth saying explicitly rather than just widening the string match.
`internal/chatapp/main.go` and `internal/gemmaapp/main.go`'s load banners now print
`model.DecodePath()` (which embeds the corrected label) instead of the raw requested quant.

Verified with a real TDD red/green: extended `TestResidentQuantLabel`
(`decoder/staged_device_note_test.go`) with `int8`/`int4mix` cases (plus explicit passthrough
cases for `int4`/`f32` on metal and `int8`/`int4mix` on cuda, proving the narrower condition
doesn't over-reach); reverted the fix, confirmed it fails red with the exact real mislabeled
strings ("int8" and "int4mix" printed verbatim instead of the corrected label), restored, confirmed
byte-identical and green. `go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean on all three touched packages;
broader `decoder` quant/residency/decode-path suite unaffected. The banner half is a straight
printf swap with no numeric behavior to TDD — verified by reading `DecodePath()`'s own
implementation (`decoder/residency.go:1000`) to confirm it already embeds `residentQuantLabel`'s
corrected string, and building both binaries clean.

#### M-26 · `--exact-prefill` exists only on `serve`; `chat` and `gemma` on Metal get the default-ON fast path with no flag, and the `Options.ExactPrefill` the task doc names does not exist
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/main.go:368-483` (the only writer of
  `GOINFER_METAL_FAST_PREFILL=0`), `internal/chatapp/main.go:210-190` (no such flag; `:174` help
  still says "All quantized modes get batched CUDA prefill"), `docs/completed/task-prefill-gap.md:187-191`
  ("`--exact-prefill` (server) / `Options.ExactPrefill` (library)"); `grep ExactPrefill decoder/`
  is empty. (And on serve the flag does not reach CUDA: M-48.)
- **Fix:** the flag in chatapp; an `Options.ExactPrefill` mapped to the same knobs.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-16** (`main`), the design decided as: add the real
`decoder.Options.ExactPrefill` field (a single load-time chokepoint), rather than only exporting/
sharing serve's own CLI-local `applyExactPrefillEnv`. `decoder.Load` now sets
`GOINFER_METAL_FAST_PREFILL`/`GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL`/`GOINFER_CPU_FAST_ATTENTION` to `"0"`
when `opts.ExactPrefill` is true — the exact three env vars `applyExactPrefillEnv`'s own
`--exact-prefill` branch sets, which is now genuinely real rather than a claim
`docs/completed/task-prefill-gap.md` made about code that didn't exist. `internal/chatapp` and
`internal/gemmaapp` each gained their own `--exact-prefill` flag wired to it; chatapp's stale
`--quant` help text ("All quantized modes get batched CUDA prefill") now also names Metal and
points at the new flag. `internal/serveapp/main.go` itself is **unchanged** — its own
`applyExactPrefillEnv` keeps its existing three-flag granularity (`--exact-prefill`/
`--cpu-exact-prefill`/`--cpu-fast-attention`, richer than the new single bool) and continues to
set the env vars directly at startup; wiring it through the new `Options` field too would have
required either collapsing that granularity or layering two independent env-setters with no clean
ordering guarantee, for no behavior change server operators would see.

**Deliberate design note on `Load`'s false branch:** setting the field only takes effect when
`true` — `false` (the zero value) is a genuine no-op, never resetting the env vars to `"1"`/unset.
This matters because `decoder.Load` is called by every library consumer, potentially many times in
one process with different `Options` each time (serve's own admin-load/model-swap paths), and an
unconditional reset would clobber whatever a caller managing these same env vars itself (serve's
own `applyExactPrefillEnv`) had already decided, independently of any one `Load` call's own
`ExactPrefill` value.

Verified with real TDD red/green: `decoder/exactprefill_test.go` (new) —
`TestLoad_exactPrefillOptionSetsAllThreeBackendEnvVars` (drives a real `Load` against
`testdata/llama-tiny`, asserts all three env vars) and
`TestLoad_exactPrefillFalseLeavesEnvUntouched` (pre-sets a sentinel value, confirms `Load` with
`ExactPrefill` unset leaves it exactly alone — the "never clobber a caller's own decision"
property, not just "does nothing when nothing was asked"). Reverted the fix, confirmed the
positive test fails red with the real empty-string env values, restored, confirmed byte-identical
and green. `TestExactPrefillFlag_realBinaryParses` (`internal/chatapp/fitflag_test.go`, new)
builds the real `demo/chat` binary and confirms `--exact-prefill` is genuinely registered on
`flag.CommandLine`, mirroring the existing `TestFitFlag_realBinaryAcceptsOff` pattern — gemmaapp
has no existing test file/infrastructure at all, so no equivalent was added there; its wiring
(one flag, one field) was verified by build + direct code reading instead. `go build`/`go vet`/
`staticcheck` clean on all three touched packages; full `decoder` suite green apart from the
expected `TestParityManifest_fresh` staleness (`decoder/model.go` is a core file), refreshed via
`scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh` (forward goldens green, confirming this change is numerically
inert — it only ever sets env vars that other code paths already read, never alters a computed
value itself).


#### Loaders, formats, admission

#### M-27 · Generic GGUF MoE loaders quantize the router; CUDA/Metal residency requires an f32 router, so every GLM/Mellum/Qwen3-MoE/DeepSeek/Laguna/Granite-H/Nemotron/Llama-4 GGUF at `int8int8|int4` silently declines to CPU — and GGUF ≠ safetensors on router precision
- **Where:** `decoder/gguf.go:2872,2183,2317,2461,2699` (router via `mat`) vs `:2054` (gpt-oss,
  `streamMat(…, quantNone, …)` with the comment "Plain mat() here was a latent bug") and `:1921`
  (qwen35); safetensors `decoder/weights.go:960` (f32); consumers `cuda/backend.go:369-375`
  (`declined(… router is %q, not f32 …)`), `metal/moe.go:467→627-630` (panic, recovered into a
  decline at `metal/backend.go:74-80`); `decoder/weightmat.go:44-52` (`matmulQuant` keys "FFN" on
  `ffn_` — so under `int4mix` the router lands **int4**, the opposite of its own comment).
- **Failure:** top-k selection on a group-32 int4 router flips experts near ties ("a cliff and not
  a small error", the CUDA comment); and the resident admission `docs/hardware-matrix.md:19,31,42`
  promises for GLM-4.5, Mellum2 and Qwen3-MoE ("picks it up for free on cuda/metal/webgpu",
  e84ea98) is unreachable from a GGUF at any quant but f32. The decline is on stderr; the banner
  reasons it as "the backend declined", not "the loader quantized the one tensor the backend
  forbids".
- **Fix:** `streamMat(…, quantNone, …)` at all five sites (one helper); `matmulQuant` excludes
  `ffn_gate_inp`.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), and the doc's own "five sites" undercounts — it's six: a
research pass found a sixth, uncited site at `decoder/gguf.go:2627` (Gemma4 MoE's `gm.routerProj`),
loaded via the same broken `mat()` pattern. All six now route through
`streamMat(p+"ffn_gate_inp.weight", …, quantNone, …)`, matching the gpt-oss/qwen35 pattern exactly:
Laguna (`:2183`), Granite-H (`:2317`), Nemotron MoE (`:2461`), Llama-4 (`:2699`), the shared
generic branch covering GLM/Mellum/Qwen3-MoE/DeepSeek2 (`:2858`), and Gemma4 MoE (`:2613`).
`matmulQuant` (`decoder/weightmat.go:44`) now excludes any name containing `ffn_gate_inp` from
its `"ffn_"` bulk match before falling into the int4 branch — not load-bearing for the six sites
above (once routed through `streamMat(…, quantNone, …)` they never call `matmulQuant` at all),
but a guardrail against the next family reaching the router through the generic `mat()` helper and
repeating this exact mistake. Bonus fix, same root cause: Gemma4's own resident-layer builder
(`decoder/residency.go:519`) silently returned `ok=false` when the router wasn't f32, unlike
`cuda/backend.go`'s named decline — now prints a specific reason so a future regression here
doesn't reintroduce a silent/confusing decline (it produced a misleading "gate/up/down proj
zero-shape" error two frames later, not "router is not f32").

Verified on this Mac (no CUDA hardware needed — the bug and fix are entirely in the loader and the
weight-precision policy, backend-agnostic):
`TestMatmulQuant_routerStaysInt8UnderInt4Mix` (`decoder/weightmat_test.go`) — direct unit test on
`matmulQuant`, TDD red (`quantInt4` returned for the router under `int4mix`) → green (`quantInt8`).
`TestGGUFRouter_staysF32AtQuant` (`decoder/gguf_router_quant_test.go`) — loads
`testdata/glm-tiny.gguf` at `int8/int8int8/int4/int4mix`, asserts every MoE layer's
`Router.Kind() == "f32"`; covers the shared branch (GLM/Mellum/Qwen3-MoE/DeepSeek2). TDD red:
`kind="int8"` at int8 modes, `kind="int4"` at int4/int4mix. `TestBuildResident_glmMoE_routerNotDeclined`
(`metal/gguf_router_quant_test.go`) — loads the same fixture at `int8int8` and asserts Metal's
`BuildResident` returns `ok=true`; TDD red reproduced the exact real decline text: `metal MoE:
weight kind "int8" is not f32 (router/shared-gate must be full precision)`. Laguna/Granite-H/
Nemotron/Llama-4/Gemma4 have no small pinned-quantized GGUF fixture today (only `realckpt`-gated
real-checkpoint tests) — those five sites are verified by code symmetry with the fixed/tested
branch (identical `mat()`→`streamMat(…, quantNone, …)` swap, confirmed by direct code read), not
independently checkpoint-tested.

#### M-28 · The fit guard prices KV at `NumLayers × NumKVHeads × headDim` for every family — 3–7× too high on hybrid (DeltaNet/conv/Mamba), sliding-window and MLA models — so it auto-pins a context several times smaller than what fits and `AdmitPrefillMemory` 413s requests that fit
- **Where:** `decoder/fitguard.go:523-412` (`kvBytesPerPosition`), `:505-506,521-536`,
  `decoder/prefill_budget.go:116`, `decoder/fitplan.go:122-122` (`kvHeadsAt` collapses to
  `NumKVHeads` for every non-gemma4 family, `decoder/residency.go:635`); what the cache holds:
  `decoder/model.go:630-535` (rings on sliding layers; no KV on linear layers).
- **Failure:** Qwen3.5-4B (32 layers, 8 softmax): priced 128 KiB/position vs 32 KiB stored; with
  ~5 GB left the guard pins ctx ≈ 43k where ≈ 170k fits, and refuses a 40k prompt + 4k max_tokens
  ("needs ~5.4 GB") when the real KV is 1.4 GB. olmo3-7B ~3.4× over at 65k; gemma3-12b ~6× at
  131k; DeepSeek-V2-Lite 7× (MLA stores 576/layer, priced 4096). The guard's own doc says an
  estimate "wrong in the refusing direction is worse than none" (`:440-441`).
- **Fix:** price per layer from the resolved arch (skip linear/conv/mamba layers, cap rings at
  `min(ctx, SlidingWindow)`, use `KVLoRARank+QKRopeHeadDim` for MLA); `fitplan.go` already loops
  per layer and `fitCheckFor` already resolves the arch.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), all three sub-fixes exactly as the doc's own Fix line
describes. New shared per-layer functions in `decoder/arch.go`: `kvDimAt` (`:508`, the WIDTH —
zero for `isLinearLayer`/`isMambaLayer`/`isConvLayer`, `KVLoRARank+QKRopeHeadDim` when
`arch.mla != nil`, else the ordinary `kvHeadsAt(i)*headDimAt(i)`), `kvPositionsAt` (`:523`, the
COUNT — `min(ctx, SlidingWindow)` for a local layer past its window), and `kvBytesForCtx` (`:541`,
summing both across every layer). `decoder/fitguard.go`'s `estimateKVBytes` (`:553`) now resolves
the real architecture and calls `kvBytesForCtx`, falling back to the pre-fix flat formula
(`kvBytesPerPosition`, `:523`, kept unchanged) only when the architecture cannot be resolved at
all — not a real load, but the shape several of this file's own unit tests construct directly
with a synthetic, unregistered `model_type`. `smallerFittingContext` (`:660`) changed from a
division to a binary search, since KV bytes are no longer exactly linear in `ctx` once a
sliding-window layer's cost flattens past its own window — still monotonic non-decreasing, so the
search is exact. `decoder/prefill_budget.go`'s two callers (`AdmitPrefillMemory`,
`FitBudgetSummary`) now call `estimateKVBytes` directly instead of `kvBytesPerPosition(...)*ctx`,
fixing the exact "refuses a 40k prompt + 4k max_tokens" request-time failure mode the finding
describes, not just the load-time guard.

**Scoping note, deliberate:** `decoder/fitplan.go`'s own `kvBytesPerPositionAllLayers` (`:122`)
— Plan's device-VRAM formula, a separate concern from the host-RAM guard above — got the
WIDTH half of this fix (`Architecture.kvDimAt`, so its MLA/hybrid pricing is now correct too) but
NOT the sliding-window COUNT cap: that function returns a flat per-position rate multiplied by
`ctx` at each of its 12 call sites (including 12 in `fitplan_test.go`, all against the dense
`llama-tiny` fixture, unaffected either way), and a sliding-window layer's true cost is not linear
in `ctx` — doing that properly needs `Plan()`'s own `tryCtx`/`chooseCtx` to search rather than
multiply, a real restructuring with no sliding-window fixture in that file's test suite to verify
against. No family in the registry combines a sliding window with an MLA/hybrid mixer today, so
this is a real, narrow gap, not a guess deferred without reason — documented in the function's own
doc comment for whoever adds one next.

Verified on this Mac (no CUDA hardware needed — backend-agnostic Config/Architecture arithmetic):
`decoder/fitkv_test.go` — `TestKvDimAt_zeroForRecurrentLayers`,
`TestKvDimAt_mlaUsesCompressedLatentNotReconstructedWidth`,
`TestKvPositionsAt_slidingWindowCapsLocalLayersNotGlobal`, `TestKvBytesForCtx_skipsRecurrentLayers`
(synthetic architectures, exact arithmetic) and three REAL-fixture regressions comparing the fixed
estimate against the pre-fix flat formula: `TestFitGuard_mlaKVPricedAtCompressedLatentNotFullWidth`
(`testdata/deepseek-tiny`, 1.33x reduction at ctx=4096), `TestFitGuard_hybridKVSkipsRecurrentLayers`
(`testdata/qwen35-tiny`, the audit's own cited family, 4.00x), `TestFitGuard_slidingWindowFlattensPastTheWindow`
(`testdata/olmo3-tiny`, the audit's own cited family, 2.91x at 8x the window). Every pre-existing
`TestFit*`/`TestPlan_*` test (including the ones driving synthetic Configs with no registered
`model_type`, which exercise the flat-formula fallback) passed unchanged — TDD-verified by
reverting `kvDimAt`/`kvPositionsAt` to their pre-fix no-ops and confirming all seven new tests
fail red with the exact real numbers logged above, then restoring and confirming green.

**Addendum 2026-09-15, found while implementing P-02:** the fix above had a residual gap on
Nemotron. `isMambaLayer`/`isLinearLayer`/`isConvLayer` are registry-time closures (e.g. Granite's
`layerIsMamba`), but Nemotron's per-layer mixer identity is *runtime data* —
`nemotronParams.blockKind []uint8`, populated from `layers_block_type` in config, read only by
`decoder/forward_nemotron.go`'s own per-layer switch. None of the three predicates ever
fires for a Nemotron layer regardless of its real kind, so `kvDimAt` (this finding's own fix) was
still pricing Nemotron's mamba/mlp/moe layers at full attention width. Confirmed concretely
against `testdata/nemotron-tiny` (`layers_block_type =
["mamba","attention","mlp","mamba","attention"]`): `kvDimAt` returned nonzero for all 5 layers
before this addendum, `[0,32,0,0,32]` after. Fixed by adding
`Architecture.hasNoAttentionKVAt(i int) bool` (`decoder/arch.go`), which combines the three
existing predicates with a new `a.nemotron != nil && a.nemotron.blockKind[i] != nemoAttn` check;
`kvDimAt` now calls it instead of the inline three-predicate check. New test
`TestKvDimAt_zeroForNemotronNonAttentionLayers` (`decoder/fitkv_test.go`), TDD red/green against
the real fixture. This method is also what P-02's own fix (below) reuses for KV cache
*allocation*, not just pricing — one shared predicate for both.

#### M-29 · The fit guard prices f32-resident mixers (Mamba-2, MLA, KDA) and GPTQ/AWQ `qweight` and the vision tower at the int4/int8 rate — a Nemotron-H-8B "≈4.7 GB at int4" lands at ≈13 GB, admitted, on the machine the guard was written for
- **Where:** `decoder/fitguard.go:412,463` (`estimateGGUFWeightBytes`/`estimateSafetensorsWeightBytes`,
  every ≥2-D tensor at `quantBytesPerElem(q)`), `:359,379,388,404` (the new `f32PinnedTensorName`/
  `visionTowerTensorName`/`gptqAWQAuxSuffix`/`gptqAWQPackFactor`); what the loaders keep f32:
  `decoder/gguf.go`'s `f32mat` closures (granite, nemotron, MLA), `decoder/weights.go:98-107`,
  `decoder/kda.go:16-26`; `TestFitEstimate_agreesWithResidentWeightBytes` runs only on the tiny
  dense qwen2 GGUF.
- **Failure:** Nemotron-H-8B's 24 Mamba layers ≈ 2.6 B params resident as f32 (10.5 GB) whichever
  quant is requested; the guard charges 1.6–3.3 GB; a 16 GB Mac with 10 GB available admits it and
  swaps — R3's founding scenario, now with a guard that says it fits. GPTQ/AWQ under-priced 8×
  (packed rows); a bundled SigLIP tower priced at 0.6–1.25 B/elem while loaded at f32 (P9(b)'s
  "tower priced automatically" is a 3–6× under-count).
- **Fix:** price by destination precision: charge tensors the loader keeps f32 at 4 B/elem;
  multiply `qweight` by the pack factor; extend the estimate-vs-accountant test to `nemotron-tiny`,
  `deepseek-tiny` and a GPTQ fixture.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), all three sub-fixes plus one correction to the audit's own
grouping: **KDA is dropped from the f32-pinned list.** `decoder/kda.go`'s dominant tensors
(`qProj/kProj/vProj/fProj/gProj/oProj`) are `linalg.WeightMat` loaded via the quantizing `mkQ`
helper (`decoder/weights.go`'s `loadBailingKDA`) — already priced correctly today by the ordinary
quant-rate formula; only its small 1-D vectors are f32, which is correct and immaterial. Including
KDA would have introduced a new over-pricing bug where none exists — confirmed by
`bailing_hybrid-tiny`'s own test case below staying within the same tight band as every other
fixture.

`f32PinnedTensorName` matches Mamba-2 (Granite's `mamba.` prefix, GGUF's `ssm_` prefix,
Nemotron-H's `mixer.in_proj`/`mixer.out_proj`/`mixer.conv1d` — never bare `mixer.`, which
Nemotron-H also uses for its ordinary quantizable attention/MLP/MoE layers) and MLA (GGUF
`attn_q_a`/`attn_q_b`/`attn_kv_a`/`attn_kv_b`, safetensors `q_a_proj`/`q_b_proj`/`kv_a_proj`/
`kv_b_proj`). **Known, documented residual gap, left unfixed rather than over-matched:** MLA's
output projection (`o_proj`/Bailing's `dense.weight`) is also f32-pinned, but those names are
ordinary, widely-used quantizable tensor names in every non-MLA family — matching them bare would
misclassify most of the registry, so this one term stays under-priced (confirmed small: DeepSeek's
own test case needed a 0.80 floor instead of the other three fixtures' 0.85, not a wide miss).
GPTQ/AWQ: `qweight`'s on-disk `[in/8, out]` packed shape is corrected by the pack factor (8)
before pricing; `qzeros`/`g_idx`/`scales` are excluded entirely (they are consumed during
reconstruction and never themselves kept resident, so pricing them as ordinary weight matrices
would double-count). Vision tower: `vision_tower.`/`multi_modal_projector.` tensors price at f32
(the tower's own default, invisible to this Config-only estimator) instead of the text quant.

Verified on this Mac: `TestFitEstimate_f32PinnedMixersAgreeWithResident` (`decoder/fitweights_test.go`)
extends the existing estimate-vs-accountant pattern to four REAL fixtures —
`testdata/deepseek-tiny` (MLA), `testdata/nemotron-tiny` and `testdata/granite-tiny` (Mamba-2),
`testdata/bailing_hybrid-tiny` (MLA+KDA, confirming KDA is NOT over-priced) — at int4 and
int8int8, all landing within the same 0.85–1.25 band (0.80 for deepseek, the documented o_proj
gap) the dense fixtures already use. `TestEstimateSafetensors_gptqPackFactor` and
`TestEstimateSafetensors_visionTowerPricedAtF32` (`decoder/fitgptq_test.go`) use a synthetic
checkpoint (`writeSafetensors`, an existing test helper) rather than a real GPTQ download, since
the estimator only reads tensor names and shapes, never dtype-specific values — a fake `.qweight`
tensor at the real packed shape exercises the identical code path a real one would. TDD-verified:
reverting each detection helper to `return false` (and the pack-factor multiply to a no-op)
reproduced the exact under-pricing this finding describes on every real fixture (nemotron 0.64/0.54,
deepseek 0.72/0.63, granite 0.85/0.79 — bailing correctly stayed passing, since KDA was never
miscounted) and both synthetic tests (gptq: 2822144 vs the fixed 20971520; vision: text-only
3932160 vs the fixed 9699328 including both vision tensors at f32).

#### M-30 · A safetensors checkpoint that does not fit is refused with a remedy that cannot work: `-stream-weights` is `.gguf`-only in serve and ignored by `decoder.Load` for a directory, and `cmd/prequant <dir>` re-runs the same guard
- **Where:** `decoder/fitguard.go:151-166` (`remedy`; stale "Only a .gguf can reach a refusal"),
  `:481-489` (P9b prices directories), `internal/serveapp/main.go:1230-1084,1102-1103` (transcode
  and auto-retry are `.gguf`-only), `decoder/model.go:387-380`, `internal/prequant/prequant.go:
  128-149` (`transcodeDir` = the resident load that just refused).
- **Failure:** `serve --model ~/models/gemma-3-12b-it` (the vision path is safetensors-only) on a
  16 GB Mac → refused with "Re-run goinfer-serve with -stream-weights" → identical refusal with the
  flag → `cmd/prequant` refuses too → the only exit is `GOINFER_NO_FIT_GUARD=1`, which the message
  offers as "if this machine really fits it". New with b7715ca.
- **Fix:** branch the remedy on the source; `transcodeDir` passes `DisableFit` (its output *is* the
  remedy); fix the `:129` comment.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), branching the remedy exactly as the doc's own first
sub-fix says — **not** the `transcodeDir`/`DisableFit` half, which turned out to rest on a wrong
premise: `Options.DisableFit` is never read inside `guardFit`/`fitCheckFor` at all (confirmed by
grep — it only gates `internal/serveapp`'s own decision to *attempt* a retry, and separately
feeds `resolveCtxCapFit`'s CUDA-specific fit-by-default logic). The guard's real, already-working
bypass is the `GOINFER_NO_FIT_GUARD=1` env var (`decoder/fitguard.go:227`, unconditional,
already respected by `transcodeDir`'s internal `decoder.Load` today with zero new plumbing) — so
the fix names that instead of inventing a redundant `Options` field for it.

Also traced why `transcodeDir` genuinely can't be `cmd/prequant`'s remedy for a checkpoint that
is *actually* too big, not just refused by the guard's conservative 70% margin:
`transcodeDir` loads the checkpoint fully resident to serialize it (unlike the `.gguf` path's
real one-layer-at-a-time streaming transcode), so it hits this exact guard for the exact same
reason and cannot help there. `remedy()` (`decoder/fitguard.go:151`) now branches on a new
`fitCheck.isGGUF` field: unchanged text for a `.gguf` source; for a safetensors directory, names
`GOINFER_NO_FIT_GUARD=1` as the working escape hatch for the margin-conservative case and
`cmd/prequant` as the real permanent-fix path once that variable lets its own internal load
through — not `-stream-weights`, which is a genuine no-op for a directory (`decoder.Load` ignores
it for anything but a `.giw`). `internal/serveapp/main.go` (~line 1205) also now warns instead of
silently no-op'ing when `-stream-weights` is explicitly passed for a directory source, naming
`cmd/prequant` instead of leaving the operator to wonder why nothing happened.

Verified with `TestFitGuard_remedyNamesPrequantNotStreamWeightsForDirectory`
(`decoder/fitguard_test.go`, real `testdata/internlm2-tiny` fixture, `injectHostRAM` forcing a
deterministic refusal): asserts the refusal does NOT contain the old "Re-run goinfer-serve with
-stream-weights" recommendation and DOES mention `GOINFER_NO_FIT_GUARD`, `cmd/prequant`, and
`safetensors`. TDD-verified: reverting `remedy()`'s branch reproduces the exact wrong
recommendation this finding describes. The existing `TestFitGuard_refusesBeforeAllocating`
(`.gguf` source) continues to pass unchanged, confirming the `.gguf` remedy text is untouched.

Incidental fallout caught and fixed in the same pass, unrelated to M-30 itself: fixing M-27's
GGUF router-quantization bug meant `testdata/glm-tiny.gguf`'s router genuinely stays f32 now
regardless of ambient quant, which makes an `int4`-requested load of it genuinely mixed-precision
— `decoder.(*Weights).quantLabel()`'s own documented contract correctly re-labels that bundle
`int4mix` instead of the old (buggy) uniform `int4`. Two pre-existing `internal/prequant` tests
(`TestGiwQuantLabel_headerAsymmetry`, `TestStreamTranscodeMatchesResident`) assumed the old,
uniform label and broke; updated both (`residentLabelFor`) to expect the now-correct `int4mix`
label for that one case, with a comment tracing it back to M-27.

#### M-31 · `cmd/prequant -embed-int4` is silently ignored for every GGUF input — the flag `serve` tells users to reach for
- **Where:** `internal/prequant/prequant.go:96-98` (`StreamTranscodeGGUF(…, quant, false, …)`),
  `cmd/prequant/main.go:30`, `internal/serveapp/main.go:1224-1068` (the note pointing at it).
- **Fix:** pass `embedInt4` through (one token); assert the head's kind in `TestStreamTranscode*`.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), exactly the doc's own fix: `internal/prequant/prequant.go`'s
`Transcode` GGUF branch now passes `embedInt4` (already an in-scope parameter) instead of a
hardcoded `false` to `decoder.StreamTranscodeGGUF`. The safetensors-directory branch
(`transcodeDir`) already threaded it correctly — only the GGUF branch was broken.

Verified with two tests in `internal/prequant/stream_test.go`:
`TestTranscode_embedInt4Threaded` calls `decoder.StreamTranscodeGGUF` directly at
`embedInt4=true/false` against `testdata/glm-tiny.gguf` and asserts the resulting bundles' embed
table kind differs (int8-pinned vs int4) — a sanity check on `StreamTranscodeGGUF` itself, which
was never broken. `TestTranscode_embedInt4ThreadedThroughRealGGUF`
(`GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS=1`-gated, real `~/models/qwen2.5-coder-0.5b-instruct-q4_k_m.gguf`, since
`glm-tiny.gguf` carries no embedded tokenizer and `Transcode`'s first phase requires one) drives
the actual regression surface — `Transcode` itself, matching what `cmd/prequant` calls — so a
future re-hardcoding at the `prequant.go` call site fails this test even if
`StreamTranscodeGGUF` stays correct. TDD-verified: reverting the fix left the direct-
`StreamTranscodeGGUF` test green (it never touches the broken line) while the real-`Transcode`
test failed exactly as the finding describes — `embedInt4=true` produced `kind=int8`, identical
to `embedInt4=false`. Restored, both tests pass.

#### M-33 · `transcodeDir` still writes the bundle in place — M-12 (09-02)'s temp+rename reached the GGUF branch only
- **Where:** `internal/prequant/prequant.go:154-172` vs `:91-119`.
- **Failure:** an OOM-kill during a safetensors→`.giw` prequant (the whole-model-resident path,
  exactly where the killer fires) leaves `out` with the placeholder header; `serve` then fails
  "truncated bundle" naming the `.giw`.
- **Fix:** the same `.tmp.giw` + rename (the `.giw` suffix matters for `selfCheck`, V-01).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). `transcodeDir` now mirrors `Transcode`'s GGUF branch
exactly: writes to `tmp := strings.TrimSuffix(out, ".giw") + ".tmp.giw"`, runs `selfCheck(tmp)`,
then `os.Rename(tmp, out)`; a failure at any step removes `tmp`, never touches `out`. The `.giw`
suffix on the temp name is preserved (V-01) so `selfCheck`'s `decoder.Load` still routes to the
bundle loader.

Verified structurally (the same technique the pre-existing `TestTranscode_writesViaTempThenRenames`
uses for the GGUF branch, since the interruption that matters — SIGKILL/OOM-kill/power loss —
can't be simulated in-process): `TestTranscodeDir_writesViaTempThenRenames`
(`internal/prequant/stream_test.go`) parses `transcodeDir`'s AST and asserts it never
`os.Create`s the final path directly and renames onto it exactly once.
`TestTranscodeDir_failedWriteLeavesNoFinalFile` drives a real failure (a bogus quant string against
the real `testdata/llama-tiny` fixture) and confirms no file — final or temp — is left behind.
TDD-verified: reverted the fix, confirmed the structural test fails red with the exact real
failure messages ("os.Creates the FINAL bundle path directly", "renames onto the final path 0
times"), restored, confirmed byte-identical and green. `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean; full
`internal/prequant` suite green.

#### M-34 · The expert pager is built and its budget banner printed for gpt-oss, Llama 4 and Nemotron 3 Nano, but none of their MoE loops ever calls `pager.touch` — the RAM bound is never enforced
- **Where:** `decoder/model.go:387-307`, `decoder/moepaging.go:19-21` (the hook list, stated as
  complete), `:96-110,139-143`; `decoder/forward_gptoss.go:166-187`, `decoder/forward_llama4.go:109-133`,
  `decoder/forward_nemotron.go:101-120` (experts read directly); the two that touch: `decoder/mlp.go:126-
  130`, `decoder/forward_gemma4_moe.go:112-116`.
- **Failure:** `serve --stream-weights --weight-cache 16 --model gpt-oss-120b.giw` prints "expert
  paging: … 16 GB budget" and serves every expert through demand page-faults with no WILLNEED/
  DONTNEED — the SpanCache LRU (measured −51 pp hit rate without it) is never consulted. R3 names
  `-stream-weights` as the remedy for exactly these families. Output stays bit-exact; the promise is
  wrong. (C-09-class bias-by-slot is NOT affected on CPU: `moepaging` never relocates.)
- **Fix:** `pager.touch(&lw.Experts[e])` in the three loops, or `newExpertPager` returns nil for
  unwired families and prints "ignored" (the N-13 shape `newLayerPager` has).
- **Prior:** sibling of N-13 (09-02).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). Took the doc's own first option: `gptOssMoE`,
`llama4MoE`, and `nemotronMoE` each gained `if m.pager != nil { m.pager.touch(unsafe.Pointer(&lw.Experts[e])) }`
inside their routed-expert loop, right where `moeMLP` (`decoder/mlp.go:126-130`) already does it —
same guard, same placement. All three are methods on `*Model`, so `m.pager` was already in scope;
no parameter threading needed.

Verified with a real, working `*expertPager` — not a mock — built by hand
(`decoder/moepaging_test.go`'s `newRealMmapPager`) over genuinely mmap'd, file-backed spans
(`SpanCache.Touch` issues real `MADV_WILLNEED`/`DONTNEED`, which would corrupt a heap-backed
slice, so this can't be faked with plain memory). One real complication found along the way:
`WeightMat.MappedSpan` only pages *quantized* (int8/int4) storage, and `mmap.PageAlignedInterior`
collapses a span to zero once page-rounding is applied — so even an int8-quantized tiny testdata
fixture's few-KB expert tensors can never survive real page-alignment (16 KiB pages on this Mac),
making `TestExpertPaging_bitExact`'s existing `GOINFER_MOE_GIW`-gated real-checkpoint requirement
non-optional for that specific end-to-end proof. This fix's own test instead drives each family's
real `*MoE` forward function directly (hand-built minimal `Architecture`/`LayerWeights`, the same
discipline `TestNemotron3NanoMoE_forward` already uses) with `m.pager` wired to the real mmap-backed
pager, keyed by exactly the `&lw.Experts[e]` addresses the fixed code now touches.
`TestExpertPaging_touchesEveryFamilyThatBuildsAPager` (`decoder/moepaging_test.go`), one subtest
per family, asserts `pager.stats()` shows nonzero hits+misses afterward. TDD-verified: reverted
all three `pager.touch` calls, confirmed all three subtests fail red with "pager.stats() = 0
touches" for each family, restored, confirmed byte-identical and green.

`go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean; full `decoder` MoE-related suite (`TestLlama4_textParity`,
`TestNemotron_textParity`, `TestNemotron3NanoMoE_textParity`, etc.) green — this change touches
only *whether* a page is faulted through the cache, never a computed value, confirmed by
`scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh`'s forward-goldens proof gating the accompanying deps_hash
refresh for gpt-oss/llama4_text/nemotron_h.

#### M-35 · Nemotron-H MoE block without a shared expert fails `BuildResident` on WebGPU with an unrelated error instead of matching the CPU's optional-shared-expert path
- **Where:** `gpu/residency.go:674-648` (unconditional `proj(&lw.SharedExpert.Up/Down)`),
  `decoder/forward_nemotron.go:125-118` (gated on `SharedIntermediateDim > 0`), `decoder/config.go:
  939-940` (`n_shared_experts == 0` accepted).
- **Failure:** "residency unsupported projection precision """ and the whole model to CPU; under
  `-require-be webgpu` the server refuses to start. Not on the two real checkpoints (both ship a
  shared expert); a config-space hole with the wrong stated reason.
- **Fix:** gate the two `proj` calls on `shInter > 0` (the generic branch at `:955` does).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), the doc's own suggested fix exactly: `gpu/residency.go`'s
Nemotron-H `case 3` (MoE) branch now wraps its two `proj(&lw.SharedExpert.Up/.Down)` calls in
`if shInter > 0`, mirroring the generic Mixtral/DeepSeek/GLM branch's own gate at `:964`. The
decode-time consumer (`gpu/decoderunner.go:1122`, `if lw.shUp != nil`) already existed —
`rl.shUp`/`rl.shDown` are a `decodeWeight` interface, whose nil zero-value is exactly what an
un-built shared expert now leaves behind, so no decode-time change was needed; that guard's own
comment ("always present: SharedIntermediateDim > 0 gates rl.shUp's build") was describing an
invariant that wasn't actually true until this fix, since the whole `BuildResident` call
previously failed first for the config it claimed to handle.

Verified two ways: (1) a real TDD red/green — reverted the gate, confirmed
`TestNemotronMoEResident_noSharedExpertBuilds` (new, `gpu/nemotron_moe_resident_test.go`) fails
with the exact real error the finding names ("residency unsupported projection precision \"\""),
restored, confirmed green; (2) the existing `TestNemotronMoEResidentParityWebGPU` (real
`n_shared_experts=1` checkpoint) stays green unchanged, confirming the fix doesn't touch the
already-working case. The new test derives its `n_shared_experts=0` fixture from the real
`testdata/nemotron3nano-tiny` checkpoint (config.json edited, `model.safetensors` symlinked
unchanged — the now-unreferenced `mixer.shared_experts.*` tensors are simply never looked up by
either loader once `SharedIntermediateDim` is 0, so no synthetic weights were needed) and asserts
a full resident forward pass runs without panicking on the newly-nil `shUp`/`shDown` fields, not
just that `Load` succeeds. `go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` (`-tags 'gpu goinfer_testhooks'`)
clean; broader `gpu` MoE/residency suite unaffected.


#### Chat templates, tokenizer, multimodal, demos

#### M-36 · Ministral 3 is served through the Mistral v0.3 renderer — `[INST] ` with a space, no `[SYSTEM_PROMPT]`, system folded into the last user turn — silently, on a family v0.17.0 announces; SmolLM3 and Olmo 3 get generic ChatML
- **Where:** `chat/chat.go:169-136` (`strings.Contains(t, "[INST]")` → `Mistral()`), `chat/
  chat/templates.go:239-254`, `chat/tools.go:214-256`; upstream `Ministral-3-8B-Instruct-2512`'s
  `chat_template.jinja` (fetched 2026-09-10): `[SYSTEM_PROMPT]{system}[/SYSTEM_PROMPT][INST]{user}
  [/INST]{assistant}</s>` with a default system message; SmolLM3 always emits a `## Metadata`
  system block; Olmo 3 uses `<functions>`/`<function_calls>` tool syntax, so `RenderTools`'s Hermes
  `<tool_call>` JSON is the wrong dialect (`ParseToolCalls` returns no calls).
- **Failure:** a different token stream in every turn, no error; tool calling diverges further.
  `chat/chat.go:2-5` lists the families with native renderers — none of the seven new families got
  one, a golden, or a `Detect` branch that declines.
- **Fix:** `Detect` refuses (or routes to a `mistral-v7` renderer) on `[SYSTEM_PROMPT]`;
  `sys_user`/`sys_multi` goldens for the new instruct checkpoints; document per family which
  renderer applies.
- **Confidence:** confirmed for Ministral 3 (template fetched; renderer read); plausible on the
  SmolLM3/Olmo 3 detail.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-16** (`main`). Per the user's own design decision (this session): fix
Ministral 3 for real, decline SmolLM3/Olmo 3 rather than guess. All three checkpoints' real
`chat_template.jinja`/`chat_template.json` were fetched and read live on 2026-09-16 — not just
Ministral 3's (already confirmed by the audit); SmolLM3's and Olmo 3's audit-hedged "plausible"
details are now **confirmed**, not just plausible:

- **Ministral 3** (`mistralai/Ministral-3-8B-Instruct-2512`): a new `Ministral()` renderer
  (`chat/templates.go`) matches the real template's confirmed shape — `<s>` once, an EXPLICIT
  system message as its own `[SYSTEM_PROMPT]{system}[/SYSTEM_PROMPT]` block, `[INST]{user}
  [/INST]` with **no** space (the audit's own citation), `{assistant}</s>` with no leading space
  either. `Detect` routes `[SYSTEM_PROMPT]` to it, checked before the existing `[INST]`-only
  `Mistral()` branch (same "more specific fingerprint first" discipline as Harmony-before-Gemma4).
  **Two deliberate scoping decisions, both documented in the renderer's own doc comment:**
  (1) the real template's DEFAULT system message (injected only when the caller supplies none at
  all — a long, Mistral-product-branded prompt naming "Ministral-3-8B-Instruct-2512" and "Le Chat"
  by identity, with live date substitutions) is **not** replicated — the same documented
  divergence `ChatML()` already has for Qwen's own default system message (this file's own
  "byte-exact" scope note), and for the same reason: injecting Mistral's own product identity into
  a self-hosted goinfer response would be actively wrong, not merely incomplete. (2) tool calling
  is **not** wired for this family (`SupportsTools()` returns false) — the real wire format
  (`[TOOL_CALLS]name[ARGS]{json}` per call, no JSON-array wrapping, discovered while reading the
  real template for this fix) differs from the existing `"mistral"` tool dialect (a JSON array of
  call objects) enough that reusing it would silently mis-render/mis-parse rather than simply be
  incomplete — declining is the honest choice until that dialect is built for real, a follow-up
  this note flags rather than guesses at.
- **SmolLM3** (`HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM3-3B`): confirmed live — uses plain `<|im_start|>`/`<|im_end|>`
  markers (matching ChatML's own `Detect` fingerprint) but always emits its own `"## Metadata\n\n"`
  system preamble the caller never asked for. `Detect` now declines (`"## Metadata"` fingerprint,
  checked before the generic `<|im_start|>` branch) rather than silently rendering it as plain
  ChatML.
- **Olmo 3** (`allenai/Olmo-3-7B-Instruct`): confirmed live — also uses plain `<|im_start|>`/
  `<|im_end|>` markers, but its tool syntax is `<functions>`/`<function_calls>` XML (confirmed
  exactly as the audit guessed), not ChatML/Qwen's Hermes `<tool_call>` JSON dialect — a
  tool-calling request would render/parse the wrong shape under generic ChatML. `Detect` now
  declines (`"<function_calls>"` fingerprint, checked before the generic branch too).

Verified with real TDD red/green throughout. `TestRender_goldens`/`TestDetect_fromTemplate`
(`chat/chat_test.go`) drive the new `testdata/chat_goldens/ministral.json` — which embeds the
REAL, full `chat_template.jinja` fetched live (not a hand-summarized excerpt) plus two hand-traced
`sys_user`/`sys_multi` cases computed directly from that template's own logic — proving both the
renderer's exact byte output AND that `Detect` routes the real template string to `"ministral"`.
`TestDetect_declinesSmolLM3AndOlmo3` (new) drives `Detect` against real substrings from each
checkpoint's own live-fetched template, asserting `ErrUnknownTemplate`, plus a control case
proving the two new fingerprints don't shadow ordinary ChatML checkpoints. Reverted each of the
three fixes independently, confirmed each fails red with the exact real wrong values (e.g.
`ministral/sys_user render mismatch: got: "...[INST] What is..." want: "...[INST]What is..."` —
the audit's own cited space bug, caught for real), restored, confirmed byte-identical and green.
`go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean; full `chat` package suite green, no regressions to any
other family's detection or rendering.

**Not done, flagged rather than silently deferred:** full Ministral 3 tool-calling parity (a new
`renderMinistralTools`/`parseMinistralTools` pair matching the real `[TOOL_CALLS]name[ARGS]{json}`
dialect) and SmolLM3/Olmo 3 real renderers (would need the same live-template-verification rigor
this fix used for Ministral 3, not a guess) are both real, separate follow-ups this closure note
surfaces rather than either silently declining forever or shipping an unverified guess.

#### M-38 · The image block is spliced with a different newline layout from the HF processors — Gemma 3 gets neither of its `\n\n` sentinels, Gemma 4 and Qwen get an extra `\n`
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:153,187,224` (`block + "\n"`),
  `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:343-335`, `multimodal/gemma3_block.go:114-116`, `multimodal/gemma4_block.go:27-29`, `multimodal/qwen_block.go:154-156`; transformers `processing_gemma3.py` (fetched): `full_image_sequence = "\n\n{boi}
  {soft*256}{eoi}\n\n"`; `processing_gemma4.py`: `{boi}{soft*n}{eoi}` with no newlines.
- **Failure:** Gemma's SPM vocab has `\n\n`/`\n\n\n` pieces, so the id streams differ around both
  sentinels on every image request. The decoder goldens hand-assemble `prefix+[boi]+[img]*n+[eoi]+
  suffix` (`scripts/pin_gemma3_real.py:63`, `scripts/pin_gemma3_vl_image.py:40`) and never run the
  processor's expansion, so the numerics are pinned while the prompt SHAPE the server sends is not.
- **Fix:** Gemma 3: `"\n\n" + block + "\n\n"`; Gemma 4/Qwen: drop the `"\n"`; pin one
  processor-produced id sequence per family.
- **Confidence:** confirmed for the upstream shapes; the Qwen template detail is plausible.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-16** (`main`), all three shapes re-verified live (not just re-read from
the audit's own citations) against the real, current upstream sources on 2026-09-16:
`transformers/models/gemma3/processing_gemma3.py`'s `__init__` (`f"\n\n{boi_token}{image_tokens}
{eoi_token}\n\n"`, both sides) and `transformers/models/gemma4/processing_gemma4.py`'s
`replace_image_token` (`f"{boi_token}{image_token}{eoi_token}"`, no newlines) confirm the audit's
own citations exactly. The Qwen detail — marked only "plausible" by the audit — is now **confirmed,
not just plausible**: `Qwen/Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct`'s live `chat_template.json` splices
`<|vision_start|><|image_pad|><|vision_end|>` inline with no adjacent newline on either side,
matching Gemma 4's shape, not the trailing `\n` this repo used to append.

`multimodal.Gemma3PromptBlock` (new, `multimodal/gemma3_block.go`) is the shared "\n\n"-wrapped
shape, called from both `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go`'s `visionPrompt` and
`demo/agent/agent/agent.go`'s vision path — one function, not two independent copies that could
drift again. `qwenVisionPrompt`/`gemma4VisionPrompt` now pass the bare `multimodal.QwenImageBlock`/
`Gemma4ImageBlock(n)` straight through with nothing concatenated.

Verified two ways. (1) `TestGemma3PromptBlock` (new, `multimodal/gemma3_block_test.go`) — a direct
unit test asserting the exact `"\n\n"+inner+"\n\n"` shape. (2)
`TestVision_imageBlockNewlinesMatchTheRealProcessors` (new,
`internal/serveapp/vision_hardening_test.go`) — since driving the real `*VisionPrompt` methods
needs a real vision tower this repo can't stand up in a unit test, this parses `vision_serve.go`'s
own AST and asserts `qwenVisionPrompt`'s and `gemma4VisionPrompt`'s `block :=` assignment is
*exactly* the bare `multimodal.QwenImageBlock(n)`/`Gemma4ImageBlock(n)` call, not a repo-wide grep
that couldn't tell one family's assignment from another's. TDD-verified: reverted both fixes
(re-added the `+ "\n"`), confirmed the structural test fails red naming the exact wrong expression
for each function, restored, confirmed byte-identical and green. `go build`/`go vet`/`staticcheck`
clean on every touched package (one pre-existing, unrelated `staticcheck` finding in
`demo/agent/agent/agent.go:518` confirmed present before this change via `git stash`, same as
M-41's own verification earlier this session); full `internal/serveapp`/`demo/agent`/`multimodal`
suites green.

now call `PreTokenizerDecline()`.


#### Release engineering

#### M-42 · `publish` runs when `embedded` fails, and nothing verifies the headline one-file assets reached the release
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/release-assets.yml:412-413` (`if: always() && needs.plain.result
  == 'success'`), `:464-489` (post-publish check derives URLs from README, which names only
  `goinfer-serve-darwin-arm64`), `README.md:98-101` (table promises `goinfer-chat-0.5b-*`/`-1.5b-*`).
- **Failure:** an HF fetch failure or the pinned-digest mismatch fails `embedded`; `publish`
  uploads the six runtime binaries and a `checksums.txt` covering only them; the URL check passes.
  The release is live without the "entire 1.5B LLM in one file" asset — v0.16.0's defect one row
  over. `--clobber` on a re-run fixes it silently, which is also how a re-run could replace assets
  months later.
- **Fix:** gate `publish` on `needs.embedded.result == 'success'` when a tier was requested; assert
  the release's asset list contains every name the README table promises.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), exactly the doc's own fix. `publish`'s condition (`:423`)
now reads `if: always() && needs.plain.result == 'success' && needs.embedded.result ==
'success'` — no `|| == 'skipped'` needed, confirmed by reading `embedded`'s own "skip 1.5B unless
asked" gate (`:300-312`): it is a STEP-level `if:`, not a job-level one, so every step in a
legitimately-skipped 1.5B matrix leg is individually skipped and none of them fail, meaning the
job itself still reports `'success'` (a job reports `'skipped'` only from a job-level `if:`,
which `embedded` doesn't have). A new step, "assert every embedded asset the README promises is
present" (`:454`), runs in `publish` after `collect + checksum` (once `embedded`'s artifacts are
actually in `dist/` — `plain`'s own pre-existing "assert serve assets exist" check runs too
early to see them) and before `upload`, checking all 6 platforms for both
`goinfer-chat-0.5b-*`/`-1.5b-*` — skipping the 1.5B half of the check only when
`workflow_dispatch`'s own `tier_1_5b: false` input was set, mirroring `embedded`'s own skip
condition exactly so the two can never disagree about when the tier is legitimately absent.

#### M-43 · `RELEASING.md` creates the GitHub Release after the tag push that triggers the upload that needs it
- **Where:** `RELEASING.md:85-99,105-128,130-148`; `.github/workflows/release-assets.yml:444-455`
  (`gh release upload` only, no create).
- **Fix:** `gh release view "$TAG" || gh release create "$TAG" --draft` in `publish`, or move
  "create the Release (draft)" before Step 1 and add "confirm N assets attached" as the closing step.
- **Confidence:** plausible (run history not visible).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), the doc's own first sub-fix — not `--draft` (per an
explicit user decision: auto-created releases publish live, non-draft, since the workflow's own
`--generate-notes` output is good enough to exist even before a human curates real notes).
Research upgraded the doc's own "Confidence: plausible (run history not visible)" — run history
**is** visible via `gh release view`/`gh run list`, and it showed the gap is real: v0.16.0's
release was created before its own workflow run finished, v0.17.0's after, with nothing enforcing
either order — pure operator tacit knowledge, not a documented or automated guarantee. A new step,
"ensure the GitHub Release exists" (`:484`), runs `gh release view "$TAG" || gh release create
"$TAG" --latest --title "$TAG" --generate-notes` immediately before `upload`, idempotent
regardless of whether a human already created the release by hand in either order.
`RELEASING.md`'s "GitHub Release (non-optional final step)" section now explains the workflow's
own auto-create step and gives the correct follow-up command for EACH case (`gh release edit` if
the workflow already created it — the common case — vs `gh release create` only if it genuinely
doesn't exist yet), since `gh release create` against a tag that already has a release errors
rather than updating it (verified via `gh release create --help`/`gh release edit --help` before
asserting this in the doc, not assumed).

Verified via a real `workflow_dispatch` dry run (`gh run 35044785648`, `tier_1_5b=false`) against
the existing `v0.18.0` tag — chosen specifically because that release currently has zero assets
(an unrelated Windows cross-compile break, already fixed in code but not yet in any release), so
there was nothing real to clobber, and nothing was: `plain` failed (see below), so `publish` never
ran and touched no assets. `v0.18.0`'s tag is pinned to the commit BEFORE the Windows fix, so
`plain` failed there exactly as expected, on the exact pre-existing `syscall.SIGUSR1`/`SIGUSR2`
error — but this run still validated real, concrete things about the fix, not nothing: (1)
`embedded` reported `success` for BOTH matrix legs, including the `tier_1_5b=false`-skipped 1.5B
one (completed in 6s, confirming the step-level-skip-still-reports-job-success reasoning the fix
depends on, empirically, not just by reading GHA's docs); (2) `publish`'s new `if:` correctly
evaluated to false and SKIPPED cleanly (no expression error, no crash) the moment `plain` failed —
proving `needs.embedded.result` is a valid, resolvable reference (ruling out the "wrong job name
silently evaluates false" trap research flagged) and that the gating logic behaves correctly on
the failure path. What this run could NOT exercise: `publish`'s own new steps (the embedded-asset
assertion, the release-create step) — never reached, since `publish` correctly skipped before
them. Full end-to-end verification of those two steps happens on the next real release.

#### M-45 · `readme-smoke` checks the README at HEAD against the binary at the previous tag, in both directions
- **Where:** `scripts/readme_smoke.sh:55-86` (`@latest`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml:189-202`,
  `CHANGELOG.md:292-293` ("currently red … by design, until this tag lands").
- **Failure:** a README line for a flag added at HEAD reads red until the tag (a gate red by
  design is one that gets ignored); a flag removed at HEAD reads green until publish, then red.
- **Fix:** `go install ./cmd/serve` from the checkout on PR/push runs; keep `@latest` on the
  tag-triggered run.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), with a scope correction per an explicit user decision:
`readme-smoke` **always** tests HEAD now, on every trigger — there is no tag-triggered variant of
this CI job to preserve `@latest` behavior for (`ci.yml`'s `readme-smoke` job has no `tags:`
trigger at all), and the tag/release case is already covered by `release-assets.yml`'s own
post-publish README-URL check (M-42's own citation above), making a separate `@latest` variant
here redundant rather than a gap. The smoke-help flag-parity check now builds `serve` from the
actual checkout (`$ROOT`, `go build -o "$GOBIN/serve" ./cmd/serve`) and overwrites whatever
`$GOBIN/serve` the README's own `<!-- smoke -->`-marked `@latest` install line produced —
that `@latest` install itself is UNCHANGED and still runs (it's still a real, valuable check
that the currently-published module installs cleanly), only the binary the flag-parity check
tests against was ever the bug. Native `linux/amd64` only, matching the `root` CI job's own plain
`go build ./...`, deliberately not a cross-compile matrix — a platform-specific compile break
belongs to a different gate's job.

**Bonus fix, found while verifying this one, not in the original finding**: reproduced a genuine,
pre-existing SIGPIPE/`pipefail` race in the (untouched-until-now) flag-checking loop —
`grep -q` exits the instant it finds a match, and since `set -uo pipefail` is set (`:21`), if that
happens early in a long `--help` dump the still-writing `serve --help` producer can get SIGPIPE'd,
and ITS non-zero exit status becomes the pipeline's reported status even though `grep` itself
matched. Reproduced deterministically with `--backend` (alphabetically early, so `grep -q` returns
almost immediately) reporting "FLAG NOT IN --help" on every run despite genuinely being present;
flags later in the alphabet (`-model`, `-stream-weights`) never hit it, because by the time `grep`
found them the producer had already finished writing — explaining why this was never noticed
before. Confirmed present in the pre-fix script too (same failure reproduces against `git show
HEAD:scripts/readme_smoke.sh` before this commit), so it is unrelated to the HEAD-vs-`@latest`
change itself, not something this fix introduced. Fixed by capturing `--help`'s output to a file
ONCE (`$WORK/serve-help.txt`) before the per-flag loop instead of re-piping a fresh `serve --help`
into `grep -q` on every iteration — grepping a file has no producer process to SIGPIPE, and it is
also simply cheaper (one process spawn instead of one per flag).

Verified on this Mac (`/opt/homebrew/bin/bash scripts/readme_smoke.sh README.md` — the system
`/bin/bash` is 3.2, too old for this script's own `mapfile` usage, a pre-existing requirement
unrelated to this fix): confirmed the flag-parity check now genuinely tests a HEAD-built binary
(a deliberately-introduced test flag absent from the last published `@latest` was detected
correctly), confirmed the SIGPIPE false-failure is gone, and confirmed the script's remaining
failures (`goinfer-chat`-registry / HF-API resolution errors in the unrelated `<!-- smoke-model
-->` section) are pre-existing and unaffected by this change — reproduced identically against the
pre-fix script, not introduced by it, and out of scope for M-45.

#### M-46 · The WebGPU backend links an unpinned wgpu-native snapshot of unknown revision from ≤ May 2025
- **Where:** `gpu/go.mod:6` (`cogentcore/webgpu v0.23.0`, pkg.go.dev 2025-05-06, only version);
  the binding's `.github/workflows/build-wgpu.yml` (fetched 2026-09-10) builds `libwgpu_native.a`
  with `git clone --recursive https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu-native.git` — no tag; wgpu-native has
  since tagged v25.0.2.2 … v29.0.1.1 (2026-06-23); `docs/webgpu-primer.md:115-116`.
- **Failure:** nothing records which naga validated the shipped WGSL, so a naga behaviour or
  security fix cannot be assessed as applying or not. Provenance, not a known CVE (RustSec was
  unreachable; search found none — "none found", not "none exist").
- **Fix:** probe the bundled archive for its version string and record it in the primer; queue a
  re-pin against a tagged wgpu-native with the WebGPU parity group re-run.
- **Confidence:** plausible (the exact bundled revision could not be read).
- **CLOSED 2026-09-12, by a migration this finding didn't motivate.** `a16a537d` replaced the
  unmaintained `cogentcore/webgpu v0.23.0` with `oliverbestmann/webgpu v1.36.0`, which pins
  `wgpu-native v29.0.1.1` (naga 29.0.1) — a real, known, tagged version, not an unpinned snapshot;
  confirmed still current in `gpu/go.mod`. The migration's own motivation was `dot4I8Packed`/
  `dot4U8Packed` WGSL builtin support (unblocking `task-gpu-batched-prefill.md`), not this finding
  — but it satisfies M-46's actual ask regardless: the provenance gap is closed, the WebGPU parity
  group was re-run (78 PASS / 20 SKIP / 0 FAIL, matching the pre-migration baseline).
  `docs/webgpu-primer.md` was not updated by that migration and still named `cogentcore/webgpu
  v0.23.0` in ten places as of this note; fixed 2026-09-13 (all ten references updated to
  `oliverbestmann/webgpu v1.36.0` / `wgpu-native v29.0.1.1`, plus a short migration note explaining
  the switch and citing this finding).


#### Documentation that misleads on a Hard-tier surface

#### M-48 · `--exact-prefill` promises "BIT-EXACT prompt ingestion on ALL backends" but never touches CUDA's default-ON tensor-core prefill
- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/main.go:353` (help), `:510-483` (sets `GOINFER_METAL_FAST_PREFILL`
  only), `:496-500` (CPU), `cuda/prefill.go:736-739` (`GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL` unset → both
  levers above 512); `docs/completed/task-prefill-gap.md:35-36,66-68,183-184` ("when it lands,
  `--exact-prefill` maps to `GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL=0` on this backend" — never wired).
- **Fix:** `os.Setenv("GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL", "0")` in the same block; say so in the help and
  `env-vars.md:62`.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), exactly the doc's own fix. `internal/serveapp/main.go`'s
exact-prefill env-setting block was factored into a new pure function, `applyExactPrefillEnv(cfg
config)` (`:365`), called once from `Main()` right after `flag.Parse()` — the block itself was
inline in `Main()` before, which isn't unit-testable (`os.Args`, `os.Exit`, starts a server), and
had zero test coverage even for its pre-existing Metal/CPU wiring. The new function adds
`os.Setenv("GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL", "0")` alongside the Metal/CPU calls, unset otherwise
(matching Metal's own pattern — unset means default-on to CUDA's own env-reading code, no
separate CUDA-specific flag exists to need an explicit "1" branch the way CPU's does).
`exactPrefillHelp` (`:353`) now names CUDA explicitly instead of implying universal coverage
while describing only two backends. `docs/env-vars.md`'s `GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL` row appends
"Server flag: `--exact-prefill` sets this to `0`." — the same sentence shape the Metal row
already used.

Verified on this Mac: `TestApplyExactPrefillEnv_coversAllThreeBackends` and
`TestApplyExactPrefillEnv_defaultLeavesMetalAndCUDAUnset` (`internal/serveapp/exactprefill_test.go`)
drive the new pure function directly with `t.Setenv`/`os.Unsetenv`, asserting all three env vars
(not just the new CUDA one — this closes the coverage gap for Metal/CPU too). TDD-verified:
reverting just the new CUDA `os.Setenv` call reproduces the exact failure this finding
describes (`GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL` stays unset with `--exact-prefill` passed). **Not
verified here, honestly**: whether `GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL=0` genuinely reverts CUDA to the
bit-exact `attn_batched`/`gemv_w4a8_rn` kernels — that numerics claim already has its own gate
coverage in `cuda/prefill_gate_ref_test.go`, which needs real CUDA hardware (nobara) and was not
re-run as part of this fix, since the fix itself is pure env-var wiring on the CPU/serveapp side
and does not touch `cuda/prefill.go`'s own env-var-reading logic at all.

#### M-54 · `docs/multimodal.md`'s status header and `docs/server.md`'s vision paragraph describe the June state; the same doc's body says most of it is superseded
- **Where:** `docs/multimodal.md:3-13` ("one family (Gemma 3)", "Qwen2.5-VL … text side only",
  "~171 s/image", "the cgo-free release binaries have no GPU vision at all", "Nothing multimodal is
  in `serve check`, the fit guard, the recommendation registry") vs `:56-149` (gap 0 RESOLVED),
  `:157-176` (P6a/CUDA), `:229-232` (P7); `docs/server.md:151-159` ("With a Gemma 3 VL
  checkpoint", "~3 min/image", "an int8 tower is the planned speedup" — `-vision-quant int8`
  shipped and was measured a wash, `internal/serveapp/main.go:488`); `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:118-134,
  169,206` (three families served).
- **Fix:** rewrite the header from the body (three families, per-backend table); replace the
  server.md paragraph with the current family list, `-vision-quant` and the §A/§B tower numbers.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). `docs/multimodal.md:3-17` rewritten from the current
reality confirmed against `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go`: three families (Gemma 3, Qwen2.5-VL
— image path now real, not text-only — Gemma 4), a full per-backend breakdown (CPU all three;
CUDA Gemma 3 tower resident as of *this session's own M-18*, 1.58×, plus GPU-resident decode for
Gemma 3/Qwen2.5-VL; WebGPU Gemma 3 tower resident; Metal no tower for any family yet), and the CPU
tower cost corrected from the stale "~171 s/image" to the current measured ~31.3 s
(`docs/benchmarks.md` §A). `docs/server.md`'s vision paragraph (its family-list sentence was
already fixed by an unrelated commit, `b1bfb4b8` — left untouched) had its stale caveat sentence
replaced: `-vision-quant` is shipped, not planned (default `f32`; `int8` only helps on
AVX512-VNNI, a wash on AVX2); `docs/completed/task-cpu-vision-prefill.md` is archived, not a
future task; current costs ~31.3s CPU / 26.1s CUDA-resident cited directly.

Verified by direct comparison against the current source files cited above — this is a docs-only
fix with nothing to build or test; citation-lint (below) confirms every path:line reference in the
rewritten prose still resolves.

#### M-55 · `server.md`'s prefix-reuse exactness promise names the CPU knob only; on CUDA/Metal resident reuse the suffix goes through the default-ON fast kernels, and resident reuse itself is not on the page
- **Where:** `docs/server.md:166-179` ("Reuse is exact … under `GOINFER_CPU_FAST_ATTENTION=0`"),
  `decoder/resident_reuse.go:5-27`, `cuda/prefill.go:736-739`, `metal/backend.go:503-359`.
- **Fix:** one sentence naming resident prefix reuse and the per-backend opt-out
  (`--exact-prefill` for Metal/CPU; `GOINFER_CUDA_FAST_PREFILL=0` for CUDA until M-48 lands).

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), exactly the doc's own fix, plus M-48 landed in the same
pass so `--exact-prefill` genuinely covers CUDA now rather than needing the "until M-48 lands"
hedge. `docs/server.md` gained a new paragraph (`:214-222`, "GPU-resident models reuse prefixes
too, by a different mechanism") naming resident prefix reuse (`decoder/resident_reuse.go`,
`prefill_reused_tokens`) and its per-backend opt-out: Metal and CUDA both via `--exact-prefill`;
WebGPU has no fast/exact split to opt out of.

**Bonus fix, found during verification, not in the original finding**: the dsh section
(`:266-278`) contained a factually wrong claim — added by a different, unrelated commit
(`b1bfb4b8`, 2026-09-11) — that GPU-resident models take a stateless decode path with "no
cross-turn prefix reuse at all," reprefilling the whole history every turn. False: resident
prefix reuse (shipped 2026-09-02, predates that claim) means only the divergent suffix reprefills.
Corrected in the same pass since both paragraphs describe the identical mechanism inconsistently
with each other.

Verified by direct comparison against `decoder/resident_reuse.go`, `cuda/prefill.go`, and
`metal/backend.go` — docs-only, nothing to build or test.

#### M-58 · The published primer (`docs/book/`) quotes figures its own cited source has withdrawn or superseded
- **Where:** `docs/book/10-kernels-and-backends.md:195-197` ("about 1.7× at 0.5B … parity at
  1.5B") vs `docs/benchmarks.md:58-60` ("both figures are withdrawn; §B8 measured 332.7 vs
  268.7"); `docs/book/08-prefill-versus-decode.md:50-55,236` (3,020 tokens: 101.6 s, "3× more time
  for 2× tokens") vs `benchmarks.md:42,429` (38.9 s; "the rate no longer falls with length");
  `docs/book/07-mixture-of-experts.md:71,157` ("10.74–15.69 tok/s" — the June record; current peer
  row 23.5). `README.md:338-341` sends new readers here first; it is rendered online. ch. 4, 5, 6,
  9, 11 spot-checked and holding.
- **Fix:** re-derive from the current rows, or label "as of <date>, superseded — see …" the way
  benchmarks.md itself does.

**Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`), all three re-derived from `docs/benchmarks.md`'s current
rows. `docs/book/10-kernels-and-backends.md`: "1.7× at 0.5B... parity at 1.5B" (both withdrawn) →
current short-context CUDA figures (0.5B 1.24×, 1.5B 1.13×, parity at 7B), preserving the
withdrawal note and the doc's own long-context-inversion caveat rather than just swapping numbers.
`docs/book/08-prefill-versus-decode.md`: the whole 1-vs-6-worker table and its "3× time for 2×
tokens" superlinear-scaling narrative — the book's own teaching example — were re-derived from the
current `docs/benchmarks.md` numbers (3,020 tokens now 38.9s, 8.61× faster than the pre-2026-09-01
record, rate now flat rather than falling), reframing the lesson honestly: the O(K²) attention
cost is still real, but a well-optimized kernel keeps it from dominating within this practical
range; two further citations of the stale 101.6s figure (the "What it costs" worked example and a
stray "4-5× prefill gap" reference actually describing the CUDA lane, not this chapter's CPU one)
were corrected too, the latter to the current 1.9-3.2× CUDA figure. `docs/book/07-mixture-of-
experts.md`: "10.74-15.69 tok/s" (a June record whose own cited source, `docs/deltanet-residency-
plan.md`, has since been archived to `docs/completed/` with no current figures of its own) → the
current M35 peer-comparison row, 23.5 tok/s, both citations (lines ~71 and ~159).

Verified by direct comparison against `docs/benchmarks.md`'s current tables — docs-only, nothing
to build or test. Chapters 4/5/6/9/11 were reported as already holding by the audit and were not
re-checked here (out of this finding's cited scope).

#### Decoder core and lifecycle

  inserted `prefillDeclineOnce` block; the orphaned half ("is positional. from == 0 is the cold
  path.") sits above the func.

  comment and removed it from `prefillDeclineDigitsRE`'s var declaration, where it had landed by
  mistake. Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-02** `decoder/model.go:1277-1142` — `generateInto`'s comment still says prefix reuse keeps the
  CPU path and batched on-device prefill "is deferred"; the G3 paragraph below it is current.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Fixing this accurately requires reconciling the
  comment against this session's own M-42/M-45/M-48/M-55 resident-generation work rather than a
  batch-nit rewrite; pulled out of Batch A to avoid a rushed, possibly-wrong doc edit.
- **N-03** `decoder/residency.go:35-40` — "v1 scope … stateless Model.Generate only … prompt prefill
  runs on the CPU … only decode runs on the GPU": every clause is false today.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review**, same reasoning as N-02 — the two findings describe the
  same stale-architecture-claim class and should be corrected together against current resident
  behavior, not mechanically in a comment-fix batch.
- **N-04** `decoder/config.go:325-329` — "GenerateGemma4VL implements ONLY the causal (E2B/E4B) case;
  a checkpoint reporting a non-empty value here must be refused" — P7 Phases C/D serve the
  "vision" case; the sentence tells the next reader to add a refusal that would break it.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Rewrote the comment to say the "vision" case (26B-A4B/31B) IS
  served, via `prefillLogitsGemma4VLBidirectional`/`runLayersGemma4FromEmbedN`
  (`decoder/forward_gemma4_batched.go`), confirmed against `generate_gemma4_vl.go`'s own doc
  comment. Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-05** `decoder/model.go:630,519` — two hand-listed family exclusions (`kvI8`, rings) remain in
  `NewCache` after the chokepoint work; not wrong today, the shape the 09-02 audit named.
  **Status: No action needed.** The finding's own text concedes this is "not wrong today" — a
  shape note, not a defect. No code change.
- **N-06** `decoder/features.go:126-132` — `FeatKDA` says "a delta-rule recurrence structurally
  identical to Gated DeltaNet" while the dispatch table marks the family non-recurrent (C-03); the
