  already matches the dispatch table — `bailing_hybrid`'s `ownForwardFamily` entry (`decoder/arch.go`)
  carries `Recurrent: true`. The finding's premise doesn't match current code; no change made.
- **N-07** `decoder/weightmat.go:839,493` vs `:467-473` — `matmulInto` sets the per-stream
  `Workspace` threshold in the Q8 and int4 branches, never in the W8A8 branch, so after an int4
  projection the next W8A8 matmul on the same stream (int8-pinned head, int4mix attention) runs at
  `int4ParThreshold` (1<<20) instead of 300K. Bit-identical by aikit's contract; fan-out only.
  **Status: No action needed.** Re-verified directly: `decoder/weightmat.go:839-840` already calls
  `ws.SetThreshold(DefaultDecodeParallelThreshold)` ahead of the W8A8 matmul. Already fixed

- **N-08** `decoder/kda_rehearsal.go:6-8` — "NOT wired to any registered family" is stale since
  e6b31cc; `kdaMixerStep` calls both functions on the served path.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Rewrote the header comment: `kdaMixerStep` (`decoder/kda.go`),
  called from `forward_bailing.go`'s served path, now reuses `kdaLowerBoundGate`/`kdaRecurrentStep`

- **N-09** `decoder/deltanet_chunked.go:89-87,115` — the chunked scan ignores `p.NegEigval` (β×2) and
  `p.ONormEps`, so for Olmo Hybrid it is not "algebraically equivalent to gatedDeltaNetStep"; unwired,

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Appended a warning to the header comment citing the exact

- **N-10** `decoder/spec_ngram.go:180`, `decoder/forwardn.go:114-117` — the refusal string names
  "(Mamba-2 / Gated DeltaNet)"; the predicate also covers LFM2 conv (and should cover KDA).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `hasRecurrentState` (`decoder/forwardn.go`) is a
  generic predicate over the dispatch table's `Recurrent` bit, genuinely covering LFM2's conv
  window and KDA (bailing_hybrid) alongside Mamba-2/Gated DeltaNet. Reworded
  `specRollbackSafe`'s doc comment and `spec_ngram.go`'s refusal string to name all four rather
  than implying only two are covered. Comment/string-only; no behavior change.
- **N-11** `docs/audit-2026-09-02.md:1406-1411` (M-11 disposition) — the deferred list `mla.*,
  mamba.*, delta.*, qattn.*` predates v9; `kda.*` (13 tensors, none length-checked in
  `validateShapes`) belongs on it.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `kdaWeights` (`decoder/kda.go`) has exactly 13 tensor
  fields and that `validateShapes` (`decoder/serialize.go`) checks none of them, same gap as the
  other four sub-structs already on the deferred list. Added `kda.*` to
  `docs/completed/audit-2026-09-02.md`'s M-11 deferred-list note. Doc-only; no behavior change.
- **N-12** `decoder/kvcache.go:113-116` — the MLA comment ("cache the low-rank latent … not a
  reconstructed full K+V") is contradicted by `NewKVCache` reserving exactly that full-width
  capacity for every layer (P-02).
  **Status: No action needed — already fixed.** This session's own earlier P-02 work
  (`decoder/model.go`'s `NewCache`, `kvCapHint = 0` when `a.mla != nil`) already stopped
  `NewKVCache` from reserving full-width `keys`/`vals` capacity on MLA layers; the comment at
  `decoder/kvcache.go:113-116` is accurate again. The finding predates that fix landing; no further
  change made.
- **N-13** `decoder/generate_vl.go:103-100,263-267` — on a P9a fast-path prefill error the turn
  returns `g.err` without trying the cold path; every other decline in these functions falls through.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed accurate at both call sites
  (`GenerateVL`'s `residentPrefillSeed` and `GenerateQwenVL`'s `residentPrefillSeedMRoPE`): an
  error from either aborts the whole generation via `g.err = err; return`, unlike every other
  decline in the same functions (busy, cap check, not-fully-reused), which release the resident
  claim and fall through to the tower + CPU prefill path instead. This is real behavior, not a
  comment nit — the fix needs to reason about resident-claim/cache-state safety on a
  fall-through-after-partial-write path and deserves its own TDD-driven fix rather than a batch
  mechanical change. Pulled out of Batch A for individual review.


#### Sampling and speculation

- **N-14** `decoder/sampler.go:277-279` vs `:289` — with `Logprobs` set and no filters the draw is
  `drawFull` over a sequential softmax; without it, `sampleChunked` over the 64-chunk fold. Same
  seed, same logits, ULP-different `Z`, so toggling `logprobs:true` can move a boundary draw.
  `decoder/spec_sample_test.go:81-85` still says plain `Generate` is `drawFull` — stale since P2b.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Corrected the test's doc comment: plain `Generate` under pure
  temperature with no `Logprobs` goes through `sampleChunked`, not `drawFull` — `drawFull` only
  fires on the `Logprobs` branch. The test's actual assertion (same seed → same first token) was
  already correct since both compared paths use the same function; only the comment's naming was
  stale. Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-15** `decoder/blockspec.go:373-364`, `decoder/blockdrafter.go:25-47` — `NewBlockSpec` accepts
  drafters with `HasOwnHead()` (poolside Laguna DFlash) or DSpark (own head, predicts from slot 0)
  and then drafts through the target's embedding/head/slot-1 — silent acceptance collapse, the
  failure class `decoder/dflash.go:92-96` names. Unreachable in `serve` only because Laguna declines residency.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed real: `BlockDrafterWeights` (the interface
  `NewBlockSpec` accepts) has no way to expose `HasOwnHead()` to the resident path at all, so a
  library caller passing an own-head drafter gets silent acceptance collapse rather than a
  refusal. A correct fix needs either a refusal at `NewBlockSpec` or a real interface addition,
  plus a test proving the refusal fires — real logic risk, not a batch-safe nit.
- **N-16** `decoder/blockspec.go:332-323` — the acceptance-guard fallback picks
  `m.resident.(ResidentGreedy)` whenever the MODEL has a resident, not when the HOST is it; a
  `NewCPUBlockSpec` on a resident-capable model would decode its tail from a device KV holding a
  different sequence. Unwired by design; the predicate should be `host == m.resident`.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed real and confirmed the suggested one-line
  fix (`host.(ResidentGreedy)` instead of `m.resident.(ResidentGreedy)`) is behavior-preserving for
  the existing GPU path (`host` and `m.resident` are the same underlying object there) while
  correctly resolving false for `NewCPUBlockSpec`'s CPU host. Held back anyway: proving it via a
  real regression test needs a mock `ResidentGreedy` resident plus a mock drafter/verify flow to
  reach the `guard.stopped` fallback branch — more scaffolding than this batch's comment-only
  fixes, and a state-corruption bug deserves that proof before shipping the fix.
- **N-17** `decoder/blockspec.go:309-296,351-353` — with `MaxTokens` unset (library) the fallback
  branch has no cap clamp (`ForwardArgmax` at `pos == ctxCap` is refused → error); a width-1 round
  runs the whole drafter to verify the anchor alone.
  **Status: No action needed — already fixed.** `blockSpecRoundWidth` (`decoder/blockspec.go`,
  landed by M-13's earlier fix, commit `b3ad71f4`) clamps by `ctxCap` unconditionally — the clamp
  does not depend on `MaxTokens` being set. The finding's premise (no clamp when `MaxTokens` is
  unset) doesn't match current code; no change made.
- **N-18** `decoder/blockspec.go:574-544` — `Accepted = len(toks) − rounds − 1` counts tokens the
  guard's plain-decode fallback emitted as accepted drafts; telemetry, but it is what the response
  metadata reports.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed real: the `guard.stopped` fallback branch
  (`decoder/blockspec.go`) never increments `rounds`, so every fallback-emitted token inflates
  `GenerateStream`'s `stats.Accepted` as if it were a confirmed draft. Output is still lossless —
  this is a telemetry-accuracy bug, not a correctness one — but fixing it correctly means
  threading a fallback-token count through `generate()`'s signature or callback and adding a test
  that pins the corrected count, which is more than a comment nit.
- **N-19** `decoder/eagle.go:150-164` — `d2t` is not range-checked against `vocab` (DFlash's loader
  is, `decoder/dflash.go:390-396`); library-only.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Ported DFlash's exact bounds check (`decoder/dflash.go:390-396`,
  same `i + int(off)` target-id convention `EagleHead.TargetID` also uses) into
  `LoadEagleHead`: an out-of-range `d2t` entry now fails to load with a clear error instead of
  indexing the target's embedding out of bounds at verify time. Neither loader has an isolated
  unit test for this check (dflash.go's own predates this session); both are exercised implicitly
  by any real-checkpoint EAGLE/DFlash load, so no new heavy-checkpoint test scaffold was added.
- **N-20** `decoder/fidelity_testhook.go:157-165` — the near-tie band is 3% of the reference's FULL
  logit range (~2 nats on Qwen): a flip between probabilities 0.85 and 0.11 is excluded as
  "near-tie". The tree's established rule, but stated in logit-range units where the gates are read.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Added a paragraph to `NearTieArgmaxForTest`'s doc comment making
  the real-world magnitude explicit (a 2-nat Qwen gap ≈ a 0.85/0.11 probability split), so a
  reader doesn't mistake "3%" for a tight tolerance. Did NOT change the threshold itself — it's
  the same rule used inline by `cuda/realforward_test.go` and `gpu/kv_i8_parity_test.go`, and
  moving it would need to happen in all three places at once, which is out of scope for a
  documentation fix. Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-21** `decoder/mtp.go:35,229` — `MTPHead.normEps` is loaded and never read.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed real: `MTPStep` uses `arch.NormEps` (the
  target's own epsilon) for every norm in the MTP head's forward, never `h.normEps` (loaded from
  the head's own `rms_norm_eps` config field). This may be intentional — the head's doc comment
  says it reuses the target's own attention/MLP blocks "not a second copy of them," so a shared
  epsilon could be by design — or it may be a real bug if a trained MTP head's own epsilon ever
  differs from its target's. Deciding needs checking a real MTP checkpoint's `rms_norm_eps`
  against its target's, which risks changing decode-critical speculative-drafting numerics if
  gotten wrong — not a call to make inside a nit batch.


#### Serving

- **N-22** `internal/serveapp/openai.go:912-680,1000-1006` — both comments say adapters "route to  the staged/CPU session path"; false since G3 (the wrong premise behind M-01).
  **Status: No action needed.** Both cited comments already carry the G3-aware correction ("Since
  G3 … the session path itself is NOT always staged/CPU for an adapter", and "N-39: not purely
  staged since G3"), fixed elsewhere and unannotated. The finding's premise doesn't match current
  code; no change made.
- **N-23** `internal/serveapp/webui.go:429-185` — `handleWebPull` writes SSE with `fmt.Fprintf` +
  `Flush` directly while `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:215-208` says `frame` "is the ONLY place this package writes to
  a streaming ResponseWriter"; single writer, but no write deadline — a tab that stops reading pins
  the single-flight `pullState` and the download.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Extracted `sseJSONSender` (`internal/serveapp/helpers.go`), a
  small helper that wraps `sseWriter.frame` (the same write-deadline mechanism `frame`'s other
  callers already use) and calls a `context.CancelFunc` on a failed write. `handleWebPull` now
  derives its own cancellable context from `r.Context()` and uses `sseJSONSender` instead of a
  raw `fmt.Fprintf`+`Flush` — a stalled-but-open reader now trips the write deadline and cancels
  the download, the same way an outright closed tab already did via `r.Context()`. TDD: new
  `TestSSEJSONSender_cancelsOnWriteFailure` (`internal/serveapp/sse_writer_test.go`) confirmed red
  against the un-wired version, green after.
- **N-24** `internal/serveapp/anthropic_stream.go:62-78`, `internal/serveapp/openai.go:699-559`, `vision_serve.go:
  285-290,376-386` — nothing is sent between the opener and the first token: on CPU that window is
  the whole prefill (an 8k agent prompt ≈ 270 s; an image ≈ minutes) against 300 s harness idle
  timeouts. Two lines of `sseHeartbeat` per site; the M-19 gate scans `lm.drive(` only and never
  enumerates the `driveVL` sites.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16, wider than cited.** Reading every `lm.drive(`/`lm.driveVL(` call
  site directly (rather than trusting either the audit's citations or the M-19 gate's own
  coverage) found SIX sites missing a heartbeat, not the three named: `serveChatText` and
  `serveCompletion` (`openai.go`, `drive` — chat-completions and legacy completions streaming),
  `streamMessages`'s plain-text branch (`anthropic_stream.go`, `drive`), `serveVisionChatWith` and
  `serveVisionMessages` (`vision_serve.go`, `driveVL`), and `serveResponsesWith`'s plain-text
  branch (`responses.go`, `drive` — not cited by the audit at all). Each now wraps its
  `lm.drive`/`lm.driveVL` call with `sseHeartbeat`/`stopBeat()`, the exact pattern already proven
  at `streamMessagesTools`/`responses.go`'s tools branch/`tools.go`. Did NOT widen the existing
  M-19 gate (`TestSSE_everyBufferedStreamSiteHeartbeats`) — its buffer-then-stream classifier
  (`driveCallback`/`unconditionalStreamCall`) is deliberately narrower than this gap (a per-token
  streaming site is silent during PREFILL regardless of whether it also buffers) and has its own
  V-21 history of subtle breakage; widening it to a shape it wasn't designed for risked a false
  negative more than it bought coverage. Added a new, explicit
  `TestSSE_everyStreamingDriveSiteHeartbeats` instead, pinning the six sites by name. TDD:
  confirmed red (stripping one site's heartbeat) before green.
- **N-25** `internal/serveapp/main.go:645-597`, `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:61-119` — `maxBytes` (413),
  `limitInflight` (503) and `requireAuth` (401) answer in the OpenAI error shape on `/v1/messages`
  and `count_tokens`; the Anthropic envelope is used only once the handler runs (N-16 (09-02)'s
  class).
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed real: all three middlewares call the
  generic `writeErr` (OpenAI shape) unconditionally, and they are shared across every route
  (including non-API ones like `/web/models/pull`), so fixing this means threading a
  route-specific error-shape choice through all three wrappers' call sites for the two Anthropic
  routes — a real refactor, not a nit, and bigger in scope than this batch's other fixes.
- **N-27** `internal/serveapp/main.go:489` — the `--model` help lists per-model keys and omits
  `ctx=` (accepted at `:130`).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Added `ctx` to the `--model` flag help's key list. Help-text only;
  no behavior change.
- **N-28** `internal/serveapp/main.go:1039-873` — `loadVisionTower` counts `s.models`, which already
  holds every `--adapter` entry, so `--model base --adapter ft=… --vision dir` refuses with "-vision
  needs exactly one --model (got 2)".
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Changed the count check from `len(s.models)` to `len(cfg.models)`
  — matching the auto-discovery branch just above it, which already used `cfg.models` (the raw
  `--model` list) rather than the adapter-inflated registry map. TDD: new
  `TestLoadVisionTower_countsCfgModelsNotSMap` (`internal/serveapp/adapter_test.go`), reusing
  `TestServe_multiAdapter`'s synthetic base+adapter fixtures, confirmed red against the old
  `s.models` count before green.
- **N-30** `internal/serveapp/openai.go:508-389` — `n` is not parsed; `n: 3` gets one choice under
  200. Cheap to 400.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Added `N *int` (`json:"n"`) to `chatReq` and `completionReq`, and
  a shared `validateN` helper called from `handleChat`/`handleCompletions` right after decode: `n`
  omitted or `1` passes, anything else is a clean 400 naming the value instead of a silent
  one-choice response. TDD: new `TestHandleChat_nRejected` (`internal/serveapp/validation_test.go`)
  confirmed red (404 from the empty test server, not 400 naming `n`) before green.
- **N-31** `internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:211` — Gemma 4's raw bytes go to aikit's
  `vision.Gemma4Preprocess`; the Qwen path has a `DecodeConfig`-before-`Decode` bomb guard, the Gemma
  3 path was checked on 09-02, but the Gemma 4 preprocessor lives in aikit v1.39 (not in this
  tree), so whether a 24 MiB PNG declaring 30000×30000 allocates before the ratio math is
  **unverified** (aikit-side).
  **Status: No action possible from this repo.** The code in question lives in aikit, not
  goinfer; verifying or fixing it needs a pass over the aikit repo itself, out of scope here.
- **N-32** `internal/serveapp/liveness.go:122-132` — `closeEntryNatives` drops `venc`/`qwenEnc`/
  `vproj` but not `gemma4Enc` (5b6a49b); plain reference today, the site that will miss a native
  `Close` if one appears.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16, narrower than the audit predicted.** `gemma4Enc`
  (`*vision.Gemma4Encoder`) turns out to have no `Close` method at all — same shape as `vproj`
  (plain weights) — so the real gap wasn't a missing native `Close` call, it was a missing
  nil-out: unlike `vproj`, `gemma4Enc`'s reference was never dropped, retaining a served Gemma 4
  vision tower's weight memory past unload. Added `lm.gemma4Enc = nil`, matching `vproj`'s
  existing line. TDD: new `TestCloseEntryNatives_dropsGemma4Enc`
  (`internal/serveapp/liveness_test.go`) confirmed red before green.
- **N-34** `internal/fitcmd/fit.go:114-111` — `fit` exits 0 when every backend DECLINEs.
  **Status: No action needed — premise doesn't hold.** Traced `Plan`'s CPU handling
  (`decoder/fitplan.go`): CPU is structurally exempt from every decline branch, and `decline()`'s
  own doc comment says why — "CPU alone never declines outright — its whole point is that it can
  always fall back to weight-paging from disk … a CPU 'decline' reads as weight-paged … instead of
  a hard refusal." `CompiledBackends()` (`decoder/backend.go`) unconditionally prepends `"cpu"`.
  So `admitted` can never be empty in current code — "every backend declines" is unreachable by
  design, confirmed by the existing `TestRun_pinnedCtxThatCannotFitDeclinesNotShrinks`
  (`internal/fitcmd/fit_test.go`), which explicitly asserts exit 0 is correct for a bad-but-CPU-
  weight-paged plan. No change made.
- **N-35** `internal/serveapp/main.go:520` — `-require-backend` help ("int8int8 on cuda, ~9× slower
  TTFT") is stale (CUDA batched prefill covers int8 bundles; the `-quant` help at `:433-434` says
  so — the two help strings disagree); `:408` `-vision` help names only SigLIP.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed the disagreement directly: `-quant`'s own help already
  says "All quantized modes (int4/int4mix/int8/int8int8) get batched CUDA prefill … only native
  f32 falls back to the ~9x slower sequential prefill." Reworded `-require-backend`'s example to
  native f32, matching `-quant`'s authoritative claim. Also reworded the `-vision` flag help and
  `loadVisionTower`'s doc comment: the tower is per-family (SigLIP for Gemma 3, Qwen2.5-VL's own
  ViT, Gemma 4's own encoder — confirmed via `visionModelType`'s three-way dispatch in
  `loadVisionTower`), not SigLIP-only. Help/comment-only; no behavior change.


#### CUDA

- **N-36** `cuda/kernels.go:105-108` — "The committed glue.ptx still contains a dead copy [of
  argmax_reduce] until the next regen"; glue.ptx was regenerated in 23c46b1 and has no
  `argmax_reduce`. Stale after the very regen this audit is about.
  **Status: No action needed — already moot.** The exact stale phrase ("dead copy", "until the
  next regen") no longer exists anywhere in `cuda/*.go`; the current comment already reads
  "argmax_reduce moved to argmaxPTX; see below." Confirmed via `grep`, no false negative.
- **N-37** `cuda/backend.go:787-788` — "only moe.ptx + the bench kernels are the audited 12.6.85":
  `moe.ptx`'s banner is `V12.9.86` (M-35 (09-02)); one of the "six places" `moe_ptx_version_test.go`
  was written to catch and not one it reads.
  **Status: No action needed on the original claim — already moot — but found and fixed a real,
  separate bug while checking it.** `moe.ptx`'s embedded banner already reads
  `V12.6.85` (the audited version), and `cuda/backend.go`'s comment already says "moe.ptx, glue.ptx
  and gemv_fwd.ptx are the audited artifacts" (plural, not "moe.ptx + the bench kernels") — both
  fixed by the same M-35 regen this finding's own text anticipated. `TestMoEPTX_versionMatchesItsDocumentation`
  (the self-correcting gate M-35 added) confirmed green. A divergence the audit did not list,
  found while checking this one: `cuda/moe_ptx_version_test.go` was the ONLY test file in the
  entire `cuda` package with no `//go:build cuda` tag (verified: checked every `*_test.go` in the
  package), even though it references `fmaLintExempt`, a map defined in
  `kernel_fma_lint_test.go` under `//go:build cuda` — so a plain `go test ./cuda/...` (no tags)
  failed outright with `undefined: fmaLintExempt`, unrelated to anything this test actually
  checks. CI never notices (its cuda job always passes `-tags cuda`), but it is a real footgun for
  anyone running the package's tests the ordinary way. Added the missing tag, matching every
  sibling file. Confirmed untagged `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./cuda/...` all clean afterward.
- **N-38** `cuda/resident.go:2045-1957` — split-KV comment: "gpt-oss … is not resident-eligible yet";
  gpt-oss is admitted and `r.sinkArg(l)` is passed on that line.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16 (comment-only).** Confirmed: gpt-oss is fully wired into the CUDA
  resident path (`gptOssSw`/`gptOssRoute`/`fMoEWaccBias` are real, dispatched kernels; no decline
  gate rejects it anywhere), and `sinkArg` genuinely returns its real per-layer sink buffer rather
  than always `ArgNull()`. Rewrote the comment to state this instead of the stale "not
  resident-eligible yet" claim. No behavior change.
- **N-39** `docs/cuda-backend.md:43-47` ("`-ctx` unset ⇒ 4096" — no longer after 6c3645d), `:211`
  ("a MoE with a shared expert → declines" — both shared-expert shapes are wired).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16 (both halves, docs-only).** Confirmed `6c3645d` genuinely changed the
  unpinned default: unless `--fit=off`/`-moe-cache-experts`/an unusable free-VRAM probe, an
  unpinned load now tries `min(model context window, 8192)` via `resolveCtxCapFit`, never less
  than 4096 but often more — rewrote the section to state 4096 is now a FLOOR, not the
  unconditional default, and explained when each rule applies. Confirmed shared-expert MoE
  (`cuda/backend.go:413-433`) is fully wired — both the ungated form and Qwen-MoE's sigmoid-gated
  `shared_gate_combine` variant pack and dispatch for real; rewrote the two doc bullets that said
  "not built yet"/"declines" to state that. Docs-only; no code behavior change.
- **N-41** `cuda/prefill.go:171-196` — `PrefillImageLast` does not store `prefillChunkCap` on an OOM
  decline, so a card where 2048 rows is too wide re-drives the context to `OUT_OF_MEMORY` on every
  image turn — the repetition `cuda/resident.go:601-537` calls a hazard.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16, verification limited.** Confirmed real: unlike `prefillChunked`,
  `PrefillImageLast` cannot retry a bidirectional image block at a smaller width (an image OOM is
  a function of that block's own M, which is fixed by the actual image, not of `chunk`), but it
  also never informed `prefillChunkCap` so every later image call re-tried the same doomed width —
  and per `prefillChunkCap`'s own doc comment, repeatedly driving the context to
  `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` risks it afterward launching kernels that "return SUCCESS and execute
  NOTHING," not just wasted retries. On `errPrefillOOM`, it now halves and stores the budget (same
  floor `prefillChunked` uses) so the NEXT, smaller image call is caught by the cheap pre-check
  instead of repeating the real OOM; this call still declines (correctly — it cannot retry
  itself). Extended `prefillChunkCap`'s doc comment to record it as shared state now. **Could not
  compile-check, vet, or test this under `-tags cuda`**: this Mac's `-tags cuda` build fails on
  unrelated pre-existing errors (confirmed via a clean `git stash`: every `gpu.MappedHostBuffer`/
  `gpu.Graph`/etc. symbol cuda/*.go needs is undefined) traced to `aikit/gpu`'s `cuda.go` being
  `//go:build linux` — CUDA has no macOS backend at all, so no local environment fix is possible;
  this is permanent, not a version-skew bug. `gofmt` confirms the edit is syntactically valid Go;
  correctness beyond that relies on CI's Linux-hosted cuda job (build/vet/staticcheck; there is no
  real GPU on that runner either, so an actual OOM-retry path stays untested by any CI gate today —
  the same gap N-40's own closure flagged for GPU-panic-safety surgery).
- **N-42** `cuda/kernel_local_memory_test.go:150-156` — "22 .ptx blobs … 12 are here" is stale twice
  over (15 listed, 22 embedded; G-13b).
  **Status: No action needed — already moot.** The cited comment already reads "31 .ptx blobs are
  go:embed-ed across cuda/*.go today, 22 of them in kernels.go — exactly what `ptxModules()`
  reports, since G-13(b) made it read that same file" — a real 2026-09-12 audit
  (`docs/completed/cuda-megakernel-closeout.md`) already fixed this, unannotated in this doc.
  `ptxModules()` is confirmed genuinely self-deriving (reads `kernels.go`'s own embed list, not a
  hand-maintained count), so this class of staleness cannot recur silently. No change made.

- **N-44** `metal/backend.go` (`metalFastPrefillEnabled`'s doc comment, historically ~377-340) was

- **N-45** `metal/backend.go` (`GOINFER_METAL_BATCHED_PREFILL`, historically ~395-356) accepted only
  `"1"` as on; `true`/`on` silently meant OFF, unlike the FAST_PREFILL parser three lines above and
  `env-vars.md:42`. **(moot as of the same N-08 check)** — the current code's own comment

  PREFILL` var's flexible `1/true/on` / `0/false/off` parsing — not a silent-OFF bug, a documented
  design choice between two variables with different contracts.
- **N-46** `metal/prefill.go:339-380,386` — `attention_prefill_fused` loads full 8-row K/V tiles up
  to `j0+7 ≥ nKeysMax`; in-bounds only because `ctxCap` is a multiple of 8 today. An explicit `-ctx`
  that is not (accepted by `resolveMetalCtxCap`) with a prompt at the cap reads up to 7 rows past
  the buffer. Round up in `resolveMetalCtxCap` or clamp the last tile. **(fixed, audit-metal-
  2026-09-12.md C-01, 84c3f29f)** — the kernel's own read pattern is unchanged (the tile is still
  loaded whole, masked after), but `buildResident` now rounds the `kc`/`vc` *allocation* up to a
  multiple of 8 rows, so the same read lands in allocated padding instead of past the buffer end.
- **N-47** `metal/prefill.go:444-467` — a failed `ensurePrefill` leaves `r.pf == nil`; every later
  `PrefillLast` recompiles and re-panics, and each attempt's partial pipelines stay on the ledger.
  Latch the failure. **FIXED 2026-09-13**: added `resident.pfErr` (`metal/model.go`) — `ensurePrefill`
  now checks it first and re-panics the SAME cached error immediately instead of re-running the
  full MSL compile; a recover-and-latch `defer` inside the compile path sets it exactly once, on
  the first failure. "Each attempt's partial pipelines stay on the ledger" is unaffected by this fix
  (a separate, smaller leak on the failure path itself, not the repeated-recompile cost this closes)
  — not addressed here. New `TestEnsurePrefill_latchesFailure` (device-independent: the short-circuit
  runs entirely before `ensurePrefill` touches the real Device) confirmed red without the fix — with
  the check removed, a cached-error resident fell through to a real compile attempt and panicked
  with an unrelated compile-landmine message instead of the cached sentinel — then green with it.
- **N-48** `metal/lora.go:166-125` — `SetAdapter` returns on the first bad projection with the
  earlier projections' buffers allocated and never released; `metal/moe.go:805-764` and
  `gemma4_moe.go` paged forwards write `uPos`/`uNKeys` directly instead of `setPos`, so
  `uQTempScale` is not refreshed there (no paged family has `FeatAttnTemp` today; `setPos`'s
  "called at every forward entry point" is false); `metal/backend.go:289-202` `residentKVBytes`
  prices a KV cache for DeltaNet layers that allocate none.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16 (all three sub-claims), two already fixed elsewhere.** The core leak
  was already closed by C-04 (`audit-metal-2026-09-12.md`) — `SetAdapter` already has the
  `bound`-flag + `defer releaseLoRALayers` idiom (`TestSetAdapter_partialBindErrorReleasesBuffers`
  is its regression gate), unannotated in this doc. `residentKVBytes` was already fixed by N-36
  (same metal audit) — it already excludes Gated-DeltaNet linear layers from KV pricing.
  The `setPos` bypass was genuinely still open: grepped every direct `uPos.SetU32`/`uNKeys.SetU32`
  call site in `metal/` and found 3 real production forward entry points bypassing `setPos` (the
  two paged-MoE forwards this finding named, plus `ForwardArgmax` — the fast-greedy path, not
  named by the finding but the same gap) alongside several `...ForTest` test-only helpers
  (correctly out of `setPos`'s "every forward entry point" scope, left untouched). Replaced all
  three direct writes with `r.setPos(pos)` — a strict superset (writes the same two uniforms plus
  qTempScale) and a behavioral no-op today (no family combines a paged/argmax dispatch with
  `FeatAttnTemp`), closing the gap for the day one does. Full `metal` suite green (161s, no FAIL).
- **N-49** `decoder/model.go:1133-1002` with `metal/backend.go:616-431` — every Metal process prints
  "batched prefill declined for a N-token prompt, falling back … (slower TTFT)" the first time a
  prompt of 8–511 tokens arrives; the floor is the intended path. `decoder/spec_adaptive.go:242-233`
  — the M-14 predicate treats `PrefillPath()==batched` as "ForwardN is batched"; Metal now reports
  batched while its `ForwardN` is a per-row loop (harmless only because `thetaFor("metal")` is
  1.02). `metal/backend.go:26-37` vs `docs/measurements/prefill-gate-l1-ref-b-2026-09-09.md:41-45`
  — the registered probe returns 0.70×`hw.memsize`, yet the D7 refusal in the record came from a
  live-available number: two guards judged the same load by different rules.
  **Status: split — three sub-claims, one already moot, one documented (comment-only), one
  deferred (2026-09-16).** (1) The misleading print: still real. `warnPrefillDeclined`'s per-reason
  dedup (N-35, `audit-metal-2026-09-12.md`) already keeps this to one line, and the underlying
  Metal error text already names it a deliberate floor ("§3 floor"), but the OUTER wrapper's
  "declined … falling back (slower TTFT)" framing still reads as regrettable for what is, on
  Metal, the ROUTINE path for most real (short) prompts — not the rare case
  `warnPrefillDeclined`'s own doc comment assumes ("this should be rare"). Distinguishing "this
  decline is BY DESIGN and expected" from "this decline is a genuine capability gap" in the
  message is a real change to a shared, backend-agnostic function's contract — deferred rather
  than special-cased unilaterally this batch, the same call this session made for N-29/N-33's
  design-decision findings. (2) The M-14/Metal coupling: confirmed real and exactly as described —
  `metalResident` implements `PrefillPathReporter`, but its `ForwardN` is unconditionally
  sequential regardless of what `PrefillPath()` reports, so `verifyTheta`'s predicate is
  structurally answering the wrong question for Metal and is only numerically correct today
  because `thetaFor("metal")` independently also disables speculation either way. Documented at
  both `verifyTheta`'s doc comment and `TestThetaFor_cudaConstantUnchanged` (which already asserts
  `thetaFor(metal) >= 1` and is now explicitly named as the tripwire: if a real batched Metal
  verify path ever ships and that constant drops below 1 without this predicate being revisited
  for Metal, that test fails first). No behavior change — the existing test already made this
  safe; what was missing was the reader-facing explanation, not a code fix. (3) The two-guards
  claim: already moot. Confirmed both `residentFitsMemory` (the real guard) and the registered
  probe use the identical `hw.memsize × 0.70` formula — no "live-available" query exists anywhere
  in `metal/backend.go` (checked every RAM-related syscall in the file). The registered probe's
  own comment already says why: "not a live 'available' query, deliberately: darwin's UBC reclaim
  makes 'available' report what survived rather than what can be asked for … Plan sees exactly the
  number the real guard would judge it against, so the two can never disagree" — this appears to
  have been fixed in direct response to the measurement record this finding cites, unannotated in
  this doc. No change made.


#### Engineering and gates

- **N-50** `scripts/gate_ledger.py:62-89` — `source_sha256` hashes the `func TestX(` body only;
  `TestGGUF_Q4_K_M_parity` and seven siblings are one-line calls into `testGGUFParity`, so loosening
  the helper leaves eight ledger entries CONFIRMED against changed semantics — the case the
  docstring says the key exists to catch.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Confirmed real (verified `TestGGUF_Q4_K_M_parity`'s
  body is one line calling `testGGUFParity`). The fix itself (hash the callee's body too, for a
  wrapper whose body is a near-literal delegation) is well-scoped, but `testdata/gate_ledger.json`
  currently has 71 CONFIRMED entries, several of them exactly this wrapper shape — changing the
  hash formula immediately invalidates them (correctly, per the mechanism's own "renaming/changing
  drops to stale, SAFE and loud" design), requiring a human to re-promote each one. That is the
  right call for someone who owns the ledger to make deliberately, not a side effect of a batch
  nit nobody asked to trigger.
- **N-51** `scripts/queue_citation_lint.py:692-732` vs `:908-911` — `--update` refuses to launder a
  SHIFTED citation but silently re-keys a CONTENT-ABSENT one to whatever now sits at that line; the
  guard's comment ("Weakening a key is a decision, never a side effect") describes the case it does
  not cover. Many commits in the log run `--update`.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed precisely: the launder-refusal loop's `at is not None`
  check only fires when the old content is found ELSEWHERE in the file (a genuine shift); when it
  is found NOWHERE (deleted or rewritten, not moved — including resolve_path's own
  line-past-EOF `""` case), `at` stays `None` and the citation silently fell through to the
  unconditional re-key at the bottom of the function. Added an `else` branch that refuses this
  case too, with its own "CONTENT GONE" message naming what vanished. TDD: new
  `TestContentGoneRefused` (`scripts/test_queue_citation_lint.py`), isolated-temp-repo discipline
  matching the existing test class, confirmed red (the old code silently succeeded) before green.
  Sanity-checked the fix against this actual repo's 700+ citations (`--update`, no new refusals) —
  it does not disturb the current corpus.
- **N-52** `cuda/lora_resident_parity_test.go:95`, `metal/…:104`, `gpu/…:100` — the G3 gates pass at
  cosine ≥ 0.95 while measuring 0.99996; a dropped projection site is likely inside the margin;
  the vacuousness check proves only "some effect". Tighten to the measured floor minus noise.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Choosing a new floor is a measurement-design
  decision this repo's own measurement-discipline rules (docs/CLAUDE.md) take seriously —
  interleaved runs, a pre-registered decision rule, noise characterization across real seeds and
  checkpoints, on THREE backends (only two of which this Mac can even run) — not a number to pick
  by inspection in a batch pass.
- **N-53** `RELEASING.md:287-288` — "All 7 declared check groups" is stale: `cmd/gate/gpu.go:445`
  declares nine for Metal (`parity`, `webgpu` added).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `cmd/gate/gpu.go`'s Metal declaration
  (`cleangpu seam suite parity cgofree lifecycle prefill webgpu repo`) is 9 groups, not 7, and the
  RELEASING.md list itself was ALSO stale (missing `parity` and `webgpu`, not just the count).
  Fixed both. Doc-only; no behavior change.
- **N-54** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:344-346` — comment says metal/go.mod `replace`s the root but
  does not `require` it; `metal/go.mod:8` requires `goinfer v0.17.2` with no replace. `:69-70` —
  "apidiff (Hard tier vs the last release)" runs against v0.13.0 (deliberate per the 1.0 clock;

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `metal/go.mod` requires the root (currently v0.18.0,

  `apidiff_check.sh`'s own usage comment to call v0.13.0 what it is — a deliberately frozen

- **N-55** `pull/registry_tokenizer_test.go:27-35` — CHANGELOG v0.17.1:136-140 says the tokenizer
  gate "reads each entry's committed GGUF-header fixture"; the map covers 2 of 5 registry checkpoints.

  `phi3-mini-4k`, `gpt-oss-20b`, `gemma-4-26b-a4b` SKIP for lack of a fixture. Adding the missing

- **N-56** `.github/workflows/fuzz-weekly.yml:59-73` — the flake-retry path is dead for

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `constrain/testdata/fuzz/FuzzJSONSchema/` carries 5

  `compgen -G` against the live crash dir always matched, making every failure (flake or real)
  read as "reproducer written" on attempt 1 with no retry. Snapshot the crash dir's file listing
  BEFORE fuzzing and diff (`comm -13`) against it after a failure: a genuinely NEW file is a real
  crash, an unchanged listing (empty, or exactly the pre-existing seeds) is the flake case.
  Verified the diff logic directly in an isolated shell sandbox against all three shapes
  (pre-existing seeds unchanged, a new crasher appearing, always-empty) before editing the

- **N-57** `.github/workflows/book-pages.yml:12-17` — triggers on `docs/book/**` only;

- **N-58** `.github/workflows/release-assets.yml:25-26` — `contents: write` is workflow-wide while

  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** A workflow-permissions change on the release

#### Loaders and formats

- **N-62** `decoder/weights.go:1568-1509` — the schema comment says `"" ⇒ no override, same suffix
  on every layer`, but `buildWeightsFromSafetensors` (`:864-867`) treats the four `*Linear` fields
  as all-or-nothing (what `olmoHybridTensorSchema:1845,1847` relies on); the comment describes the
  opposite contract.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed: `hasLinearNormOverride` ORs all four fields together
  and swaps them in AS A GROUP for `isLinearLayer(i)` rows — a field left `""` within that group
  means "no norm here," not "fall back to the generic field," which is what the old comment
  implied. Rewrote the comment to describe the real group-or-nothing contract, including the
  concrete failure mode (leaving one of the four set and expecting per-field fallback silently
  drops the other three's generic norms on linear layers). No current family hits this — Olmo
  Hybrid, the only user, sets all four together. Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-63** `decoder/serialize.go:1225-1138` — "the KDA mixer (presence byte + the nine tensors)":
  `v9Layer` writes thirteen (`kdaWeights` has 13 fields); reader matches; comment only.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Counted `v9Layer`'s own writes directly: 13, matching
  `kdaWeights`' 13 fields exactly. Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-64** `decoder/gguf.go:1560-1536` — "the qwen35/gemma4 dedicated paths reject [streaming]" is
  stale: qwen35 streams per layer; gemma4 is routed by `needsResidentSerialize`.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `needsResidentSerialize` lists gemma4 plus five other
  families (gptoss, laguna, granite, nemotron, llama4) — qwen35 is NOT among them, and its own
  branch does call `sink.layer` per iteration (streams). Reworded the comment to name both facts
  accurately and point at `needsResidentSerialize`'s own doc comment for the authoritative list.
  Comment-only; no behavior change.
- **N-65** `internal/pullcmd/pull.go` (was line 136) — "the partial .part file is cleaned up by
  Download" contradicts `pull/pull.go:590-562`, which deliberately keeps it for resume.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `pull/pull.go`'s own comment on the same line: "Deliberately
  NOT removed: the bytes already fetched are the whole point of resume" — covers Ctrl-C
  cancellation too, since context cancellation surfaces as an `io.Copy` error on that same path.
  Rewrote the pullcmd comment to state the real, opposite behavior. Comment-only; no behavior
  change.
- **N-66** `decoder/registry.go:1090-1099` (ministral3) — the mscale override fires when *either*
  `mscale`/`mscale_all_dim` is present and overrides an explicit `attention_factor`; HF uses the
  ratio only when both are truthy and `attention_factor` is None. No effect on the released
  checkpoint (both 1.0). Plausible.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** The finding is explicitly uncertain ("Plausible") and
  concedes zero effect on any released checkpoint. Confirming HF's exact conditional (both fields
  truthy AND `attention_factor` absent, vs. this code's either-field-present check) needs real HF
  source verification, not code inspection here — getting a "fix" wrong on an uncertain, currently
  inert premise risks introducing a genuine regression for a future checkpoint shape.
- **N-67** `decoder/registry.go:833-808` (olmo_hybrid) — an *absent* `rope_parameters` also yields
  `noRope = true` and the top-level `rope_theta` is never consulted, so a pre-5.10 flat config
  loads position-blind silently (M-06 (09-02)'s class). No released checkpoint has that shape.
  **Status: No action needed — premise doesn't hold.** `olmoHybridArchitecture` calls
  `cfg.validateOlmoHybrid()` as its very first statement, and that validation already has
  `case len(c.RopeParameters) == 0: return fmt.Errorf(...)` — an absent `rope_parameters` is
  refused with a clear, actionable error before the `noRope`/`base` logic this finding describes
  ever runs. (Confirmed by first writing the described fallback, then finding it was dead code —
  reverted rather than shipped.) No change made.
- **N-68** `decoder/registry.go:181-200` — `validateConfigBounds` bounds `num_experts` but not
  `n_routed_experts`/`num_local_experts`; the `.giw` reader's own caps keep it from being an OOM.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `NumLocalExperts` (mixtral/gpt-oss/llama4) and
  `NRoutedExperts` (glm4_moe/deepseek/nemotron_h) are separate `Config` fields, neither in the
  bounds-check loop. Added both, same `maxGGUFExperts` ceiling as `num_experts`. Defense-in-depth
  per the finding's own framing (the `.giw` reader already prevents the OOM downstream), not
  closing a live exploit. TDD: extended `TestValidateConfigBounds_hostileCountsAreRefusedBeforeAllocation`
  with both cases, confirmed red before green.
- **N-69** `decoder/gguf.go:498-492` (mellum) — `attention_factor` is always synthesized from
  `rope.scaling.yarn_attn_factor`, including llama.cpp's `1.0` "unset" sentinel and `0` when absent —
  the trap `ggufLagunaConfig:567-572` documents and avoids.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed `ggufMellumConfig` passed `rope.scaling.yarn_attn_factor`
  through unconditionally, unlike `ggufLagunaConfig`'s own guard for the identical GGUF field.
  Applied the same conditional-omission pattern: the field is now included only when the value is
  neither `0` (metadata-absent) nor `1` (llama.cpp's own "unset" sentinel), letting the decoder
  compute YaRN's default mscale otherwise. TDD: new
  `TestGGUFMellumConfig_attentionFactorSentinelOmitted` (4 cases: absent, `1.0` sentinel, `0`, a
  real explicit value) confirmed red on the first three before green.
- **N-70** `pull/pull.go:523,538-540` — `f.Size == 0` (non-LFS) with a complete `.part` → Range past
  EOF → 416 reported as a plain error and the `.part` never cleared; `:251` — `info.Gated.(string)`
  treats a JSON `true` as ungated (fails late with a 401 instead of the early message).
  **Status: FIRST HALF FIXED 2026-09-16; second half no action needed, doesn't match reality.**
  The Range/EOF half is real and live: for `f.Size <= 0`, the resume guard admits ANY existing
  `.part` regardless of completeness, so a prior run that fetched every byte but was interrupted
  before the digest-check-and-rename re-requests a Range starting exactly at EOF next time — a
  416 the old code reported as a bare error while leaving the `.part` in place, so every retry hit
  the identical 416 forever. Added a `StatusRequestedRangeNotSatisfiable` case that verifies the
  already-hashed `.part` against the digest and renames it directly (or removes it and reports
  clearly on a mismatch) instead of falling into the generic-error default. TDD: two new tests
  (`TestDownload_rangeNotSatisfiableOnAnAlreadyCompletePart`,
  `TestDownload_rangeNotSatisfiableWithBadDigestIsCleanedUp`) confirmed red (reproducing both the
  confusing error and the un-cleared `.part`) before green. The `Gated` half does NOT hold:
  fetched HuggingFace's own authoritative OpenAPI schema (`.well-known/openapi.md`) directly — the
  `gated` field's documented type is `false | "auto" | "manual"`, never a bare `true`. The
  existing `info.Gated.(string)` check already covers every real value this field takes; no
  change made.


#### Constrain, tokenizer, chat, demos

- **N-71** `internal/chatapp/main.go:681-608`, `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:564-541` vs
  `internal/serveapp/openai.go:996` — the demos' masker EOS set is `{EOS, EndOfTurn}` while serve  uses `eosIDs ∪ stopIDs`; for Llama-3 (`<|eot_id|>`) and harmony (`<|end|>`) the template's stop id
  is masked as an illegal surface under `/json`. Three copies.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed both demos' `jsonMasker`/`schemaMasker` unioned only
  `{EOS, EndOfTurn}`, matching serve's own `eosIDs ∪ stopIDs` precedent
  (`internal/serveapp/openai.go`). Added `eos = append(eos, s.stopIDs...)` to both. Tests:
  `TestJSONMasker_holdsBackTemplateStopIDs` (chatapp), `TestSchemaMasker_holdsBackTemplateStopIDs`
  (demo/agent — mapping the stop id specifically to grammar-legal byte `{` so the test isolates
  EOS-registration, not incidental grammar-invalidity). Red/green confirmed both ways.
- **N-72** `tokenizer/gguf.go:322-327` — the `"gpt-2", "default", ""` arm's comment ("not a claim of
  correctness … the walker") is stale: `splitGPT2Original` exists, so no decline is set; and
  llama.cpp's `PRE_TYPE_DEFAULT` is not GPT-2's regex, so a `pre="default"` GGUF is walked with a
  shape the file never declared (plausible, from llama.cpp as remembered).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16 (comment-only).** Verified against llama.cpp's actual current source
  (`src/llama-vocab.cpp`, fetched directly): `LLAMA_VOCAB_PRE_TYPE_GPT2` uses one alternation
  pattern; `LLAMA_VOCAB_PRE_TYPE_DEFAULT` has no explicit case and falls to the switch's own
  `default:`, which uses four separate patterns (leading punctuation, shared alternation, unbounded
  digit runs, fixed 3-digit grouping) — a real, confirmed divergence from `shapeGPT2Original`.
  Rewrote the comment to (a) state `shapeGPT2Original` is real and golden-tested, not merely
  historical, and (b) document the confirmed `gpt-2` vs `default` divergence, explicitly deferring
  a real `shapeDefault`-equivalent implementation as out-of-scope new work. No behavior change.
- **N-73** `tokenizer/split_o200k_test.go:44-58`, `split_gpt2orig_test.go` — the oracle's
  `\s+(?!\S)` branch slices bytes, wrong for a multi-byte trailing whitespace rune; the random
  alphabet has only ASCII whitespace, so the oracle is never exercised where it is wrong.
  **Status: DEFERRED to individual review.** Attempted a rune-aware fix (tracking rune count and
  last-rune byte length via `unicode.IsSpace`), which looked correct in isolation but failed against
  the real oracle-vs-walker comparison on `a  b`: Go's RE2 `\s` (used by the surrounding
  alternatives, e.g. `^ ?[^\s\p{L}\p{N}]+...`) is ASCII-only, while `unicode.IsSpace` is
  Unicode-aware — the same oracle disagreeing with itself about what counts as whitespace for a
  multi-byte rune. Reverted the fix entirely (`git checkout --`) rather than ship a change that
  reproduces a wrong answer with more confidence. Added comment-only documentation of both the
  byte-slicing bug and the deeper RE2-vs-Unicode mismatch to both files, explicitly marked
  "investigated 2026-09-16, NOT fixed." No behavior change.
- **N-74** `constrain/reflect.go:220-225` — a `[]byte` field maps to an integer array;
  `encoding/json` decodes `[]byte` from a base64 STRING, so the constrained output is guaranteed to
  fail `Unmarshal` (M-28 (09-02)'s "shape is guaranteed" claim); `[N]T` gets no `maxItems`.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** `typeSchema`'s slice/array case now special-cases `[]byte` to
  `{"type": "string"}` (matching `encoding/json`'s own base64-string special case) and sets
  `minItems`/`maxItems` = `t.Len()` for a fixed-size `[N]T` array (Go silently truncates/zero-pads a
  wrong element count on unmarshal rather than erroring — confirmed directly via a standalone test
  script before relying on it). Test: `TestGrammarFromStruct_byteSliceAndFixedArray` — a hand-built
  valid-base64 payload round-trips, plus direct schema-map shape assertions; deliberately does not
  fuzz full round-trips, since a random grammar-legal string isn't guaranteed valid base64 content
  (no `"pattern"` keyword support here) — an earlier fuzz-based test version failed on that flaw,
  not on the fix. Red/green confirmed.
- **N-75** `constrain/reflect.go:215-218` vs `:87-94` — `hasExportedFields` ignores promoted fields
  from an unexported embedded struct: accepted at top level, refused as a nested type. Loud.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** `hasExportedFields` now mirrors `structSchema`'s existing V-14 fix:
  an anonymous embedded field of an unexported struct type recurses into
  `hasExportedFields(embedded)` instead of being skipped outright. Test:
  `TestSchemaFromStruct_unexportedEmbedPromotedAsNestedFieldToo` — a `Person{ base }` with no OTHER
  exported field, nested inside `Wrapper{ P Person }`, asserting the nested promotion succeeds. An
  earlier test version also gave `Person` a separately-exported field, which meant even the old,
  buggy code passed via that field alone without ever exercising the embed-promotion path being
  fixed — corrected before trusting the result. Red/green confirmed.
- **N-76** `constrain/schema.go:337-340` — `int(f)` for `maxItems`/`minItems` ≥ 2^63 is
  implementation-defined; a huge `maxItems` can read back negative and become "unbounded".
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** `intKeyword` now rejects `f > math.MaxInt` with a clear compile
  error instead of converting. Test: extended `TestSchema_rejectsUnsatisfiable` with `maxItems`/
  `minItems` at `1e30`. Red/green confirmed.
- **N-77** `internal/chatapp/serveonly.go:133-138` — the redirect scans every argv token including
  flag VALUES: `--system serve` exits 2 with "serve belongs to goinfer-serve".
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** `serveOnlyInvocation` now tracks chat's own value-taking flags (the
  space-separated `--flag value` form only — `--flag=value` is already one token) and skips checking
  the token that is that flag's value, rather than matching it against the serve-only word list.
  Tests: three new cases in `TestServeOnlyInvocation` — a flag value equal to a serveOnly word
  (`--system serve` → no redirect), the same with a genuine serve-only flag after it (→ redirects on
  `--web`), and a genuine serve-only flag NOT preceded by a value-taking flag (still caught). Red/
  green confirmed.
- **N-78** `internal/chatapp/prequant.go:118-146` — `loadEmbedded` ignores `opts.LoRA` (and the
  documented `opts.Quant`); `--lora` on a prequant build is a silent no-op. `internal/serveapp/main.go:711-613` —
  `jsonMasker` falls back to the free JSON grammar when the schema fails to compile (unreachable
  today; the silent-fallback shape `constrain` refuses elsewhere).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16, both halves.** The `opts.Quant` half needed no fix: `version.go`'s
  `quantIsFixedAtBuildTime` already reports "baked at build time; --quant has no effect on this
  binary" via `--version`, so that half was already handled, just not by this file. The `opts.LoRA`
  half was real: `decoder.NewModel` (what the prequant `loadEmbedded` calls, unlike
  `decoder.Load`/`loadFromPath`) takes no `*decoder.Options` at all, so there is no chokepoint that
  could ever merge a LoRA adapter into a `.giw` bundle's pre-serialized weights. Added an explicit
  `opts.LoRA != ""` check returning a clear error instead of silently proceeding. Test:
  `TestLoadEmbedded_rejectsLoRAInsteadOfSilentlyIgnoringIt` (prequant-tagged, requires a staged
  `model.giw` like the file's existing prequant tests — the check fires before `giw.Read`, so a
  tiny local stub sufficed to prove it without a real bundle). Red/green confirmed. The
  `jsonMasker` silent-fallback did not exist in `internal/serveapp/main.go` as cited (that file's
  `grammarFor`/caller already propagate a schema-compile error, never swallow it) — the actual
  instance was in `internal/chatapp/main.go`'s own `jsonMasker` (a separate silent
  `constrain.JSON()` fallback from N-71's fix, same function). Confirmed genuinely unreachable
  today (main's flag parsing already fail-fasts via `os.Exit` before `s.schema` is ever set to
  anything invalid) but changed the swallow to a loud `stderr` warning instead of silence, so a
  future caller that sets `s.schema` some other way doesn't get a silently weaker constraint. Test:
  `TestJSONMasker_warnsOnUnreachableSchemaCompileFailure`, driving the branch directly with
  intentionally-invalid schema bytes (bypassing the CLI check, which is the point) and asserting
  the warning reaches stderr. Red/green confirmed.
- **N-80** `chat/tools.go:109-117` — `json.Marshal` HTML-escapes `<>&` (matching Jinja's `tojson`)
  but not `'`, which Jinja renders as `&#39;`; cosmetic prompt drift for tool descriptions.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16 — and the audit's own citation was wrong.** Fetched jinja2's actual
  source (`src/jinja2/utils.py`, `htmlsafe_json_dumps`, what `| tojson` calls) directly: the
  apostrophe substitution is the same six-character backslash-u-NNNN escape form as the other
  three (`<`/`>`/`&`, which `encoding/json`'s default `SetEscapeHTML` already matches exactly), NOT
  the HTML entity this audit's own bullet claimed — corrected the claim rather than reproducing it.
  Added `tojsonEscape`, applied in `funcDefJSON` and `jsonStr`. Test:
  `TestFuncDefJSON_escapesApostropheLikeJinjaTojson`, asserting the escape via the production
  code's own `apostropheEscape` value rather than a hand-typed literal (typing the escape sequence
  directly in a tool-call parameter silently decoded it back into a raw apostrophe once already
  during this fix — building it via `fmt.Sprintf` at runtime instead avoids the trap entirely).
  Red/green confirmed.
- **N-81** `multimodal/qwen_preprocess.go:186-190` — `qwenSmartResize` does not enforce HF's
  `max/min ≤ 200` aspect rejection (acknowledged; a 1×20000 strip produces a legal 28×28 grid where
  HF raises).
  **Status: No action needed — already a deliberate, documented tradeoff.** Re-verified against
  current code: the existing comment already explains the choice (flooring to one cell rather than
  matching HF's hard `ValueError` past ratio 200, to avoid an empty-`pixel_values` footgun on a
  degenerate input no real model produces). `qwenSmartResize`'s signature has no error return, so
  matching HF exactly would mean threading an error through every caller for what upstream treats
  as malformed-input rejection — real scope, not warranted for an already-acknowledged edge case.
  Added an explicit N-81 citation to the existing comment for traceability. No behavior change.


#### WebGPU

- **N-82** `gpu/lora_resident.go:191-201` — `SetAdapter` returns mid-loop on a `mk` error with the
  already-built entries never released; `r.steps` is not reassigned on that path (the doc at
  `:97-99` claims the restore happens).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16, and worse than a leak alone.** Confirmed both claims: the mid-loop
  `mk` error path leaked every already-built `loraRunLayer` in `built`, AND left `r.steps`
  pointing at the PREVIOUS successful bind's spliced-in dispatch steps — whose bind groups had
  already been released by `SetAdapter`'s own unconditional top-of-function
  `releaseLoraLayers(r.loraLayers)` — so a `Run()` after a failed rebind would dispatch against
  freed WebGPU resources, not just leak memory. Applied the same `bound`-flag + `defer` idiom
  metal/lora.go's C-04 already uses, releasing `built` AND calling `r.rebuildSteps()` (which
  resets `r.steps` to `r.baseSteps` since `r.loraLayers` is already nil) on any early return.
  TDD: new `TestSetAdapter_partialBindErrorRestoresSteps`
  (`gpu/lora_resident_bind_leak_test.go`), run against a real WebGPU device on this machine
  (llama-tiny, 4 layers × 7 projections), confirmed red (110 stale steps left over from a prior
  successful bind, want 54 = `len(r.baseSteps)`) before green. Full `gpu` suite green (195s, no
  FAIL), including the existing `TestLoRAResidentParityWebGPU` numeric gate.
- **N-83** `gpu/gemv_w4a8.go:94` — `bScales … f32 per-group scales`: they are f16 pairs
  (`packF16Pairs`, `:215,256`).
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16 (comment-only).** Confirmed both writers (`UploadW4A8`,
  `UploadW4A8Packed`) pack via `packF16Pairs`, never raw f32. Rewrote the field comment.
- **N-84** `gpu/decoderunner.go:1504,1564` — `Run`/`runBatch` ignore `pass.TryEnd()` and
  `CopyBufferToBuffer` errors where `run()` (`gpu/gpu.go:769-776`) checks them; a validation error
  surfaces later as a map failure with no cause. (Renamed from `pass.End()` by the webgpu binding
  migration, a16a537d — the finding's shape is unchanged, only the method name.)
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** Confirmed both `Run` and `runBatch` discarded `pass.TryEnd()`'s and
  `enc.TryCopyBufferToBuffer(...)`'s errors, unlike every other fallible call in the same
  functions and unlike `gpu.go`'s `run()` (the reference both now mirror exactly). Verified on
  real WebGPU hardware: full `gpu` suite green (97 real PASS, 0 FAIL, 14.6s), including
  `TestDecodeRunnerMoE_parity`/`TestDecodeRunnerMoEShared_parity` which exercise `Run` directly.
  No dedicated error-injection test added (forcing a real WebGPU validation error needs a
  contrived invalid-buffer setup outside this batch's scope) — verified by mirroring an
  already-tested pattern and confirming no regression, not by TDD red/green on this specific path.
- **N-85** `gpu/attention.go:50-36,383,393,502-511,623-629` — RoPE angle `f32(p.pos)*invFreq[d]` into
  WGSL `sin`/`cos` at the i8 ceiling (65536 positions) is ~6.5e4 rad through implementation-defined
  range reduction; CPU computes in f64. Unmeasured; the 09-02 Metal note (`metal/kernels.go:529`)
  applies verbatim.
  **Status: MEASURED 2026-09-16 (real hardware; not fixed — no code change indicated).** The
  "i8 ceiling" is `decoder/fitplan.go`'s int8-KV fit-by-default context cap (65536 positions), a
  real position a served request can reach, not a typo. New
  `TestRoPE_parityAtLongContextCeiling` (`gpu/attention_test.go`) drives the real GPU RoPE kernel
  at pos=65535 against the CPU f64 reference on this machine's real GPU: cosine 0.99999978, maxAbs
  2.9e-3 — about 1000x worse than the existing `TestRoPE_parity`'s pos=37 baseline (maxAbs
  3.1e-06). A real, now-measured effect, not just a theoretical one. Deliberately NOT turned into
  a pass/fail gate: reusing the short-position test's tolerance for this case would assert a
  quality bar (is ~2.9e-3 maxAbs acceptable at the practical context ceiling?) this batch has no
  basis to pick — recorded as a measurement instead. Metal's identical `float(pos)*invf[dd]`
  pattern remains unmeasured; this result does not carry over (GPU vendors' `sin`/`cos` range
  reduction is independently implementation-defined per backend).
- **N-87** `gpu/residency.go:1049-1030`, `gpu/decoderunner.go:1275-1279` — every non-gpt-oss layer
  uploads its own one-float dummy sink AND the runner allocates `noAttnSinks`. Cosmetic.
  **Status: No action needed — confirmed real, genuinely cosmetic (audit's own characterization).**
  `noAttnSinks`'s own doc comment confirms it exists purely as a runner-local fallback for
  hand-built test fixtures that skip `BuildResident`, not as a reuse mechanism for the production
  path — the two buffers have different owners/lifetimes (resident, shared across runners, vs
  per-runner) for a real reason. The redundancy is a handful of 4-byte buffers against
  weight/KV-cache VRAM. Documented the reasoning at the call site; no behavior change.
- **N-88** `gpu/backend_w4a8_test.go:30-31` — K=517 exercises only the unpack-and-repack fallback;
  the production `K%32==0` path is covered only transitively.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16.** `TestWebGPUBackend_MatmulW4A8_matchesCPU` is now table-driven over
  both K=517 (fallback) and K=512 (the production `K%32==0` fast path, confirmed via
  `residentW4A8For`'s own branch condition that this call actually takes `UploadW4A8Packed`).
  Both verified against the CPU reference on real hardware: PASS, cosine 1.000000 for both.
- **N-89** `gpu/decoderunner.go:1323-1386` — the MoE FFN branch has no `postMLPNorm`, so a
  sandwich-norm MoE family would silently drop the post-FFN norm; unreachable today (Gemma 4 MoE
  declined by `PerLayerGeomOK`); a build-time refusal of `sandwichOK && isMoE` would make it loud.
  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-16, per the finding's own suggested shape.** Confirmed real: the dense
  FFN branch checks `lw.postMLPNorm != nil` before the residual add; the MoE branch (both the
  gpt-oss and generic sub-paths, plus the shared-expert add) writes straight into `r.xd` with no
  equivalent check anywhere. Implementing real `postMLPNorm` support in the MoE dispatch is
  feature work outside this batch's scope (no family needs it today). Added the suggested
  build-time refusal instead: `BuildResident` now fails loudly with a named error when
  `sandwichOK && moeOK`, so the gap can never ship silently on the day something admits that
  combination. No dedicated fixture test added — constructing a `decoder.Model` with both
  properties needs an architecturally-invalid synthetic GGUF fixture (no real family combines
  them, and no test hook exists to fake it directly); verified instead via `go build`/`go vet`
  plus a clean full `gpu` suite run (97 PASS, 0 FAIL) confirming no real sandwich-norm or MoE
  fixture accidentally trips the new guard.
- **N-90** `docs/gpu-residency-coverage.md:233-239` — footnote 5 still says "WebGPU still declines
  [Gemma 4] — it lacks the four Gemma kernels"; after db81b08 the decline is per-layer geometry.
  **Status: No action needed — already moot.** The file is 147 lines today (the cited 233-239
  range no longer exists) and already says the correct thing: "The decline is... needs a per-layer
  geometry seam CUDA and [Metal have]" — matching what this finding asked for, unannotated fix.
