### §5 — Performance observations (second sweep)

- **P-01** `decoder/model.go:1056,1225-1226` (fixed, both halves) — `Model.Generate` no longer
  allocates the host KV cache before the resBusy CAS; it passes a `newCache func() *KVCache`
  closure into `generateInto`, which calls it only on `!useGPU && cache == nil` — the CAS-loser and
  never-resident branches. A resident-and-won call now allocates no host KV capacity at all
  (`TestGenerate_residentPathAllocatesNoHostKV`, gated on `prefillEnters`'s exact delta); a
  CAS-loser still lazily allocates exactly one CPU cache and completes correctly
  (`TestGenerate_casLoserStillCompletesOnCPU`). `Session.Generate` is untouched — its cache always
  exists already, so `newCache` is never invoked for it. Second half:
  `decoder/prefill_budget.go:114-116` — `AdmitPrefillMemory` now skips the host-KV term when
  `residentPath && m.ResidentActive()`, using the same `residentPath` predicate
  `internal/serveapp/openai.go`'s `prepare()` already computes (false for vision/adapter requests,
  which still need the term); no longer prices VRAM-resident KV against host RAM, so an 8 GB CUDA
  box no longer 413s a request its resident path would have served entirely in VRAM.
- **P-02** `decoder/kvcache.go:191-183` — `NewKVCache` reserves full-width K/V capacity for MLA
  families that only ever write `mlaLatent`: DeepSeek-V2-Lite ≈ 0.66 MB/position of never-written
  capacity (~3.4 GB for a 4k+1k request), Kimi K2/V3 geometry 6–12 MB/position. Recurrent families
  reserve the same dead capacity on their non-attention layers.

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-15** (`main`). The MLA half was already fixed before this session
  touched it — `decoder/model.go`'s `NewCache` already zeroed `capHint` for the whole cache when
  `a.mla != nil` (MLA layers hold no attention-shaped KV at all; their state lives in the separate
  `mlaLatent` array), left unannotated in this doc. The remaining, real half — recurrent families'
  non-attention layers still reserving the same dead per-layer capacity as an attention layer —
  is the actual work here: `decoder/kvcache.go`'s `NewKVCache` gained a 6th, nil-safe parameter
  `skipLayer func(l int) bool`; a skipped layer's `keys[l]`/`vals[l]` are allocated with zero
  capacity instead of `capHint*kvDim`. `decoder/model.go`'s `NewCache` wires this to a new
  `Architecture.hasNoAttentionKVAt(i int) bool` (see M-28's addendum below for why a new method
  was needed rather than reusing `isLinearLayer`/`isMambaLayer`/`isConvLayer` directly).
  `decoder/mtp.go`'s single-layer cache and all 12 pre-existing `NewKVCache` call sites across the
  test suite pass `nil`, preserving exact prior behavior.

  Verified on this Mac: `decoder/kvcache_alloc_test.go` (new) —
  `TestNewKVCache_skipLayerZeroesCapacity` (synthetic, exact capacity arithmetic),
  `TestNewKVCache_nilSkipLayerReservesEveryLayer` (confirms the nil-safe default matches every
  pre-fix caller exactly), `TestNewCache_hybridRealFixtureSkipsRecurrentLayerAllocation`
  (`testdata/nemotron-tiny`, the real hybrid fixture — mamba/attention/mlp/mamba/attention —
  proving the wiring end to end: real per-layer `cap()` against `hasNoAttentionKVAt` for every
  layer, not just the isolated parameter's own logic). TDD-verified: reverted `skipLayer`'s effect,
  confirmed all three fail red with real captured capacity numbers (mamba/mlp layers reserved the
  same 131072-float/512 KB-per-layer capacity as an attention layer on the tiny fixture), restored,
  confirmed green. Full `go test ./decoder/...` (209s) green apart from the expected
  `TestParityManifest_fresh` staleness (refreshed via `scripts/refresh_parity_hashes.sh`, 37
  forward goldens green including `TestNemotron_textParity`/`TestNemotron3NanoMoE_textParity` —
  confirming this change, which only alters allocated *capacity* and never touches a computed
  value, is numerically inert as expected).
- **P-03** `decoder/scratch.go:317-313`, `decoder/attention.go:166-184` — the K=1 decode
  `headWorkerPool` grows `kh`/`vt` (2×nKeys×hd floats per slot, ×2 headroom, up to 6 slots) that
  the acc64 decode path never reads: 100–200 MB per stream at 32k on hd=128, zeroed by `make`,
  multiplied by the session LRU. Sibling of M-03 (09-02), one field over.

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-10** (`f5d4a4de`), same day as this audit — never annotated here until
  now (found while surveying §5 for a verifiable win, 2026-09-15). `headWorkerPool` takes
  `useAcc64` explicitly and skips `kh`/`vt` growth whenever it's true; `attention.go`'s decode path
  hardcodes `acc64 := true` at all three of its call sites, so the elimination is unconditional in
  production today. `scores` stays unconditional (acc64 still reads it); a future non-acc64 caller
  still grows `kh`/`vt` correctly on its own first call, since the guard checks `useAcc64` itself,
  not a decision baked into the pool's lifetime. Verified both states directly:
  `decoder/prefillattnpool_test.go`'s `TestPrefillAttnPoolInvariance` (`kh == nil` in the acc64
  fast path) and a companion check (`kh != nil`, non-nil length reported, when `useAcc64=false`) —
  re-run 2026-09-15, still green, alongside `TestAttendStrided_matchesGatherReference` (acc64 vs.
  gather-reference correctness, unaffected) and `TestDecode_fusedScheduleCostsNothingPerToken`
  (71232 B/token, fused-schedule on vs. off — confirms this elimination changed only allocation,
  never a computed value).
- **P-04** `decoder/spec_sample.go:36-38` — P-14 (09-02) was fixed on the filtered branch only;
  the server's default sampling (`Temperature 1`, no filters — `prepare` drops `top_p: 1`) calls
  `softmaxStable` (`make([]float64, V)`) at every verified position: 9 × 1.2 MB ≈ 11 MB per round at
  V=152k on the most common served configuration. P-14's benchmark ran at `TopK=40`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (`c28c847`).** `softmaxStableInto` (a scratch-reusing sibling
  of `softmaxStable`) added; the default-sampling branch now calls it with `s.distBufN(len(logits))`
  — the same reused scratch the greedy and filtered branches beside it already used, closing the
  one branch P-14 missed (`decoder/spec_sample.go:36-41`, self-documented in place). Verified
  current, not just claimed: `decoder/spec_sample_test.go` gates it, mutation-checked (fails red
  reverted per the commit's own note).
- **P-05** `decoder/spec_ngram.go:338-324`, `decoder/blockspec.go:241,265-279` — R-03's commit is
  never consumed: the speculative loops commit `resIDs` but always cold-prefill from 0, so a
  `--spec`/`--drafter` agent loop still gets no prefix reuse (the whole per-turn prefill — 8.85 s at
  2.3k tokens on the 7B in R-01's measurement — on exactly the loops speculation is aimed at).
  `docs/tasks/task-recompute-audit.md:62` says "fixed". `residentReuseLen` before the forget is the same
  three lines `generateInto` has. `decoder/blockspec.go` half (fixed, commit only) —
  `decoder/blockspec.go:452-441` — `generate`'s ONLY fully-completed exit (the `for` round loop
  falling through, not an early error or the caller's `emit` stopping consumption) now calls
  `m.residentCommitIDs(prompt, out, nil)`, mirroring `generateInto`'s own natural-completion commit.
  Gives the next PLAIN `Generate` turn a warm prefix after a `--drafter` turn.
  **Drafter-context reuse (fixed)** — `decoder/blockspec.go:241,252,462` — a `--drafter` turn on
  a strict prefix extension now reuses BOTH the target's resident KV and the drafter's own
  fused-hidden-state context: `reuseFrom := m.residentReuseLen(prompt, nil, nil)` computed
  before the forget, `rd.TruncateContext(reuseFrom)` in place of an unconditional
  `TruncateContext(0)`, and the seed/fuse steps embed and fuse only `prompt[reuseFrom:]`.
  Token-id matching alone is not sufficient — a NEW `Model.resDrafterSynced *BlockSpec` field
  (`decoder/model.go`) names which `*BlockSpec` instance's drafter context the current `resIDs`
  actually reflects; `residentForgetIDs` clears it on every resident write (any writer), and only
  this same instance's own fully-completed `generate()` sets it back — so a token-identical
  `resIDs` committed by a PLAIN `Generate` turn (or a different `BlockSpec`) still forces a cold
  drafter refuse, because that write never touched THIS drafter's context at all.
  `TestBlockSpecGenerate_reusesDrafterContextOnExtension` drives two calls on the same instance
  and asserts the second seeds/fuses only the new suffix and truncates to `reuseFrom`, not 0;
  `TestBlockSpecGenerate_declinesDrafterReuseAfterOtherWriter` simulates a foreign commit between
  calls and asserts the second call falls back to cold. Both mutation-checked.
- **P-06** `decoder/speculative.go:235,184` (fixed: now `residentPrefillSeed`, was the target's and
  draft's own one-token-at-a-time `Forward` loops) — `GenerateSpeculative` prefilled the resident
  target and draft one token at a time (+2.66 ms/token vs 0.42 batched), the third copy of the loop
  `residentPrefillSeed` was unified to prevent; library-only today.
- **P-07** `decoder/sampler.go:253-257,381,564-571` (original locations; see the disposition below
  for where the fix landed) — remaining per-token full-vocab allocations:
  `slices.Clone(logits)` per token whenever a penalty or bias is set (600 KB); `computeLogprobs` →
  `softmaxStable` (1.2 MB) with `logprobs:true`; `TopP == 1` with no other filter → a V-element
  `[]indexedProb` (2.4 MB) plus an O(V log V) sort (library-reachable; serve drops `top_p: 1`).

  **DISPOSITION — FULLY FIXED, in two rounds.** `c28c847` (2026-09-10) closed only the
  `computeLogprobs` allocation: it now takes a `dst` scratch and is called with
  `s.distBufN(len(work))` (`decoder/sampler.go:292`), reusing the same buffer the sampling
  branches beside it already share. The other two named in this finding were found UNCHANGED
  while writing this disposition (verified directly against current code, not inferred from the
  commit message, whose own "P-04/P-07" note only claimed `computeLogprobs` and
  `distVectorFrom`) — and were closed the same day, 2026-09-12: `work = slices.Clone(logits)` is
  now `work = s.workBufN(len(logits))` (`decoder/sampler.go:256`); `topFilterLogits` gained two
  more caller-owned scratch parameters, `candScratch`/`ipsScratch`
  (`decoder/sampler.go:591,632,642`), covering the no-filter default case's candidate list and
  the indexed-probability pairs every case builds regardless of branch — the audit's own "2.4 MB"
  figure. `TestTopFilterLogits_MatchesReference`'s 24018-case bit-for-bit sweep against the
  reference implementation passed unchanged after the scratch swap. All three allocations this
  finding named are now gone; P-07 is closed.
- **P-08** `decoder/residency.go:1116-1084` — `embedResident` allocates a fresh `[hidden]float32`
  per decoded and per prefill token (14 KB on a 7B); the one steady-state allocation left on the
  resident decode loop other than the readback.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (`c28c847`).** `embedResidentInto(id, dst)` added
  (`decoder/residency.go:1153`); `embedResident` itself is now a one-line wrapper (`:1121`,
  `dst=nil`) kept for the batch-collection call sites that must NOT share a buffer (several
  embeddings retained at once would alias on a shared `dst`). The resident decode loop's two hot
  call sites now pass a reused `embScratch` (`decoder/model.go:1573,1463`). Verified against
  current code, not just the commit message: `decoder/embed_resident_scratch_test.go` gates it.
- **P-09** `internal/serveapp/openai.go:210-182`, `decoder/model.go:1385-1209` — a request cancelled
  while waiting for the model lock (`tryEnter` blocks with no context) reaches `generateInto`, forgets
  a warm `resIDs`, then returns `context.Canceled` one token later; the next live turn cold-prefills
  the whole conversation. One `ctx.Err()` check before the forget keeps the cache (sibling of R-02).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (`c28c847`).** Took the Fix exactly: a `ctx.Err()` check now
  runs before `residentForgetIDs()` at the resident branch's own entry (`decoder/model.go:1371-1266`,
  self-documented in place as "P-09 (audit-2026-09-10, sibling of R-02)"), returning early with
  `g.err = err` so a request cancelled while queued for the model lock never discards the warm
  cache for a prefill about to be refused anyway. `decoder/model_cancel_commit_test.go` gates it.
- **P-10** `cuda/lora.go:119-171`, `decoder/model.go:1338-1186` — every adapter generation re-uploads
  the whole adapter synchronously and allocates/frees all its device buffers: rank-16 on
  Qwen2.5-7B ≈ 90 MB H2D and 392 allocations per request before the first token; rank-64 ≈ 360 MB.
  A per-adapter device cache keyed on the `loraRuntime` removes it. **P-11** `cuda/lora.go:179-191`,
  `cuda/lora.cu:14-44` — `applyLora` is 14 extra launches per layer per token (392/token on 28
  layers), one of them single-block: ~2 ms of launch overhead on a ~20–25 ms token (8–10%) before
  the serialized reduction. Mirrors Metal by design; recorded so it is not assumed.

  **DISPOSITION (P-10) — FIXED 2026-09-10 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`).** Took the Fix, as a
  one-entry cache. Clearing an adapter now UNBINDS it but keeps its device buffers, and the next
  bind of the same adapter is a pointer swap (`cuda/lora.go:119-171`). The key is not the
  `loraRuntime` pointer the Fix proposed, because `SetAdapter` never sees the runtime — it gets
  `[]decoder.ResidentAdapterLayer`. It is the identity of every projection's A and B backing arrays
  plus shape and scale (`cuda/lora.go:209-213`). Those slices alias the runtime's own `loraDelta`
  storage, which a fresh `LoadAdapter` never shares even under the same name, and holding them keeps
  the GC from recycling that memory while it is cached. One entry costs no VRAM beyond what a bound
  adapter already costs. A different adapter evicts and frees it, so two adapters that alternate
  still upload on every bind, as before. `GOINFER_NO_LORA_CACHE=1` restores upload-per-bind; it is
  both the A/B switch and the escape hatch.

  Measured on qwen3-4b int4 CUDA-resident (RTX 2070 SUPER), with a synthetic rank-16 adapter on all
  7 projections of all 36 layers (132.1 MB and 504 allocations per upload), over 10 interleaved
  pairs, cached vs `GOINFER_NO_LORA_CACHE=1`:

  - bind + clear: 0.064 ms vs 59.7 ms (cached faster 10/10);
  - time to first token on a 16-token prompt: 295 ms vs 349 ms, paired −50.6 ms (10/10).

  Harness `cuda/lora_cost_measure_test.go`; the log is archived on the Linux box in the
  release-v0.15.0 working directory, under `p10`.

  Gated by `TestLoRAAdapterCacheCUDA` (`cuda/lora_cache_test.go`), which drives bind, clear, rebind,
  a second adapter and back, and asserts the upload/hit counters and the logits at every step:

  - a rebind through a fresh slice header of the same adapter must hit and reproduce its logits;
  - a different adapter must upload, land near its CPU reference, and differ from the first;
  - a clear must give back the base logits.

  The rebind step was red before the fix (`uploads=2 hits=0`). Three mutations each go red: a key
  that ignores the adapter (the second adapter is served from cache), a cache that never hits, and
  a clear that does not unbind. **Metal is now fixed too** (below) — `metal/lora.go:83` had released
  and re-run `NewBufferFloats` for every A and B on every bind, the same pattern, now closed on both
  backends.

  **DISPOSITION (P-11) — FIXED 2026-09-11 (branch `audit-2026-09-10-fixes`). The audit's estimate
  was wrong by an order of magnitude, and the fix cleared the rule registered before it.** Measured
  first, on the same qwen3-4b resident as P-10: adapter bound vs unbound, 48 decode steps per arm
  over 10 interleaved pairs, cost 20.06 ms vs 8.89 ms per token. That is +11.0 ms, or 124%, where
  the audit estimated 8–10% of a ~20–25 ms token; the base token on this card is 8.9 ms. The 504
  extra launches account for perhaps 2 ms. The rest was `lora_delta_down` running as ONE block that
  looped over every rank, pulling ~61 MB of f32 A matrices per token through a single SM. Since
  C-01, adapter prefill is token by token, so the cost landed on every prompt token too.

  The kernel now strides ranks by `blockIdx`, and `applyLora` launches one block per rank
  (`cuda/lora.cu:22-44`, `loraDownCfg` in `cuda/lora.go`). A `GridX=1` launch is still the old
  kernel exactly, and each block of the grid runs the same per-rank arithmetic, so the output is
  bit-identical rather than merely close:

  - `TestLoRADownGridBitIdenticalCUDA` holds that. All 256 logits were identical across the two
    launch shapes at max rank 4, with non-vacuity checks.
  - A mutation that makes the kernel ignore `blockIdx` goes red, with 256 of 256 logits different.
  - `lora.ptx` was rebuilt at the 12.9.86 toolchain that built it, after an unchanged rebuild
    reproduced it byte for byte. `lora_delta_up` is byte-identical, and the FMA lint is green.

  The decision rule, registered before the change: **ships** if `TestLoRAResidentParityCUDA`
  holds its cosine and the paired per-token overhead falls by ≥50% in ≥9/10 pairs; **parked** at
  25–50%; **rejected** below 25%. Result, over 10 rotated triplets (unbound / single-block / grid)
  on one resident:

  - overhead: 10.79 ms (122%) single-block → 3.74 ms (42%) grid. That is a mean reduction of
    65.3%, halved in 10/10 pairs — **ships**;
  - parity: cosine 0.999963, unchanged;
  - time to first token on a 16-token adapter prompt: 295 ms → 177.5 ms (separate runs).

  The remaining 3.74 ms is not attributed; the extra launches and `lora_delta_up` are the
  candidates. Metal's `lora_delta_down` was the same single-threadgroup design
  (`metal/kernels.go:863-862`, now the fused `lora_delta` kernel); it got the same class of fix,
  below, with its own measurement on the Mac. The harness is `cuda/lora_cost_measure_test.go`; the
  logs are on the Linux box, in the release-v0.15.0 working directory under `p10`.

  **Metal halves fixed (fixed)** — `metal/lora.go`, `metal/kernels.go`. P-10: `SetAdapter(nil)` no
  longer releases the just-uploaded device buffers; they stay cached (`r.loraCached`,
  `r.loraCacheSrc`) for a same-adapter rebind, matched by comparing the `A`/`B` slices' DATA
  POINTERS (never copied by `residentAdapterLayers`, so stable for as long as the adapter stays
  loaded) — a rebind of the SAME adapter reuses the cached `*residLoRAProj`s with zero device
  allocation or upload; a DIFFERENT adapter still evicts and re-uploads exactly as before, so this
  never holds more than one adapter's device memory at a time.
  P-11: `lora_delta_down`/`lora_delta_up` fused into one `lora_delta` kernel/dispatch — the
  down-project's `t[R]` moved from a device-memory scratch buffer (round-tripped through a second
  dispatch) into the SAME threadgroup's shared memory the up-stage reads directly, halving the
  dispatch count per targeted projection (up to 14/layer → 7/layer on an adapter that targets all
  seven projections). `TestSetAdapter_cachesDeviceBuffersAcrossRebind` gates P-10 at the pointer
  level (same-adapter rebind reuses the exact `*residLoRAProj`; a different adapter, built from
  identical content but a separate `loraRuntime` instance, does not); `TestLoRAResidentParityMetal`
  gates P-11's correctness (cosine unchanged at 0.999960 pre/post fusion) and was mutation-checked
  by disabling the fused kernel's up-stage accumulate (cosine drops to -0.025, argmax wrong). CUDA
  was fixed separately, above.
- **P-12** `pull/resolve.go:33-40` → `pull/pull.go:624-606` — every `serve --model demo:<tier>` (and
  `hf:repo:file`) start re-hashes the whole checkpoint through SHA-256 before touching the network
  (~1 s for the 0.5B, 30 s+ for a 20 GB gpt-oss on a laptop disk), then the CRC'd load reads it
  again. Cache `(size, mtime, sha256)` in a sidecar.

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-10** (`452622bf`), same day as this audit — never annotated here until
  now (found while surveying §5 for a verifiable win, 2026-09-15). `cachedFileSHA256`
  (`pull/pull.go:675`) wraps `fileSHA256` behind a sidecar dotfile (`.<name>.sha256`) keyed on
  exactly `(size, mtime)` as the doc's own Fix line asks; `cachedIntact` (`pull/resolve.go`, shared
  by both `Resolve`'s offline check and `Download`'s own) now calls it instead of hashing the whole
  file on every start. A missing/mismatched/corrupt sidecar falls through to a real hash and
  rewrites it — never a wrong answer, only a repeat cost. Deliberately scoped to only this half:
  the loader's separate CRC pass at actual load time (`decoder/serialize.go`) is untouched, per the
  09-02 audit's own P-10 disposition already carving that out as a bigger, cross-cutting
  on-disk-format change.

  Verified the cache is actually consulted, not just correct either way:
  `pull/digest_cache_test.go`'s `TestCachedFileSHA256_actuallyTrustsTheSidecar` plants a sidecar
  with a wrong digest but matching size/mtime and confirms it's trusted verbatim — the only way to
  observe from outside that re-hashing was skipped — mutation-checked by disabling the sidecar
  read. `TestCachedFileSHA256_invalidatesOnRealChange` confirms a genuine content+size+mtime change
  still forces a fresh hash. Re-run 2026-09-15: both green, alongside the full `pull` package suite
  (`TestResolveOffline_verifiedCacheHitNeedsNoNetwork`, `TestDownload_badDigestIsNotKept`, etc.),
  unaffected.
- **P-13** `decoder/hostram_darwin.go:56-62` from `decoder/prefill_budget.go:100` — one
  `exec.Command("vm_stat")` per chat request (fork+exec+parse on the serving path). Rate-limit the
  probe (~250 ms).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-10 (`c28c847`).** `cachedHostRAMAvailable`
  (`decoder/prefill_budget.go:41`) wraps `hostRAMAvailable` behind a 250ms TTL, matching the
  audit's own suggested window; `AdmitPrefillMemory` now calls the cached wrapper instead of
  shelling out on every request. Test-only `resetAvailProbeCache` clears the cache immediately so
  a test's fake override still takes effect on its very next read. `decoder/prefill_budget_test.go`
  gates it.
- **P-14** `metal/prefill.go:548-495` — the host f32→f16 conversion of the prompt is a serial scalar
  loop (7.3M calls at M=2048, H=3584) on the TTFT path; `parallelF32ToF16` (`metal/model.go:455-429`)
  exists and is unused here.

  **Status: FIXED 2026-09-10** (`b9aa35d2`), same day as this audit — never annotated here until
  now (found while surveying §5 for a verifiable win, 2026-09-15). `parallelEmbedsF32ToF16`
  (`metal/prefill.go:497`, called at `:549`) is a purpose-built sibling of `parallelF32ToF16`
  (`metal/model.go:455`) — not a direct reuse, since `parallelF32ToF16` wants one flat
  `[]float32` and the prompt embeddings are `[][]float32` (one slice per row), so this one splits
  by row instead, same worker/threshold shape. Every element's conversion is independent
  (`f32ToF16` is a pure function of its single input), so parallelizing changes only wall time,
  never a value.

  Verified byte-identical against a serial reference across four shapes (below the parallel
  threshold, at the 8192-element boundary, and the audit's own cited 2048×3584), mutation-checked
  (a wrong per-element value in the parallel branch fails immediately):
  `metal/parallel_embeds_f32tof16_test.go`'s `TestParallelEmbedsF32ToF16_matchesSerial`, re-run
  2026-09-15, still green. `TestPrefillParityGemma`/`TestPrefillParityMoE` (cosine 0.9998/0.9999
  against the sequential reference) unaffected, confirming this conversion sits upstream of
  anything either parity check exercises.
**P-15 (C-08 half, split out 2026-09-16)** — **C-08 confirmed and fixed**
(`metal/model.go`'s `prefillOK`, same file, same predicate) — a PAGED generic MoE's per-layer
expert buffers are zero-value (the real weights live in the slot pool), but `PrefillLast`'s row
loop called the non-paged `encodeMoERoute`/`encodeMoEExperts` pair unconditionally; `prefillOK`
had the dense Gemma-4 MoE guard (`!m.HasGemma4MoEResident()`) but no generic twin. Added
`!(r.moe != nil && r.moe.paged)`.
`TestPrefillLast_declinesPagedGenericMoE`/`_admitsNonPagedGenericMoE` reproduced the bug on
`testdata/mixtral-tiny` forced paged (`GOINFER_METAL_MOE_SLOTS=4` of 8 experts) before the fix,
confirmed the non-paged case is unaffected, and mutation-checked (reverting the added term
reproduces the failure). (The remainder of the original P-15 entry — the MoE-TTFT measurement gap
— is still open; see the live doc.)
- **P-16** `gpu/backend.go:160-218` vs `:255-297` — staged int4 has no `W4A8Op` batch twin, so an
  int4 model on the staged WebGPU path syncs once per projection: q/k/v and gate/up each cost three
  syncs against int8's one (~4 extra syncs/layer, tens of ms per token on 28 layers — larger than
  the GEMV). A `MatmulW4A8Batch` mirroring `BatchGEMV`.
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED.** `gpu/backend.go:240` adds `MatmulW4A8Batch`, mirroring
  `MatmulW8A8Batch` exactly — resolves each op's resident weight via a new shared
  `residentW4A8For` helper (`gpu/backend.go:192`, factored out of `MatmulW4A8` so both entry
  points cache through the SAME map), then makes ONE call into `gpu/gemv.go:194`'s `BatchGEMV`,
  which is now generalized from `[]*ResidentW8A8` to `[]decodeWeight` — `ResidentW4A8` already
  implemented that interface (`gpu/gemv_w4a8.go`, built for `GEMVRunner`), so the whole dispatch
  loop is precision-agnostic with one added per-op `SetPipeline` (was set once outside the loop,
  now inside — needed for a heterogeneous batch, though every real caller builds a homogeneous
  one). `decoder/backend.go` adds `QuantBatchBackend4` and threads `be Backend` into
  `matmulW4A8Batch` (both `decoder/attention.go` and `decoder/mlp.go`'s call sites updated) —
  the exact same routing shape `QuantBatchBackend`/`matmulW8A8Batch` already has for int8.
  Gated by `TestWebGPUBackend_MatmulW4A8Batch_matchesCPU` (real device, cosine 1.000000 against
  `linalg.MatmulBTW4A8Batch`), `_declinesPrefill` (M>1), `_oneSubmit` (proves `MatmulW4A8` and
  `MatmulW4A8Batch` share one resident cache — a same-bytes rebind after `MatmulW4A8` is a cache
  hit, not a re-upload), and `TestMatmulW4A8Batch_routesThroughQuantBatchBackend4` (decoder-level,
  fake backend, proves the routing/decline contract without a GPU). All four mutation-checked.
  Full `go test -tags "gpu goinfer_testhooks" ./gpu/...` clean; `go test ./decoder/...` clean
  except the pre-existing `TestOlmo3_forwardParity`.
- **P-17** `constrain/tool_grammar.go:17-24`, `constrain/plainstring.go:339-341,376-379` — forced
  tool-call decoding never takes the plain-string fast path: `toolGrammar` does not implement
  `InPlainString`, so every `tool_choice`-forced call pays P-20's full walk (6.03 ms/step at `fsStr`
  on Qwen's vocab) on the path that is mostly strings. `return g.phase == 1 &&
  g.inner.InPlainString()` is exact. Demo half (fixed, trivial part only) —
  `internal/chatapp/main.go:421,620`, `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:570,590` — both demos rebuilt the
  ~152k-entry token→bytes table per turn (N-23 (09-02)'s demo half); `newSession` in each now builds
  it once into a `tokenBytes [][]byte` field, mirroring serve's own `cachedTokenBytes`, and
  `jsonMasker`/`schemaMasker` read that field instead.

  **DISPOSITION (core mechanism) — FIXED 2026-09-10 (`c28c847`).** `toolGrammar.InPlainString()`
  (`constrain/tool_grammar.go:104-106`) now returns `g.phase == 1 && g.inner.InPlainString()`, the
  exact fix this finding proposed — a forced tool call's plain-string runs take the cheap bitmap
  check instead of paying the full grammar walk. `constrain/plainstring_test.go` gates it,
  mutation-checked. The demo half noted above was already separately fixed (N-23, 09-02) before
  this commit; both halves of P-17 are closed as of `c28c847`.
  **O(n²) `Decode(out)` half (fixed)** — `internal/chatapp/main.go:540`,
  `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:454` — this is the demos' own twin of R-08
  (`internal/serveapp/openai.go`'s `streamTokens`, already fixed there): both extracted their
  inline streaming loop into a `streamGen` that decodes INCREMENTALLY, `DecodePiece(id)` appended
  to a `strings.Builder` per token, instead of re-decoding the whole growing `out` slice on every
  flush. `DecodePiece`, not `Decode`/`DecodeContinuation`: these ids continue the prompt, so the
  SentencePiece dummy-prefix leading-space strip must not apply per-token (M-25) — the ORIGINAL
  code's final `s.tk.Decode(out)` call had exactly that M-25 bug on the STREAMED (not stored;
  `strings.TrimSpace` already normalized the stored copy either way) output, fixed for free by
  reusing the same accumulator for both. `TestStreamGen_matchesWholeSequenceDecode` (both
  packages) drives a real tokenizer (`tokenizer/testdata/chatml-tiny.gguf`, no model needed —
  `streamGen` takes the token channel directly) and asserts the concatenation of every flushed
  chunk equals `tk.DecodeContinuation(ids)`, the whole-sequence reference;
  `TestDecodeContinuation_isIncrementallyAssociative` (`tokenizer/`) is the independent proof that
  per-piece concatenation is byte-identical to a fresh decode at any split point. Mutation-checked
  in both packages (dropping the `printed`/`emitted` offset when slicing the tail reproduces a
  slice-bounds panic on the first flush past the first token).

## Suggested order of work (original audit's prioritization, kept for the record)

Every finding named below closed between 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-16; this section is no longer
actionable, kept only because it documents the order the original audit recommended.

Fix order, if the team takes this as one batch: C-01 + C-02 + G-06 together (one seam, three
lines each plus the gate); C-03 + G-05 (three lines plus the reflection test); C-04, C-05, C-06,
C-09 (each a small kernel or dispatch fix with a gate that can fail); C-07, C-08 (Metal, one
predicate each); then G-01..G-04 (the evidence chain — an hour each and they stop the next
recurrence); then the Majors that mislead users today (M-14, M-47, M-48, M-49, M-53) before the
next tag; the rest by the groupings above. The per-item citations are keyed to `c7ef16a`; run the
citation lint's `--update` once after the first fix batch lands.
